# Decisions

This file is the record of decisions made while building the site: every default of SPEC.md section 10 that was relied on (one line per default, with the date and the reason, as SPEC.md 9.3 asks), every dependency added to the site (version, licence, and size when it is shipped to the reader), every [U] or [D] item a builder had to settle, and every deviation a builder reported.

Entries are appended by the MERGE and GATE agents from the fragments in `decisions/*.md`, one fragment per agent id, in the form of `build-plan/COMMON.md` section 5.1. No build agent edits this file directly: a build agent writes its fragment and MERGE or GATE copies it here verbatim under a dated stage heading. The owner's part of each gate (SPEC.md 9.1) is recorded here as due when the gate's automatic part has passed and the red team is dry.

## 2026-10-10 MERGE: P0 stage 0.2

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: registry check of the pinned versions
Decision: every pin of SPEC.md 7.1 is used as given; every one exists on the registry. What the specification left open: nothing. Default used: none. Reason: the registry answered each `npm view <pkg>@<version> version` with the version asked.

| package | asked | registry | installed in |
|---|---|---|---|
| astro | 7.3.8 | 7.3.8 | site (dependencies) |
| @astrojs/mdx | 8.0.3 | 8.0.3 | site (dependencies) |
| svelte | 5.57.2 | 5.57.2 | site (dependencies) |
| @astrojs/svelte | 9.0.1 | 9.0.1 | site (dependencies) |
| typescript | 6.0.3 | 6.0.3 | root and packages/engine (devDependencies) |
| nanostores | 1.5.5 | 1.5.5 | site (dependencies) |
| vitest | 5.0.3 | 5.0.3 | root and packages/engine (devDependencies) |
| fast-check | 4.10.2 | 4.10.2 | packages/engine (devDependencies) |
| @playwright/test | 1.64.0 | 1.64.0 | root (devDependencies) |
| @axe-core/playwright | 4.13.0 | 4.13.0 | root (devDependencies) |

Peer ranges checked on 2026-10-10: `@astrojs/svelte@9.0.1` peerDependencies `{ astro: ^7.0.0, svelte: ^5.43.6, typescript: ^5.3.3 || ^6.0.0 }`; `@astrojs/check@0.9.10` peerDependencies `{ typescript: ^5.0.0 || ^6.0.0 }`; `svelte-check@4.7.6` peerDependencies `{ svelte: ^4.0.0 || ^5.0.0-next.0, typescript: ^5.0.0 || ^6.0.0 }`. All three admit typescript 6.0.3. `astro@7.3.8` engines `node >=22.12.0`; `vitest@5.0.3` engines `node ^22.12.0 || ^24.0.0 || >=26.0.0`. This machine: node v22.22.0, npm 10.9.4, python3 3.13.16.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: the two check tools
Decision: `@astrojs/check` 0.9.10 (MIT, build only, not shipped) and `svelte-check` 4.7.6 (MIT, build only, not shipped) added to `site/devDependencies` for `npm run typecheck` (`astro check` and `svelte-check`). What the specification left open: their versions (SPEC.md 7.2 item 4 names them without a version). Default used: none. Reason: the current versions on the registry whose peer ranges admit astro 7.3.8, svelte 5.57.2 and typescript 6.0.3.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: @types/node added as a build-only dependency (deviation)
Decision: `@types/node` 22.20.5 (MIT, build only, not shipped) added to the root `devDependencies`. What the specification left open: SPEC.md 7.1 does not list it; the P0-A1 instruction (5.2) allows only the two check tools beyond the table. Reason: `astro check` type-checks `site/tests/unit/*.test.ts`, which read files, hash the reference files and spawn `npm` through `node:fs`, `node:crypto` and `node:child_process`, and TypeScript cannot resolve those modules or `process` without the Node type definitions (seventeen errors in the first `npm run typecheck`). The same need returns for every script of `scripts/` and every test that reads the golden file. `vitest@5.0.3` lists `@types/node` among its peer dependencies. The version is the current 22.x line, matching the Node 22 of `.nvmrc`. Reported under `deviations` for MERGE; the engine's `tsconfig.json` keeps `"types": []`, so nothing in `packages/engine/src` can depend on Node by accident (P0-A2 may add `"types": ["node"]` for its test harness).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: D4, code licence answered by the owner, content licence by default
Decision: `LICENSE` is the owner's GNU Affero General Public License version 3 (placed on GitHub on 2026-10-10); `"license": "AGPL-3.0-only"` in the root, engine and site `package.json`. `LICENSE-content` states CC BY-SA 4.0 (SPDX `CC-BY-SA-4.0`) for the text and figures, with the sentence that code samples shown in the text fall under the code licence, and a link to the legal code. What the specification left open: D4, the licences. Default used (SPEC section 10, D4): CC BY-SA 4.0 for content; the code licence is the owner's choice, not the MIT default. Reason: the owner's LICENSE file settles the code side; nothing has settled the content side. If the owner chooses CC BY 4.0, GATE replaces `LICENSE-content` on the owner's instruction (README 6). Debt: both licences are to be stated in the footer of the built site (SPEC 7.9), for the layout owner.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: no linter, no formatter
Decision: no ESLint, Prettier, actionlint or YAML package is installed. What the specification left open: tooling beyond the stack table. Default used: none. Reason: SPEC.md 7.1 does not name them, and COMMON.md 3 forbids a dependency nobody's instruction names. The `.editorconfig` (UTF-8, LF, final newline, two-space indentation) is the only formatting convention.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: the root Vitest configuration is vitest.config.ts
Decision: the root Vitest configuration is `vitest.config.ts` with `test.projects` (`packages/engine` by its own `vitest.config.ts`, and the project `site-unit` with `include: ['site/tests/unit/**/*.test.ts']`, `environment: 'node'`); no `vitest.workspace.ts` exists. What the specification left open: the file name (CONTRACTS.md 1 allows either). Default used: none. Reason: the installed vitest 5.0.3 types declare only `test.projects`; its distribution contains no reader of a `vitest.workspace` file, so such a file would be dead. The roster's owns list `vitest.workspace.ts` is read with this substitution (deviation, reported).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: astro.config.mjs
Decision: `siteAndBase(env)` and `outDirFrom(env)` are exported beside the default export; `base` is normalised to begin and end with one `/`; an empty string counts as unset for `SITE_URL`, `BASE_PATH`, `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` and `OUT_DIR`; `output: 'static'`; `integrations: [mdx(), svelte()]`; no `markdown` key (P0-A3's). What the specification left open: nothing (SPEC 7.2 item 10 and CONTRACTS 1 fix the rule). Reason: as contracted. Checked by building: a build with `BASE_PATH=/sub/path/ OUT_DIR=<scratch>` wrote `index.html` into that directory and left `site/dist` untouched; `import.meta.env.BASE_URL` is `/sub/path/` (Astro's default `trailingSlash: 'ignore'` keeps the configured base as given). The installed Astro inlines the placeholder's small stylesheet into a `<style>` element, so the built page carries no `<link>` and no `<script>`. Unhandled case for the owner: a repository named `owner.github.io` is served at `/`, but SPEC 7.2 item 10 states only the `owner/repo` rule, which would give `/owner.github.io/`; the rule is implemented as written.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: tsconfig and path aliases
Decision: `tsconfig.base.json` with `strict`, `target ES2022`, `module ESNext`, `moduleResolution bundler`; `site/tsconfig.json` extends `["../tsconfig.base.json", "astro/tsconfigs/strict"]` (the Astro preset last, so its settings win) and declares `@engine` (to `../packages/engine/src/index.ts`, the source entry of `@two-games/engine`), `@components/*` and `@lib/*`; `packages/engine/tsconfig.json` extends the base with `noEmit` and `types: []`, includes `src` and `test`. The engine package's `exports` points at `./src/index.ts`. What the specification left open: the details. Default used: none. Reason: CONTRACTS.md 1 and the installed Astro's `tsconfigs/base.json`, which already sets `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `allowImportingTsExtensions`, `noEmit` and `jsx: preserve`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: colours not fixed by SPEC 6.3
Decision: `--focus-ring` `#0072B2` light and `#56B4E9` dark (the positive fill hues, 5.2 to 1 on white and 8.1 to 1 on `#121212`); `--road` and `--arrow` equal to the text colour (`#1A1A1A` light, `#E8E8E8` dark, well above 3 to 1); prose in the system font stack, numerals in the monospaced system stack with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`; a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` rule removing transitions and animations. What the specification left open: the three colours and the font stacks (SPEC 6.3 and 8.3 are [D]). Default used: SPEC 7.5 A-6 (3 to 1 for outlines, roads, arrows and focus rings). Reason: contrast computed with the WCAG formula against both pages; P1-A3 may restructure `base.css`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A1: workflows and the Pages publisher (deviation from 7.1 "official Astro action")
Decision: `ci.yml` (pull_request and push to main; checkout, setup-node with `.nvmrc` and the npm cache, `npm ci`, `npm run check`, `npm run build:release`, `npm run build`, upload `site/dist` as `site-dist`; `permissions: contents: read`). `deploy.yml` (push of tags `v*` and workflow_dispatch; the job carries `if: vars.DEPLOY_PAGES == 'true'`; `permissions: contents: read, pages: write, id-token: write`; environment `github-pages`; `npm run build` with `RELEASE: '1'` in the step environment; `actions/configure-pages@v5`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v4` with path `site/dist`, `actions/deploy-pages@v4`). `links.yml` (weekly cron `17 6 * * 1` and workflow_dispatch; `permissions: contents: read, issues: write`; build, then `lycheeverse/lychee-action@v2` over `site/dist/**/*.html` with `continue-on-error: true`, excluding localhost and github.io addresses; on failure `gh issue create` with the report). Action tags: `actions/checkout@v5`, `actions/setup-node@v5`, `actions/upload-artifact@v5`, `actions/configure-pages@v5`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v4`, `actions/deploy-pages@v4`, `lycheeverse/lychee-action@v2` (Apache-2.0 for the action; lychee itself MIT or Apache-2.0; a dependency the specification does not name, used only in CI). What the specification left open: the publisher mechanics. Deviation from SPEC 7.1 "GitHub Pages through the official Astro action": `withastro/action` runs its own install and `astro build` inside the site directory, which would skip the root `prebuild` step (`scripts/names.mjs`, SPEC 7.3) and the root workspace install; building with `npm run build` at the root under `RELEASE=1` and publishing with the GitHub Pages actions keeps one build path for CI, the gate and the deploy. The major version tags were taken from memory of the actions' current releases and could not be read from GitHub from this container (no GitHub API access during stage 0.1); RT-12 or the owner should confirm them on the first run. The two release archives of SPEC 7.8 are built by GATE at releases 0.5 and 1.0 (README 7), not by `deploy.yml`.

Decision: this build runs in a cloud session on the branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud`, which plays the part that `build-plan/` calls `main`: build agents branch from it in worktrees, MERGE and GATE commit to it, and the owner merges it into `main` on GitHub. What the specification left open: nothing; the plan assumed the owner's machine. Reason: the session's rules require all work on that branch; nothing is pushed to `main`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: versions observed
Decision: built and tested against node v22.22.0, typescript 6.0.3, vitest 5.0.3, fast-check 4.10.2 (all as pinned by P0-A1) and @types/node 22.20.5 (P0-A1's recorded deviation). What the specification left open: nothing. Default used: none. Reason: `node --version` and the `version` fields under `node_modules/`. No dependency was added.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the branch is based on the integration branch
Decision: the worktree branch `worktree-agent-a9527a8f00d522ac8` (the plan's `agent/P0-A2`) was fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only` from `5f439e7` to `278495a` (`P0-A1: scaffold`), the head of the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud`, before any file was written. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: the worktree was created before P0-A1's scaffold landed; the instruction's inputs (package.json, the engine package, the root Vitest configuration) exist only from that commit, and a fast-forward of a branch with no commits of its own is the same state as branching from the integration head. Nothing was merged from another agent's branch.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: generics, not two copies (E-2 [D])
Decision: `mutate` and `fire` are written once, generic over `board.arith`, with the signatures of CONTRACTS.md 2.4. What the specification left open: E-2 allows writing the core functions twice if generics prove awkward. Default used: one code path. Reason: the generic form is short, and property 1 runs both arithmetics through the same code.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: placement of the shared type aliases (plan)
Decision: `Vec`, `Matrix` live in `src/arith.ts`; `Word`, `Game`, `Choose` in `src/moves.ts`; `Admissibility`, `Reason` in `src/errors.ts` (because `NotAdmissible` carries one); `EngineOverflow` is defined in `src/errors.ts` and re-exported from `src/arith.ts`, so that both file descriptions of CONTRACTS.md 2.1 hold. All are re-exported by `src/index.ts`. What the specification left open: the file that declares each alias. Reason: the P0-A2 instruction, section 4.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: error messages and data fields
Decision: every class sets `name` to its own class name. Default messages: `NotABoard` "not a board" (constructors pass a more specific sentence); `NeedsTwoWayRoads` "this function needs a board on which every road has a road back"; `NeedsConnectedBoard` "this function needs a connected board"; `NotFiniteType` "this function needs a board of finite type"; `NotAffine(cls)` "this function needs an affine board; this board is <cls>" with `data: { class: cls }`; `NotAdmissible(data, message?)` "the pair is not admissible" with `data: Admissibility`; `EngineOverflow` "a result is not a safe integer" (SafeInt passes the operation and the offending value). What the specification left open: the texts (E-10 names only the classes). Reason: short, plain, no em-dash; the `data` field names are the plan's.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: SafeInt checks every result and normalises -0
Decision: `SafeInt.add`, `sub`, `mul` and `fromInt` check their result with `Number.isSafeInteger` and throw `EngineOverflow` otherwise; `fromInt` of a non-integer (0.5, NaN, Infinity) throws `EngineOverflow` too; `mul`, `neg` and `fromInt` return `0` for `-0`, so `show` gives "0", `sign` gives 0 and `eq` behaves as for integers. What the specification left open: E-1 admits no non-integer and the engine has no other error class for one; `-0` is a JavaScript artefact. Reason: `(-1) * 0` is `-0` and `Math.sign(-0)` is `-0`; the oracle's integers have no such value.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the rank ranges of `cartan`
Decision: `cartan(name)` accepts exactly a letter A to G followed by a decimal rank without leading zeros, with the ranges of SPEC.md 2.8: A for n >= 1, B and C for n >= 2, D for n >= 4, E for n in {6, 7, 8}, F for n = 4, G for n = 2; ranks above 8 are allowed; everything else throws `NotABoard`. What the specification left open: 5.3 says "any rank the families allow". Reason: the oracle's `cartan(letter, n)` does not validate ranks and returns nonsense for B1, D3, E9, G3, A0 and raises IndexError for E5, F3 (instruction, trap 7); the engine must not copy that. A leading zero (`A03`) is rejected so that a board's `name` is always the canonical spelling.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: `boardFromName` throws `NotABoard` on phase 2 names in phase 0
Decision: in phase 0 `boardFromName` accepts only the finite names of `cartan` and throws `NotABoard` on `X~`, `B2~`, `C2~`, twisted names, `pentagon` and `square`. No test pins this. What the specification left open: 5.3 assigns those names to phase 2. Reason: the phase 2 engine agent extends the function in the same file; a test of the phase-0 behaviour would break then.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the phase-0 naming rule of `fromAdjacency`
Decision: `fromAdjacency(A)` validates `A` (square, non-negative integers, zero diagonal, at least one node, `boardKind(2I - A^T)` not `not-a-board`; otherwise `NotABoard`), then compares `C` entry by entry with `cartan(X)` for every finite name `X` of size `n` in the family order A, B, C, D, E, F, G; on the first match it returns `boardFromName(X)` (SafeInt, `name: X`), except that the C2 matrix `[[2, -2], [-1, 2]]` gets the same SafeInt board with `name` undefined; otherwise a `Board<bigint>` with `Big`, `kind: boardKind(C)`, labels 1..n and no name. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 2.4 says "SafeInt if identify names a finite type (phase 2), else Big; phase 0: Big unless the matrix equals a named finite type's". Reason: a name derived from a matrix is a computed name, and computed names say B2 (COMMON.md 2, SPEC.md 5.5 "Naming"); naming this matrix B2 without reordering the nodes would put a name on a board whose position order is not the Bourbaki order of `cartan("B2")`, and reordering is `identify`'s job in phase 2. Open question raised to the orchestrator in the report: which name, if any, `fromAdjacency` should give the C2 matrix in phase 0. The phase 2 agent replaces the exact comparison with `identify`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: `boardKind` on non-square input and on the empty matrix
Decision: `boardKind` returns `'not-a-board'` for a value that is not an array, a matrix that is not square, a ragged matrix, or a row holding a non-number (plan additions; the oracle would raise); the empty matrix returns `'gcm'` as in the oracle; `fromAdjacency([])` throws `NotABoard`. What the specification left open: the oracle's `board_kind` assumes a square numeric matrix. Reason: tools receive matrices from parsed links; 2.1 defines a board on nodes 1..n, so no board has no nodes, but `boardKind` follows the oracle on the empty matrix because the roster names the oracle as its specification.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: `toLabels` throws `RangeError`
Decision: `toLabels` throws `RangeError` on a position outside 0..n-1 (including a non-integer), mirroring the contract's rule for `toPositions` on an unknown label; both return fresh arrays and accept words as well as node lists. What the specification left open: the contract states the rule for `toPositions` only. Reason: symmetry, and a stray index in a word should fail loudly rather than yield `undefined`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: `mutate` and `fire` throw `RangeError` on bad input
Decision: both throw `RangeError` when `i` is not a position or the vector's length is not `n`; otherwise they take any sign of entries and any kind of board, with no guard and no cap. What the specification left open: 5.6 says the moves "work as defined" off the finite boards; the error on malformed input is the plan's. Reason: E-5 (pure) and the instruction, 5.6.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: boards are frozen
Decision: every board built by `boardFromName` and `fromAdjacency` is frozen, with its `labels`, its `cartan` and every row. Hand-built boards in tests are plain objects. What the specification left open: 5.5 of the instruction allows freezing. Reason: E-11 relies on immutability for its cache; a frozen board makes an accidental write throw in strict mode.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the overflow move is 38, not the roster's "move after 39" (deviation)
Decision: `overflow.test.ts` asserts that `SafeInt` throws `EngineOverflow` during move 38 of the `bigint_test` replays (mutations and firings), and also that this move is at most the golden `first_move_after_which_an_entry_exceeds_2_to_53` (39). What the specification left open: the roster's line "throws EngineOverflow at the move after first_move_after_which_an_entry_exceeds_2_to_53" (move 40 read literally) against its own request to derive the index semantics from `verify.py`. Reason: `verify.py` lines 1421 to 1425 apply move k + 1 and record `first_unsafe = k + 1` the first time a stored entry exceeds 2^53 afterwards, so the entries are at most 2^53 after 38 moves and exceed it after 39; a per-operation `SafeInt` throws one move earlier because move 38 forms the product 3 * 3416454622906707 = 10249363868720121 > 2^53 - 1 before its safe result 8944394323791464. Listed under `deviations` in the report.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the coverage mechanism and the shape of coverage.json
Decision: `covers(...keys)` writes one small JSON file `{ keys }` under `packages/engine/test/golden/.coverage/`, named by a hash of the sorted keys; the global setup `test/golden/_coverage.setup.ts` (declared in `packages/engine/vitest.config.ts` as `globalSetup`) clears that directory in `setup()` and in `teardown()` merges every registration into `packages/engine/test/golden/coverage.json` with the shape `{ "keys": [sorted unique spellings], "missingByPhase": { "0": [...], ..., "7": [...] } }`. Both paths are build outputs and are not committed; a request asks P0-A6 (owner of `.gitignore`) for the two ignore lines. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 3 names "the Vitest reporter/globalSetup" without choosing. Reason: Vitest runs each test file in its own worker, so module state in the harness cannot be shared (trap 12); the global setup runs in the runner process, where `vitest` itself must not be imported, so the setup file imports only the harness and `node:fs`. The include patterns of the project configuration are `test/golden/**/*.test.ts`, `test/properties/**/*.test.ts`, `test/unit/**/*.test.ts`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the hash guard
Decision: the harness computes the SHA-256 of `reference/golden_data.json` with `node:crypto` before parsing and throws unless it equals `c45474636fc9a7990c97c3664d2603f090b5b654a39352a87fc992f744e95a2f` (CONTRACTS.md 9); the check is exported as `verifyGoldenHash(file?)` (an addition to the export list of CONTRACTS.md 3, not a change) so that `harness.test.ts` runs it on a temporary copy with one byte changed. The file is loaded by `new URL('../../../../reference/golden_data.json', import.meta.url)` and deep-frozen. What the specification left open: how a changed reference is noticed by the engine tests. Reason: CONTRACTS.md 9 calls a difference a blocker; every golden test should fail loudly rather than compare against a changed file.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the key spellings of `GOLDEN_KEYS_BY_PHASE`
Decision: the table of SPEC.md 5.7 transcribed with one grammar: a top-level key as it stands, `<key>[*].<field>` for a field of every record of a list or keyed object, `<key>.<field>` for a field of a single record; "without X" rows give the plain key plus X as its own key in its own phase. The row "none" (`wildberger_lists`, `wythoff_hull_checks`, `wythoff_note`, `about`, `conventions`) is not in the record. What the specification left open: the machine-readable form. Reason: the instruction, 5.8; `harness.test.ts` checks that every spelling resolves in the golden file (each field on at least one record of its key, since `A1` lacks the short-root and `coxeter_plane` fields).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the fast-check seed
Decision: `test/properties/p1-involution.test.ts` runs `fc.assert` with `seed: 20261010` and `numRuns: 1000`, drawing a type from `TYPE_NAMES`, a vector of its rank with entries in [-9, 9] and a position, and checks both involutions on `boardOf(T)` (SafeInt) and on a hand-built `Board<bigint>`; a third property guards against an identity implementation. What the specification left open: the seed and the number of runs. Reason: CONTRACTS.md 3 asks for a fixed seed recorded in the file; 1000 runs take well under a second.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A2: the two added test files
Decision: `test/unit/moves.test.ts` (the Appendix A.1 firing tables and mutation lines on A2, B2, G2, the one-way play of 2.11, purity, RangeError) and `test/unit/harness.test.ts` (the comparison helpers, `TYPE_NAMES`, the key spellings, `dependencies: {}`, "src imports only src", `CAPS`, the error classes, the public API from `@two-games/engine`, the hash guard) exist beside the roster's files. `<span class="katex-display"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" display="block"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mtext> </mtext><mo>⟼</mo>`

A first build with `$$...$$` written on one line, even as a paragraph of its own, rendered those formulas as inline mathematics (0 elements with `display="block"`); the plan's sentence "`$$...$$` must stand as its own paragraph" is not enough. `math-check.mdx` keeps the fenced form.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: kB is 1000 bytes, and "exceeds" is strictly greater
Decision: `scripts/size.mjs` counts 1 kB as 1000 bytes and prints the unit in the table header; a route fails when its gzip sum is strictly greater than its limit, so a sum equal to the limit passes. What the specification left open: SPEC.md 7.6 does not define kB. Default used: none (7.6 is [D]; no limit was changed). Reason: 1000 is the unit's meaning and the stricter reading; `size-classify.test.ts` tests the boundary (60000 bytes passes, 60001 fails).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: the route classifier
Decision: `classifyRoute(routePath, html)` gives `/modules/*` the lesson row (120 kB); `/tools/*` and `/embed/*` the tool row (90 kB); otherwise it counts the elements carrying a `data-tool` attribute by scanning tags in the built HTML, skipping the contents of `<script>` and `<style>` elements and comments: none is `no-tool` (15 kB), exactly one with the value `board` is `one-board` (60 kB). Two cases are the plan's and open for the orchestrator: exactly one tool with another value gets the tool row (90 kB, the nearest row), and two or more tools on a page that is not a lesson get the lesson row (120 kB, the only row for several tools). Neither occurs in phase 0. What the specification left open: those two cases and the `/embed/` routes of SPEC 7.4. Default used: none. Reason: the plan's rules (P0-A6 instruction 5.1); the index page must carry `data-tool="board"` (P0-A5) or it is held to 15 kB.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: size.mjs options, port 4323 and the base from siteAndBase
Decision: `size.mjs` starts its own `astro preview` on port 4323 (so a Playwright server on 4321 never collides), refuses to start when the port is already taken (so it never measures another worktree's server), and stops the server in a `finally` block and on SIGINT and SIGTERM. Options: `--url` (measure a running server, start none), `--dist` (default `site/dist`, or `OUT_DIR` resolved from `site/` as `astro.config.mjs` resolves it), `--base` (override), `--port`, `--json <file>` (rows as JSON beside the table; GATE may use `reports/size.json`), and `--limit-override <kB>`, which replaces every limit and exists only for the unit test: it is never used in a script body, in `ci.yml`, in a gate run or by hand, and `size-classify.test.ts` checks that no script body and no `ci.yml` line passes it. The base of every route URL is `siteAndBase(process.env).base`, imported from `site/astro.config.mjs`, so the measurement uses the base the build used (`/<repo>/` in CI through `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`). What the specification left open: everything beyond the bullet "Measured by `npm run size`". Default used: none. Reason: the plan (P0-A6 instruction 5.1).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: Astro 7 preview and coding agents (--ignore-lock)
Decision: every `astro preview` this work starts carries `--ignore-lock`: in `size.mjs`, and in the `webServer.command` of `playwright.config.ts`, which now reads `npm run preview -w site -- --port 4321 --ignore-lock`. What the specification left open: SPEC 7.2 item 9 reports, for `astro dev`, that Astro detects coding agents and runs as a background server; the installed astro 7.3.8 does the same for `astro preview` (`dist/cli/preview/index.js`: with an agent detected through `am-i-vibing` it re-launches itself as a detached background process with `--json` and writes a lock file under the site root; a second server for the same root is refused). Without the flag, the first run of `size.mjs` left a detached preview on 4323 that its process-group kill could not reach (stopped with `astro preview stop`), and Playwright reported "Process from config.webServer exited early". `--ignore-lock` is documented by Astro as "a one-off foreground server" that the agent background mode yields to. The previous form `npm run preview -- --port 4321` also lost its port: npm read `--port` as its own option and ran `astro preview 4321`. Default used: none. Reason: without it no browser test or size run works in any agent session (every build agent, MERGE, GATE); in CI nothing changes (no agent is detected, the server ran in the foreground already). This replaces P0-A1's expression under an additive protocol: reported as a deviation.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: what fails a route besides its size
Decision: a route whose response is not HTTP 200 is an `ERROR` and fails the run (`route answered <status>`), because an error page fetches no script and would pass every limit at 0 kB; the one exception is the route `/404` (built from `404.html`), for which 200 and 404 are both accepted. A script request to another origin is an `ERROR` (`external script`, SPEC 7.8 "No third-party script at runtime"), and a script URL with no file in the built directory is an `ERROR`, never counted as zero. Scripts are collected from the browser context's `request` event, by `resourceType()` equal to `script` or by a pathname ending in `.js` or `.mjs` (the union), deduplicated per route; a chunk shared between routes counts on every route. Sizes are `zlib.gzipSync(file, { level: 9 }).length` of the file in the built directory, never a transfer size. After `networkidle` the script waits until no request has started for 1 second (at most 15 seconds), for `client:idle` and `client:visible` islands; it never touches the page. What the specification left open: these failure cases. Default used: none. Reason: honest exit codes (P0-A6 instruction 1).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: verify.mjs
Decision: `npm run verify` spawns the interpreter named by the environment variable `PYTHON` when it is set and non-empty, else `python3`, as `<python> -u reference/verify.py <tmp>/golden_regen.json` with the working directory at the repository root, streaming the oracle's output; the temporary directory comes from `fs.mkdtempSync` under `os.tmpdir()`, and the script refuses an output path under `reference/` before spawning. It prints the oracle's summary line, then compares bytes with `reference/golden_data.json`. When the bytes differ it compares the two files as parsed JSON in Python (exact integers, where `JSON.parse` would round above 2^53), prints whether they are equal as parsed and, if not, at most twenty differing paths. On any failure (oracle exit status, missing summary, failed checks, different bytes) the temporary file is kept and its path printed, and nothing is copied into the repository. Run on 2026-10-10 in this container with Python 3.13.16: `6649 checks passed, 0 failed, 154.5s`, byte for byte identical (455014 bytes); the three hashes of CONTRACTS.md 9 unchanged. What the specification left open: the interpreter variable and the retention rule. Default used: none. Reason: SPEC 5.7 and the plan (P0-A6 instruction 5.2).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: numbers-in-prose.mjs
Decision: `scripts/numbers-in-prose.mjs` scans `site/src/content/modules/*.mdx` in sorted order and writes `reports/numbers-in-prose.md`; it always exits 0 (a report for a person). A number is a maximal run of digits with its grouping characters (comma, U+202F, U+2009 or a space, each followed by three digits), an optional decimal part and an optional leading `-` or U+2212 that does not follow a letter or digit; digits inside a word such as `E8` are reported. Excluded: the frontmatter, `import` and `export` statements, fenced code blocks, code spans, every tag with all its props (multi-line tags included), `<G>` and `<Tex>` elements with their contents, standalone brace expressions, heading ids in braces, Markdown link and image destinations and reference definitions. Digits inside `$...$` and `$$...$$` are listed in a second section, "Numbers inside formulas not written with <Tex>". With no lesson file the report holds the line "No lesson files found under site/src/content/modules/." What the specification left open: the scanner's details. Default used: none. Reason: SPEC 7.7 Content row and the plan (P0-A6 instruction 5.3).

It requires `mode` to be `release` and the Markdown file to be present, prints `report: open items report of <generatedAt>: N open items used on M pages, K unknown items; see reports/open-items-report.md` (N: items with status `open` and at least one page; M: their distinct pages; K: `unknownItems`) and exits 0; without a release report it prints `report: no release build report found at reports/open-items-report.json; run RELEASE=1 npm run build first` and exits 1, so that a missing report never looks like an empty one. It never builds (a release build would replace the preview build in `site/dist`). ## 2026-10-10 P0-A6: the BASE_PATH build directory, .gitignore and testIgnore
Decision: the `BASE_PATH=/sub/path/` build goes to `OUT_DIR=dist-basepath`, which `astro.config.mjs` resolves from `site/` to `site/dist-basepath/`, so `site/dist` keeps the default build that `npm run e2e` and `npm run size` use. `.gitignore` gains `site/public/katex/` (P0-A3's request) and `site/dist-basepath/`; no pattern was removed. `playwright.config.ts` gains `testIgnore: process.env.BASE_PATH ? [] : ['**/basepath.spec.ts']`, so the default run never includes the spec, which fails (never skips) with "basepath.spec.ts runs only through npm run e2e:basepath" when `BASE_PATH` is not `/sub/path/`. The spec checks every same-origin request (pathname under `/sub/path/`, status 200 or 304), forbids requests to other origins, checks every URL attribute of the DOM (`a`, `link`, `script`, `img`, `source`, `video`, `iframe`, `object`, SVG `use` and `image`) and fetches each internal URL expecting 200 under the base, on the index page and on every other route enumerated from the `.html` files of the `OUT_DIR` directory. What the specification left open: the directory name and the test's method. Default used: none. Reason: SPEC 7.2 item 10; the plan (P0-A6 instruction 5.6, 5.7, 5.9).

No default of SPEC section 10 was needed (D14 and D17 are context only). No dependency was added to any `package.json`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: the golden path grammar (golden.ts)
Decision: `resolveGoldenPath` splits the trimmed path at dots outside brackets; each step is `key`, `key[index]` or `key[field=value;...]`, exactly the three forms of SPEC 7.3. What the specification left open: text comparison and error forms. Defaults used (plan 5.2): an integer field compares as its decimal digits, a string as it stands, a list as its entries joined by commas with no spaces (each entry by the same rule), a boolean as `true`/`false`, `null` as `null`; an object never matches; a record lacking the field does not match. Messages: `path <path> resolves to <n> values. The step "<step>" <why>.` with the path as written; malformed paths (empty step, unclosed bracket, a step starting with `[`, chained brackets, a negative or non-integer index, a selection term without `field=`) give `path <path> is malformed: step "<step>" ...` and `count` 0. `GoldenPathError` has `name`, `path` and `count`. Surrounding whitespace of the path is trimmed; whitespace inside a step is kept.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: value formatting
Decision: integers with U+2212 for the sign and U+202F between groups of three from five digits (the sign not counted); lists of integers as `(a, b, c)` with a comma and one ordinary space, grouping and U+2212 inside; lists of strings the same way with each string as it stands (`(A1~, A2^(2))`); strings as they stand (so the decimal strings of `bigint_test` print ungrouped and keep any hyphen-minus); `as="word"`: entries joined by U+2009, each with U+2212 if negative and no grouping; `as="count"`: the length formatted as an integer; `as="plain"`: no grouping and no brackets, for an integer and for every entry of a list (U+2212 kept). Booleans, `null`, non-integer numbers, objects, nested or mixed lists, `count`/`word` on a non-list, and an unknown `as` throw `GoldenPathError` whose message says what was found and suggests `<Tex>` or a table. `goldenProse(path, as)` (an extra export used by `<G>`) resolves and formats and adds the path to a formatting error. U+00A0 is never used. Reason: SPEC 7.3 and the plan 5.3.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: the TeX substitution
Decision: `substituteGoldenInTex(src)` replaces each `{{path}}` (inner whitespace trimmed) by the value in TeX form: integers with ASCII `-` and `\,` between groups from five digits (the prose threshold), lists as `(a, b)`, strings as they stand; anything else throws. A `{{` with no `}}` throws naming the `src`. Every `{{` is a golden path, even where it would be TeX (`\frac{{a}}{b}`); the error message of a failed path inside `<Tex>` says so and suggests `{ {`. Everything else passes through unchanged.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: the four components and their hooks
Decision: `<G>` prints `<span class="g" data-golden="<k>">...</span>` (no style, no script), throws on a missing `k` or an `as` outside word, count, plain. `<Tex>` emits KaTeX's markup through `<Fragment set:html>` with no wrapper, calls only `renderTex` from `@lib/katex` (KaTeX 0.16.47), and treats `display` as a presence flag (`Boolean(display)`). `<Cite>` prints `<a class="cite" data-cite="<k>" href="<base>sources#<k>" title="<citeTitle(k)>">[<k>]</a>`. `<Unverified>` prints, in a preview, `<span class="unverified" data-unverified="<item>"><slot/> <a class="unverified-mark" href="<base>open-items#<item>" title="Rests on item <item> of Appendix E; see its status on the open-items page">[unverified <item>]</a></span>` (mark `[unverified <item>, not in unverified.json]` for an unknown item; same mark for open and closed items); in a release, the children alone for a closed item, `<span class="unverified-fallback" data-unverified="<item>">fallback</span>` for an open or unknown item with a fallback, nothing without one. The mark is a dotted underline in `var(--text)` plus the bracketed link text (not colour alone), with a `var(--focus-ring)` outline on focus, in a scoped style. `<base>` is `import.meta.env.BASE_URL` joined with exactly one slash. None of the components has a `try`; the three that fail the build do so by an uncaught exception in the frontmatter. Hooks: `data-golden`, `data-cite`, `data-unverified`; tests find formulas by `<math`.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: anchor contracts for P1-A4
Decision: `<Cite k="X">` links to `<base>sources#X`, the key as it stands (letters, digits, `-`, `+`; no encoding); P1-A4 gives each entry `id="<key>"`. `<Unverified item="E4">` links to `<base>open-items#E4`; P1-A4 gives each item `id="<id>"`. A key defined in 11.0 or 11.7 to 11.9 (none today) is accepted by `<Cite>`, but the sources page publishes only 11.1 to 11.6: P1-A4 must decide what such a link points at if one ever appears.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: the SOURCES.md parser (sources.ts)
Decision: a key is any `**[KEY]**` with KEY matching `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+-]*`, anywhere on a non-heading line (paragraph start, list item, mid-line); 95 keys today, no duplicates; the spellings S, S2, V, U, D are excluded. `section` is the number of the last `### 11.k` heading above the key; `line` is 1-based; `text` runs from the key marker (included) to the next key marker or the next heading, trimmed; `firstLine` is `text` to its first line break; `citeTitle` drops the leading key marker, `**`/`*` emphasis, reduces `[text](url)` to text, removes `<url>` autolinks, leaves `$...$` untouched, and collapses whitespace (a removed autolink can leave `Free: .`; accepted as the plan's rule). The first bold definition of a key wins should one ever repeat. Plain string processing on `SOURCES.md?raw`; no Markdown library.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: unverified.json seed
Decision: a JSON array of seventeen objects `{ id, status: "open", statement, whereToCheck, gate }`, E1 to E17, transcribed from Appendix E with bold markers removed and `$...$` kept as text. `statement` is the bold title with the rest of its sentence plus the sentences that state the claim; `whereToCheck` is the item's instruction. Where an item gives no instruction separate from its claim (E9, E10, E11, E13, E14, E16), `whereToCheck` repeats the item's own words naming what to check (for E10 with the first word capitalised), so that the field is never empty and adds no fact. Gates from 9.1: E6 G2; E2, E3 G3; E7, E9 G4; the rest G6. All open, E12 included (P0-A3's spike is evidence; closing is the owner's act). Closed items will add `closedOn` and `closedBy`, which the type accepts.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: release mode, unknown items, and the open-items report
Decision: `isReleaseBuild()` is `process.env.RELEASE !== undefined && process.env.RELEASE !== ''`, read at call time (so `RELEASE=0` is a release). `decide`: preview gives `marked` for every item; release gives `plain` for a closed item, else `fallback` or `nothing`. An item not in `unverified.json` or not of the form `E<digits>` is treated as open and listed under `unknownItems`. `registerUsage(item, Astro.url.pathname, hasFallback)` (the third argument, optional, is an addition so that the report and the stderr line can say whether the fallback was shown); the page is stored as given, base included. In a release build only, the registry writes `<dir>/open-items-report.json` and `<dir>/open-items-report.md`, where `<dir>` is `OPEN_ITEMS_REPORT_DIR` when set and non-empty, else `reports/` under the first ancestor of `process.cwd()` holding `reference/golden_data.json` (created if absent). JSON shape: `{ mode: 'release', generatedAt, buildId?, items: [{ id, status, gate, pages, fallbackShown }], unknownItems: [{ item, pages }] }`, every item listed, pages sorted; `fallbackShown` is true when at least one usage of the item rendered its fallback in this release. `buildId` is an extra field (P0-A6's `report.mjs` ignores it). The Markdown file is a table of all seventeen items (pages, fallback shown) and a table of unknown items. One `console.warn` line per new (item, page) pair in a release: `open item E4 used on / (fallback shown)` or `(nothing shown)`; none for a closed item; nothing printed or written in a preview, so an existing release report is never touched by a preview build.
Mechanism: `openItemsReportIntegration()` returns an integration named `open-items-report` whose `astro:build:start` hook, in a release build only, sets `process.env.OPEN_ITEMS_BUILD_ID` and writes the baseline (all items, no pages). After each registration the registry rewrites both files from its map merged with the pages already in the file when the file carries the same `buildId`, so that registrations from more than one module instance of one build are kept. The release fixture's report lists every registration of its page; the merge is unit-tested on its own. Until MERGE adds the integration to `site/astro.config.mjs`, a release build of a site with no `<Unverified>` writes no report: `RELEASE=1 npm run build` on this branch wrote none (checked).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A4: the fixture builds
Decision: committed fixtures under `site/tests/fixtures/content/{good,empty,bad-g,bad-cite,bad-tex}/pages/`, built one at a time by `site/tests/fixtures/content/build-fixture.mjs` in a child Node process: it loads `unverifiedRegistry.ts` through Vite's `runnerImport` (plain Node cannot import a TypeScript module that imports JSON without an import attribute) and calls Astro's `build({ root: site, srcDir: <case>, outDir: <temp>, logLevel: 'error', integrations: [openItemsReportIntegration()] })`, so the site's own `astro.config.mjs` applies. The child runs with `cwd` = `site/` and an environment from which `RELEASE`, `BASE_PATH`, `SITE_URL`, `OUT_DIR`, `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, `OPEN_ITEMS_BUILD_ID` and the variables Vitest adds (`VITEST*`, `BASE_URL`, `MODE`, `DEV`, `PROD`, `SSR`, `NODE_ENV`, `TEST`) are removed before the case sets its own: with Vitest's variables inherited, the child's build ignored `BASE_PATH=/sub/path/` and linked from `/`. Each build has a 240 s timeout (test timeout 300 s); a build took 3 to 5 s here. Output and report directories come from `mkdtemp` and are removed in `finally`. `.astro` files cannot be imported directly in Vitest (the root configuration does not use `getViteConfig`); the container API was not used and the root Vitest configuration was not changed.

Added `site/src/components/content/astro-modules.d.ts`, a fallback `declare module '*.astro'` typed as `AstroComponentFactory`, inside the directory this agent owns. The barrel keeps its name.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: the formula on the index page
Decision: the one formula is the mutation rule of SPEC 2.2, `p_i \longmapsto p_i - \sum_{j} C_{ij}\,p_j`, typeset in display style by `renderTex` in the frontmatter of `index.astro` (MathML included). It contains no golden value; the Cartan matrix of A2 is not typeset (a golden value, `types.A2.cartan`). The caption's two results, (−1, 0) and (1, 1), are computed by the engine's `mutate` in the frontmatter, never typed. Reason: the instruction, section 5.11.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: the noscript line
Decision: "Interactive when scripts are allowed." inside a fixed-height line of the island (24 pixels) that is present, empty, when scripts run, so the line does not shift the caption between the two cases. What the specification left open: the wording (7.4: "says that the figure is interactive when scripts are allowed"). Reason: one short line that fits at 320 pixels.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: undo, redo, scrub and reset announcements; one move
Decision: Undo announces `Undid move k. Populations now ...` (the position after undoing). Redo announces the move template of the move put back (`Mutated city 1. Populations now minus 1, 0. Move 1.`). Scrubbing to an earlier position announces the undo template for the earliest undone move, to a later one the move template of the last move re-applied; a burst of either coalesces in the announcer. Reset announces `Undid move 1. Populations now 1, 0.` on the index page when at least one move was applied, and nothing when the cursor was already at the start. Undo, redo and reset with nothing to do announce nothing. Pluralisation: "Game over after 1 move." and "Played 1 move to the end."; every other count takes "moves". The announcer writes 500 ms after the last call of a burst (a trailing window), so a single move is announced once, half a second after it is made; the same text twice is cleared and set again 50 ms later. What the specification left open: these cases (A-4 gives the templates only). Reason: the plan (instruction 5.7); SPEC fixes no wording at one move.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: the undid signature extension (deviation)
Decision: `announceTemplates.undid(move, values, noun = 'numbers')`: an optional trailing parameter `'numbers' | 'populations'` chooses the noun; the default keeps the contract's `undid(move, numbers)` behaviour. What the specification left open: A-4 gives the undo template for numbers only. Reason: the Populations view needs "Populations now ..." (instruction 5.7). Reported under `deviations` for MERGE.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: hooks on the seed's root
Decision: the island root carries `data-tool="board"` (T1's id), `data-moves` (the number of moves applied, equal to the move counter `data-out="moveCounter"`) and, once hydrated, `data-hydrated="true"`. Other outputs: `data-out="historyStrip"` (the strip), `data-out="historyCounter"` (the slider's counter). Drawing hooks for the tests: `data-disc`, `data-numeral`, `data-label` (labels). `data-value` is the engine's `arith.show()` text with an ASCII hyphen; the numeral on screen uses U+2212; screen readers hear "minus". What the specification left open: the hook for hydration and the names of the strip outputs. Reason: SPEC 4.0 Test hooks; P0-A6's size classifier counts `data-tool` to hold the index page to the 60 kB row.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: Base.astro created; the stylesheet link
Decision: `site/src/layouts/Base.astro` did not exist and was created: `<html lang="en">`, charset and viewport metas, `<title>` from a prop, optional description meta, the `base.css` import (moved from the placeholder index page), the KaTeX stylesheet `<link rel="stylesheet" href={katexStylesheetHref(import.meta.env.BASE_URL)} />` (moved from `index.astro`'s head with its `href` expression unchanged, and its import from `@lib/katex`), a skip link to `#main` as the first focusable element (plan; WCAG 2.4.1), a `<nav aria-label="Site">` with Home, About and Credits, `<main id="main">`, and a `<footer>` holding a second nav with the same links. All hrefs from `import.meta.env.BASE_URL`. No page-level live region: a comment at the top says that each tool renders its own polite region (A-4). The layout ships no `<script>`. What the specification left open: the shell's markup. Reason: the instruction, section 5.12.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: P0-A3's footer request deferred
Decision: P0-A3 asked (merge report of stage 0.2) that the footer print `KATEX_VERSION`; P0-A1 noted the licence line of 7.9 as a footer debt. Neither is printed: the footer with the pinned versions and the licences is P6-A6's (README section 4, my instruction 5.12). The KaTeX version and licence appear on the credits page. Reason: the instruction.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: the credits list is generated at build time
Decision: `credits.astro` reads, in its frontmatter, the root, engine and site `package.json` files (found from the working directory by walking up to the package.json with `workspaces`), takes every name in `dependencies` and `devDependencies`, and reads each installed package's `package.json` (looked up from its workspace as Node would) for the version, the `license` field, the author (or, when no author field exists, the copyright line of the package's LICENSE file) and the repository. 17 rows, sorted by name; each row says where it is used and whether it reaches the reader: `astro` (the island loader inlined in pages with a tool), `@astrojs/svelte` (the client renderer), `svelte` (the runtime), `katex` (stylesheet and fonts, no script); everything else is build and tests only, the engine's three development dependencies among them. No licence was unreadable. KaTeX's fonts: the installed package 0.16.47 carries one notice, its `LICENSE` file ("The MIT License (MIT)", "Copyright (c) 2013-2020 Khan Academy and other contributors"), and no separate notice for the fonts (looked for `OFL.txt`, `fonts/LICENSE`, `dist/fonts/LICENSE`, `dist/fonts/OFL.txt`); the page states that as found. Reason: SPEC 7.9, 9.3; the instruction 5.14.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: size rule and the reserved box
Decision: the board's grid unit is computed in CSS from the width of the figure, which is an inline-size container: `--u: min(64px, round(down, (100cqw - 16px) / k, 1px))` (without the `round()` floor where the browser lacks it); the board's box is `k·u` by `rows·u` plus 8 pixels of padding, and each node button is a square of `min(44px, u)` centred on the node. Below 400 pixels the figure runs from edge to edge of the viewport (negative margins equal to the page gutter). The island root has a fixed height in CSS (board, a 108-pixel History block, a 24-pixel line for the noscript note, two 12-pixel gaps), so hydration changes no size. What the specification left open: how W is measured. Default used: none (6.1 is [D], applied as written). Reason: SPEC 6.1, 7.6.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: keyboard details
Decision: the roving tab stop starts on the lowest label and afterwards follows the last focused node; arrows, Home and End move it with `preventDefault()` and are ignored with Alt, Control or Meta held; Enter and Space act through the button's native click only. `u` and `r` are caught by the island root and accepted only when the key was pressed inside the board group (`data-board-group`) with no modifier (plan; A-1 says nothing on modifiers), so Board's prop list stays exactly as CONTRACTS 5.1. After hydration Undo, Redo and Reset stay enabled at every cursor and do nothing when there is nothing to do. Every past position in the strip is a button up to 50 positions; from 51 the strip is an `<input type="range">` with a counter "k of n". Reason: the plan (instruction 5.5, 5.8).

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: drawing choices
Decision: discs of radius 0.25 grid units; a second ring at 0.32 for "may act here" (every node in free play) in the text colour, with a pointer cursor on the button; positive values filled `--sign-pos-fill` with the numeral in `--sign-pos-numeral`; zero and negative values outlined (`--sign-zero-outline`, `--sign-neg-outline`) with no fill and the numeral in `--text`; roads end at the rings; labels in `--text` above right of each node; the disc's fill and stroke have a 150 ms transition, removed under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. The city icon on the index page is an inline SVG of my own, stroked in `--accent-populations`, beside the word Populations, which is underlined in the same accent. No colour literal in any component. Reason: SPEC 6.3, 8.3, A-9.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: measured sizes
Decision: none; record. `npm run size` on the preview build: `/` one-board 22.1 kB of 60 (3 scripts); `/about/` 0.0 kB of 15 (0 scripts); `/credits/` 0.0 kB of 15 (0 scripts); `/dev/math-check/` 0.0 kB. Both phase-0 limits of SPEC 7.6 hold.

## 2026-10-10 P0-A5: defaults of SPEC 10 relied on
Decision: D1, D4 (content side; the code side is the owner's AGPL), D5, as above. No other default of section 10 was needed.

## 2026-10-10 MERGE: P1 stage 1.1

# Decisions of P1-A1 (engine phase 1: matrices, translator, plays, searches, roots, finite-type data)

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: versions observed
Decision: built and tested against node v22.22.0, typescript 6.0.3, vitest 5.0.3, fast-check 4.10.2, all as installed by `npm ci --prefer-offline` from the committed lockfile. What the specification left open: nothing; recorded as the instruction asks (5.15). Default used: none. Reason: the toolchain postdates training data (COMMON.md 3); the type definitions of `fc.assert` (`seed`, `numRuns`), `fc.property`, `fc.constantFrom`, `fc.array`, `fc.integer`, `fc.bigInt` and Vitest's `it(name, { timeout }, fn)` were read under `node_modules/` before use.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: parameter types of the rational helpers, and ratNeg
Decision: `rat(num: bigint | number, den: bigint | number = 1n)`; the seven other helpers take and return `Rational` (`{ num: bigint; den: bigint }`, den > 0n, lowest terms, zero as `{ 0n, 1n }`). A `number` argument must be a safe integer, else `RangeError`; a zero denominator and `ratDiv` by zero throw `RangeError`. One extra helper is exported, `ratNeg(a)`, used by `determinant`. `ratEq` compares by cross product so that it is right even on an unnormalised input. What the specification left open: parameter types (CONTRACTS.md 2.4 names only the functions). Default used: the plan's (P1-A1 instruction 5.2). Reason: E-3, no library; the harness's `ratText` and `ratToString` agree on every value (unit test).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: determinant by rational elimination; the empty matrix has determinant 1
Decision: `determinant` is the oracle's method (lines 995 to 1009) over `Rational`: pivot the first nonzero entry at or below the diagonal, negate on a row swap, eliminate below. The empty matrix gives 1, as in the oracle. A non-square matrix or a non-integer `number` entry is a `RangeError`. What the specification left open: the elimination method ("fraction-free or rational elimination"). Default used: rational elimination. Reason: the matrices have at most 12 rows and the method matches the oracle line for line; Bareiss would give the same value.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: solve and inverse, null on a singular matrix
Decision: `solve(M, b): Rational[] | null` (Gauss-Jordan, the oracle's lines 656 to 669) and `inverse(M): Rational[][] | null` (one Gauss-Jordan on `[M | I]`, equal to the oracle's column-by-column solves) return `null` when `M` is singular, where the oracle raises `StopIteration`. `solve` throws `RangeError` on a right-hand side of the wrong length. Entry types: `number` (integer), `bigint` or `Rational`, mixed within a call. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 2.4 names only `determinant`; 2.1 places `solve` and `inverse` in `linalg.ts`. Default used: the plan's signatures (5.3). Reason: consistent with `toPopulation` returning `null`; raised under `openQuestions` in the report as a contract addition to confirm.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: transposeBoard and subBoard: name, arith, kind; the Board<T> overloads
Decision: `transposeBoard` returns `C^T` in fresh frozen rows, a copy of the labels, the board's own `arith`, the `boardKind` of the transposed integer matrix, and the finite name whose standard matrix equals `C^T` under `P0-A2`'s phase-0 rule (`finiteNameOf`, reused unchanged, families A to G at rank n), with the exception that the `C2` matrix stays unnamed; otherwise `name` is undefined. The match is by matrix only, so a sub-board whose matrix is a standard one gets a name on transposition although its labels may not be 1..n; `identify` settles names in phase 2. `subBoard` sorts the chosen positions, keeps their labels unchanged (E-7), takes the kind of the induced matrix, the board's arithmetic and no name. Both have the overload `<T>(board: Board<T>, ...): Board<T>` before `(board: AnyBoard): AnyBoard`; `components` is generic `<T>(board: Board<T>)`, which accepts `AnyBoard`. What the specification left open: `name`, `arith`, `kind` of derived boards. Default used: the plan's rules (5.4). Reason: a sub-board of a finite board is finite (E-2 gives no reason for `Big`); the rank-2 computed name is `identify`'s business (COMMON.md 2); the overloads keep the arithmetic type in `finite.ts` and the tools without `any`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: imports added at the top of moves.ts
Decision: the appended code of `moves.ts` needs `Matrix` from `arith.ts`, `determinant` and `solve` from `linalg.ts` and the `Rational` type; these three import lines were added to the import block at the top of `moves.ts`. No phase-0 statement was changed. What the specification left open: the protocol says "append after P0-A2's code". Default used: imports at the top, as ES style requires. Reason: an import placed after other statements is legal but misleading; the change is three import lines, reviewable at a glance.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: RangeError cases added
Decision: `RangeError` on a position outside 0..n-1 (`mutationMatrix`, `firingMatrix`, `kostantPlay`, `subBoard`, `longestWord`), a vector of the wrong length (`toNumbers`, `toPopulation`, `applyWord`, `legalNodes`, a seed of `orbitGraph`), an empty or repeated node list (`subBoard`, `longestWord`), an unknown `game` string (`applyWord`, `orbitGraph`), a cap that is not a non-negative integer (the searches and the plays), a `maxDepth` that is not a non-negative integer, more distinct seeds than the cap, an empty seed list, `legalOnly` with `'mutation'`, an index outside the items (`wordTo`), a `choose` that returns a node not allowed, a non-integer `number` given to `rat` or to a matrix. What the specification left open: invalid arguments the specification does not cover. Default used: `RangeError`, as `P0-A2` did for `mutate`, `fire` and `toPositions`. Reason: E-10 reserves the engine's own classes for the theory; a cap is never thrown.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: mutationMatrix and firingMatrix return number entries
Decision: both return `number[][]` whatever the board's arithmetic, converting `bigint` entries with `Number`. What the specification left open: the entry type of the matrices. Default used: plain numbers (CONTRACTS.md 2.4). Reason: road counts are small; `verify.py` and the property tests multiply these matrices as integers.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: argument rules of orbitGraph
Decision: `cap` is an integer at least the number of distinct seeds (the oracle asserts `len(graph) <= cap`); an empty seed list is a `RangeError` (the oracle's `max` over an empty graph fails); `maxDepth`, when given, is a non-negative integer; a repeated seed is kept once at its first occurrence; `legalOnly` is defined for `'firing'` only and with `'mutation'` is a `RangeError`. Positions are keyed by `arith.show` of the entries joined with commas. The order and truncation rule is SPEC.md 5.5 verbatim: `completeLayers` is the depth of the position being expanded when the cap acts, also when the cap falls exactly at the end of a layer (the triangle with a cap of 9 reports 1). `depthReached` is the depth of the last item, since breadth-first order has non-decreasing depth. No edges are stored (SPEC.md 5.4). What the specification left open: argument validation. Default used: the plan's (5.6). Reason: port of `orbit_graph_capped` with the `legal_only` test of `orbit_graph`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: orbit keeps breadth-first order and reports truncation
Decision: `orbit` returns `{ items, truncated }` of `orbitGraph` with the same arguments, in the same order. What the specification left open: the oracle's `orbit` without `max_depth` returns an unordered set and raises at the cap. Default used: the plan's (5.6). Reason: E-6 and E-10 (a cap is reported, never thrown); one search routine for every caller.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: positiveRoots: default cap, no truncation report, the internal helper
Decision: `positiveRoots(board, cap = CAPS.mainThreadItems, maxDepth?)` returns a plain sorted array and cannot report truncation; callers off the finite types use `roots` with `maxDepth`. The sort is by total in the board's arithmetic, then entry by entry, through `arith.sign(arith.sub(...))`, never through text. The filter-and-sort step is the module export `positiveRootsFrom(board, items)` of `search.ts`, shared with `finite.ts` so that the finite functions run one search and still see `truncated`; it is not re-exported from `index.ts` and is not part of the public API. What the specification left open: the default cap and the truncation report. Default used: the plan's (5.6). Reason: E-9's main-thread bound; a finite board of at most 12 nodes has at most 288 roots.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: the default caps of the plays and the wording of the cap rule
Decision: `play`, `raisingPlay` and `kostantPlay` default to `cap = CAPS.playMoves` (20000). The rule: when no node is allowed the result is `'ended'`, even at exactly `cap` moves; when nodes are allowed and `word.length === cap` the result is `'cap'` with exactly `cap` moves, before move `cap + 1`. A cap of 0 gives `'cap'` with 0 moves when a move is allowed and `'ended'` otherwise. `choose` receives the allowed positions in increasing order and the current vector. What the specification left open: the oracle's default of 1000000 raises instead of reporting; `play_fate` (phase 2) tests its cap after recording the move. Default used: the plan's wording (5.7). Reason: SPEC.md 5.6 ("stop, `status: 'cap'`, reported as still running after 20000 moves") and E-10.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: the meaning of fired in raisingPlay
Decision: `fired[k]` is the number `(C p)_i` of the city at the moment it is mutated, negative for every raising move, so the city rises by `-fired[k]`; `-1` throughout on a simply laced board (F13). What the specification left open: the field has no stated meaning for this play. Default used: the plan's (5.7). Reason: it is the quantity the bridge sentence of 2.4(a) names, and it lets a tool show the rise.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: internal caps and the NotFiniteType fallback of finite.ts
Decision: the internal root search of the six finite functions is `roots(board, CAPS.workerPositions)`, and the internal play of `longestWord` has the cap `CAPS.workerPositions`; a truncated search, a play at its cap, a count of dominant positive roots other than 1 or 2 in `highestShortRoot`, or a `-w0` image that is not a unit vector in `minusW0` throws `NotFiniteType`. Each function's first statement is a comment marking where `P2-A1`'s guard call goes; no work precedes it. No test pins a finite function on a one-way board. `coxeterNumber` divides as integers through `bigint`. What the specification left open: E-11 says the guards are phase 2; E-6 still requires a bound. Default used: the plan's (5.8). Reason: `NotFiniteType` is the class SPEC.md 5.6 gives for a `gcm` board off the finite types; `P2-A1`'s guard throws `NeedsTwoWayRoads` first on a one-way board.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: placement of Rational, Enumeration, OrbitGraph, PlayResult
Decision: `Rational` is declared in `rational.ts`, `Enumeration` and `OrbitGraph` in `search.ts`, `PlayResult` in `play.ts`, all verbatim from SPEC.md 5.2 and re-exported as types from `index.ts`. What the specification left open: the file. Default used: the plan's placement (5.2, 5.6, 5.7). Reason: each type lives with the functions that return it.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: fast-check seeds and run counts
Decision: every property file records `SEED = 20261010` (the seed of `P0-A2`'s property 1) and its `NUM_RUNS` literally: p2-translator 500, p3-transpose 500, p4-orders 200 (per type, the pairs exhaustive), p6-convergence 300, p7-roots-signs 200, p8-generic-game 300; the unit test p1-rational-linalg uses the same seed with 200 and 300 runs. Each file asserts that no input vector was changed and contains a check that the property is not vacuous (the two orders of property 6 differ in many runs; both signs occur in property 7; a joined pair does not commute in property 4). What the specification left open: the seeds and counts. Default used: the plan's minimum of 200 runs. Reason: CONTRACTS.md 3, "a fixed seed recorded in the file".

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: helper and unit test files
Decision: `test/helpers/p1.ts` exports `bigBoard`, `unitVectors`, `ones`, `tallyOf`, `seededChoose` (mulberry32, floating point allowed in tests), `digits`; the unit files are `test/unit/p1-rational-linalg.test.ts`, `p1-boards.test.ts`, `p1-search.test.ts`, `p1-plays.test.ts`, `p1-finite.test.ts`, `p1-api.test.ts`. The golden files narrow `golden.kostant` and `golden.layer_growth_examples` with local interfaces, since the harness declares them `unknown`. `p1-layer-growth.test.ts` runs the full search of each board once and shares it between its three tests, with a per-test timeout of 60 seconds. What the specification left open: the test layout beyond the golden and property files. Default used: the plan's (5.12). Reason: README section 4, every build agent owns its own tests.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A1: P0-A2's harness.test.ts pins the exact export list of index.ts
Decision: no change to `test/unit/harness.test.ts` (a phase-0 test file, not mine). Its test "has every runtime name of the instruction, section 5.7" asserts `Object.keys(api).sort()` equal to the 21 phase-0 names, so it fails once `index.ts` carries the phase-1 exports that CONTRACTS.md 2.1 and this instruction require. Reported to `MERGE` under `requests`: replace the equality of line 292 by an inclusion check, or extend the list by the phase-1 names; `p1-api.test.ts` pins the full phase-1 list. What the specification left open: nothing; the two instructions conflict. Default used: the contract (append the exports). Reason: the exports are the deliverable; the test's intent (every phase-0 name present) still holds and is also asserted by `p1-api.test.ts`.
