## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-ab4c4e37539ead383`, branch `worktree-agent-ab4c4e37539ead383`, fast-forwarded to the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` at `4dbf4f8` (G0 green) before any work; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. Playwright ran with `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` pointing at the prepared directory of symlinks; nothing was downloaded. By the session's instructions the root `package.json` and the lockfile were not edited and `npm run size` was not run.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: nanostores 1.5.5
Decision: `nanostores` 1.5.5 was already pinned exactly in `site/package.json` and in the lockfile at `4dbf4f8`, so nothing was installed. Licence MIT (read in `node_modules/nanostores/package.json`). Shipped: `highlight.ts` imports only `atom` (`atom/index.js` 4115 bytes, 1543 bytes gzip level 9, plus the small `clean-stores` helper it imports); `state.ts` adds `map` (315 bytes gzip) for linked tools. Reason: SPEC 7.1, 9.3.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: engine imports by package name in lib/
Decision: `site/src/lib/*` and the worker import the engine as `@two-games/engine`, the package the `@engine` alias of CONTRACTS 1 points at, and import each other by relative path. What the specification left open: nothing; CONTRACTS 1 gives the alias. Reason: the root Vitest project `site-unit` does not read tsconfig paths (Vite 8's `resolve.tsconfigPaths` defaults to false), so `@engine` does not resolve in unit tests; tried in a scratch test. In the browser bundle both names resolve to `packages/engine/src/index.ts`, one module. Request to MERGE: `resolve: { tsconfigPaths: true }` (or the aliases) in the `site-unit` project of the root Vitest configuration, after which the alias can be used everywhere.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: LINK_NOTICE wording
Decision: `The link could not be read, so the tool shows its default state.` Shown by tools through ToolFrame's `notice` prop in `data-out="notice"`. What the specification left open: the wording (6.6 fixes the meaning). Reason: the instruction's plan text.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: the noscript wording
Decision: ToolFrame reuses P0-A5's sentence `Interactive when scripts are allowed.`, inside a fixed-height line, wrapped in `<span data-out="noscript">`. Reason: one wording on the site (decisions/P0-A5.md).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: URL form details
Decision: (1) Key order `z g v m c s w x t r j e y k q d`, `z=1` always first, a key only when its field is present; `w=` is written for an empty move list that is present. (2) Prefix: `'b2'` and `'b2.'` both give `b2.`; empty or absent gives bare keys. (3) Only `& = # % +` and space are escaped (percent-encoded); commas, semicolons, dots, `~`, `^`, colons and `@` stay readable. (4) A key given twice (after the prefix) does not parse (rule 1). (5) `q` is a code among `hh he hf ef serre-e serre-f`, a colon and exactly two labels separated by a comma; `j` is `coxeter` or `xyz:a,b,c` with three distinct integers from 1 to n, optionally `@` and three integers (a minus sign allowed). (6) `lambda` (`d`) holds safe integers only; a larger entry fails rule 1. (7) Rule 4 counts digits without the sign (40 pass, 41 fail). (8) Board names in `g` match `[A-Za-z0-9~^()_-]+` before `boardFromName` is asked; anything it does not know fails rule 2. (9) The `@` part of `g` is kept when it parses as n pairs of numbers and dropped otherwise, never a notice. What the specification left open: these details. Reason: the instruction (5.2, 5.3).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: g without s and w, c without s
Decision: when `g` names a board other than the defaults' board, the effective state takes `start = defaultStart(newBoard, coords)` (when the defaults hold a start) and `moves = []` (when they hold moves), as a picker does (4.0), and drops the board-bound tool fields of the old board (`black`, `tau`, `rings`, `symmetry`, `node`, `relation`, `lambda`) unless the link gives them; when `c` is present without `s`, `start = defaultStart(board, c)`. Rule 9 is also checked on the effective start. What the specification left open: these cases. Reason: the instruction (5.3), plan.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: rule 8 for an unregistered tool
Decision: `boardsOffered(id)` answers `'all'` for a tool with no registry entry, so the codec works before stage 1.2 registers tools. With a list, `g` must be one of its names and a custom board fails. Reason: the instruction (5.3), plan.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: long move lists, the LONG_LIST_REPLAY switch (deviation pending MERGE)
Decision: `urlCodec.ts` exports `LONG_LIST_REPLAY = 'sync'` (the contract reading, active and tested for G1): `decodeState` replays every list of up to 20000 moves itself and on a forbidden move returns `{ state: defaults, notice }`. The proposal, selected by `'worker'`: for a list of more than 2000 moves (`whereToRun({ board, kind: 'play', moves })` says worker) under a mode that restricts moves, `decodeState` returns the parsed state with `notice: null` and the extra field `replayCheck: true`; the tool then runs `engineCall('firstForbiddenMove', [board, state], hint)` in the worker and loads its defaults with `LINK_NOTICE` when it answers a number. `decodeStateWith(search, tool, defaults, { prefix, longListReplay })` (CONTRACTS 4 signature) makes the reading injectable; tests cover both. What the specification left open: E-9 rule 2 sends "a long list of moves read from a link" to the worker, while CONTRACTS 4 makes `decodeState` synchronous and says every rule gives defaults and the notice. Reason: the instruction (5.3); MERGE decides; switching is one constant, and the CONTRACTS 4 comment on `decodeState` would then read "rule 6 on more than 2000 moves is marked replayCheck and checked by the tool in the worker".

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: firstForbiddenMove lives in state.ts
Decision: `firstForbiddenMove(board, state)` is defined in `state.ts` and re-exported by `urlCodec.ts` (the plan name). It reads the numbers (`a = Cp` from a population, kept current by `fire`), legal means a positive number and raising a negative one; a non-safe start or an `EngineOverflow` replays on `bigBoard`, never a notice. Reason: the worker must run it without importing the tool registry (`urlCodec.ts` imports `tools/index.ts`, whose lazy component imports would be code-split inside the worker bundle).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: the worker's wire format and EngineApi
Decision: `EngineApi` is the callable members of the engine's exports plus `positionsOf` and `firstForbiddenMove`. Serialization lives in a new file `site/src/lib/workerWire.ts` (plan addition, shared by `worker.ts` and `engine.worker.ts`, so that the worker bundle never contains the code that starts a worker): bigint `{ "$big": "..." }`; Map `{ "$map": [[k, v], ...] }`; a board `{ "$board": BoardSpec }` (its name, or `{ cartan }` from `cartanNumbers`); a `bigBoard` copy of a named finite board `{ "$board": "E8", "arith": "Big" }`; `undefined` `{ "$undefined": true }` (plan, so that optional arguments survive JSON); functions refuse (`TypeError`). Errors travel as `{ name, message, data? }` and come back as the same E-10 class (`NotAffine` from its `data.class`, `NotAdmissible` with its `Admissibility`), `RangeError` and `TypeError` too; any other name becomes a plain `Error` with the message. A worker `error` event rejects every pending call; nothing falls back to the main thread. A board without a name travels by its matrix, so a sub-board's labels (phase 1, `subBoard`) are not carried; noted for P1-A1 and MERGE.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: whereToRun and the playFate check
Decision: the rules as in the instruction 5.4; `finiteKnown` defaults to `isNamedFinite(board)` (SafeInt arithmetic, or a name of a finite type on a `bigBoard` copy); `satake` goes to the worker for E7, E8, or when `expectedItems` is given. `engineCall` sends `playFate` to the worker whatever the hint says (E-9 rule 2 names it; a hint carries no function name). In Node (`import.meta.env.SSR`, or no `window`) every call runs in-process. Test seams: `__testing.setRuntime`, `__testing.reset`, `__testing.workerCreated`. The worker is built from `new Worker(new URL('./engine.worker.ts', import.meta.url), { type: 'module' })` inside the function that first needs it; Vite 8 emits it as `_astro/engine.worker-<hash>.js`, and `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` intercepts `/engine\.worker/`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: bigBoard, isNamedFinite, positionsOf
Decision: `bigBoard(board)` returns the same board with `Big` arithmetic, `bigint` entries, the same labels, kind and name, frozen; a Big board is returned as it is. `resolveBoard` gives every custom board `Big`, no name, labels 1..n, also when `fromAdjacency` returned SafeInt (a matrix equal to a named type's, or the unnamed C2 matrix). `positionsOf` reads `start` in label order (labels sorted increasingly, converted with `toPositions`), uses the default start when `start` is absent and `populations` when `coords` is absent, and replays on `bigBoard` on overflow or a non-safe start. Reason: the instruction (5.1), E-2, T1.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: linked tools, publishToolState and followToolState
Decision: `publishToolState(instanceId, state)` stores a copy holding only the fields of ToolState in one Nano Store `map` per page, keyed by instance id; `followToolState(instanceId, onChange)` calls `onChange` with a copy on every publish of that instance (and at once with the last published state, when there is one) and returns the function that stops the updates; `clearPublishedStates()` empties the map. A follower recomputes its own positions; positions never travel. Reason: SPEC 7.4 "Two tools share state only when the page says so with a linkTo property"; for P1-A5, P1-A6 and later tools.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: data export
Decision: `exportJSON(state, derived)` writes `{ "schema": 1, "tool", "state", "engineVersion", "commit", "derived" }` with two-space indentation and a final newline; the state is reduced to the fields of ToolState; bigint and non-safe integer numbers become decimal strings, safe integers stay numbers, floats stay numbers, `{ num, den }` rationals become `p/q` or `p`, Maps become lists of pairs. The engine version is a named JSON import of `packages/engine/package.json`. The commit is `import.meta.env.PUBLIC_GIT_COMMIT`, falling back to `"unknown"`. `downloadData(toolId, instanceId, state, derived)` gives `{ filename: "<toolId>-<instanceId or toolId>.json", text }`. ToolFrame imports `export.ts` with a dynamic `import()` on the first click. What the specification left open: the shape and the place of the commit. Reason: the instruction (5.5). The commit constant is NOT set by the build yet: this session was told not to edit the root `package.json`, and `astro.config.mjs` is P0-A1's. Request to MERGE: set it in the root scripts, for example `"build": "PUBLIC_GIT_COMMIT=$(git rev-parse --short HEAD) npm run build -w site"` (POSIX shell; works on macOS and Ubuntu; `build:release` calls `npm run build` and inherits it), or a `vite.define` in `astro.config.mjs`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: History API
Decision: `registerInstance(instanceId, defaults, onPopState?, initial?)` throws `Error("duplicate instance id <id>")` on a second registration; `defaults` is the author's state (links write only what differs from it) and `initial` the state shown now; registering writes nothing. `stateChanged` ignores a state equal (by encoding) to the one recorded, else schedules one `replaceState` 500 ms after the last change. `exampleLoaded` (one function for an example and a catalogue entry) records the state, cancels a pending burst and calls `pushState` at once. The query keeps every parameter that is not a codec key of a registered instance (another script's keys, and keys of instances not on the page), then appends each changed instance's keys (instances in id order); `pathname` and `hash` are kept. `linkFor(id)` is `location.origin` plus that URL, built now; `copyLink(id, clipboard)` writes it inside `try` and answers a boolean. `replaceState` and `pushState` are inside `try`, refusals swallowed silently. On `popstate` every instance re-reads the URL with `decodeState` against its defaults and its callback receives the state and the notice (plan). Reason: SPEC 7.4, the instruction (5.6).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: ToolFrame
Decision: props of CONTRACTS 5.1 plus `notice`, `figureLabel`, `defaults` and `onPopState` (plan); snippets `figure`, `controls`, `table` receive `{ hydrated, announce }` (`FrameApi`, exported from the component's module script). The root is a `<section data-frame data-tool data-moves data-hydrated aria-labelledby=title>`; the figure sits in a `<figure>` labelled by `figureLabel` (`aria-label`) or else by its `<figcaption data-out="caption">` (`aria-labelledby`). Undo, Redo, Reset (`data-control="undo|redo|reset"`) render after the figure only when the tool passes the callbacks. The `controls` snippet sits in `<fieldset data-controls disabled={!hydrated}>`; "Copy link" and "Download data" (`data-control="copyLink|downloadData"`) are disabled until hydration; a comment marks the place of the phase 2 and phase 6 buttons. "Show as table" is a `<details>` with that summary, the table inside a disabled fieldset until hydration. The live region is `<div role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true" data-live>`, empty, driven by `createAnnouncer`. Copy link announces `Link copied.` (or `The link could not be copied.`); Download data announces `Downloaded <filename>.`. Hydration is `hydrated = $state(false)` set in `$effect`; registration with `history.ts` happens in an `$effect`, and every change of `state` goes through `stateChanged`. Inside the component the prop `state` is read as `toolState`, because a local variable named `state` makes Svelte read `$state` as a store (a trap for tool authors too). Reason: the instruction (5.7), CONTRACTS 5.1.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: hydration test moved to the browser suite (deviation)
Decision: the check "hydration enables the controls" (instruction: `toolframe.test.ts`) is `site/tests/e2e/toolframe-hydration.spec.ts`: it bundles a fixture tool (`site/tests/fixtures/toolframe/`) with Vite and the Svelte plugin, serves it from a stub origin through page routes, and checks in Chromium that `data-hydrated="true"` is set and no `disabled` attribute is left, that moves update the bar once after a burst with bare keys and no push, that Copy link writes the current link and announces, and that Download data saves `board-board.json`. `toolframe.test.ts` keeps the server render. Reason: the `site-unit` project has no DOM environment and none can be added (no dependency may be added), and CI runs the unit tests before Chromium is installed. Test helper added: `site/tests/unit/_svelteSsr.ts` (server render of .svelte files through Vite in unit tests).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: registry type, default preset, toolRoutes
Decision: `ToolRegistryEntry = { number; id: ToolId; title; phase; boards: 'all' | string[]; presets: { default: PresetFields } & Record<string, PresetFields>; outs: string[]; component: () => Promise<unknown> }` with `PresetFields = Partial<Omit<ToolState, 'schema' | 'tool'>> & { caption: string }`, inferred from the CONTRACTS 5.2 example (which satisfies it). Every entry must have a `default` preset with a non-empty caption (SPEC 4.0 Configuration; the route renders it); `collectRegistry` throws at build time on a duplicate id or number, an id outside ToolId, or a missing default. `registry` is sorted by number. `toolRoutes(entries = registry)` returns `{ params: { tool }, props: { entry, caption } }` per entry, and `getStaticPaths` returns it as it is. The registry test also requires each entry's number to match its id in the table at the head of SPEC 4, and its other preset ids to be among those of CONTRACTS 6.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: hydrating a tool taken from the registry (trap 1; deviation)
Decision: `pages/tools/[tool].astro` cannot hydrate a component taken from the registry: in a scratch build on Astro 7.3.8 a component from `import.meta.glob` under `client:idle` fails with `NoMatchingImport` (Astro hydrates only components its compiler sees imported statically). So the page holds a static table: one `import` per tool and one line `{entry.id === '<id>' && <TNN... client:idle {...toolProps} />}`, plus the id in the set `HYDRATED`; MERGE adds these three edits when a tool lands. A registered tool without its line is still rendered at build time from its registry entry (`await entry.component()`), without hydration, and the build prints a warning; `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` then fails on that route because the tool never hydrates, which flags the missing line. Checked in the scratch build with a fixture registry entry and component: `site/dist/tools/board/index.html` exists, holds the default caption and the `data-tool` root, its island carries `client="idle"`, and `?z=1&g=A3` is decoded on that route after hydration. The tool receives `id=""` (bare keys), `caption` (the default preset's) and `preset="default"`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: playedToEnd extension
Decision: `announceTemplates.playedToEnd(moves, values, noun = 'numbers')`: the optional noun `'populations'` gives `Played 5 moves to the end. Populations now ...` (T9's raising play); without it the string is exactly the A-4 template. The existing announcer already coalesces a burst to its last text, which carries `Move N.` or `Played N moves`; nothing else was missing. Reason: the instruction (5.9), mirroring P0-A5's `undid`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: the run without JavaScript
Decision: `nojs.spec.ts` discovers every `.html` file of `site/dist` (or `OUT_DIR`) through `site/tests/e2e/routes.ts`, skipping only the asset folders `_astro` and `katex`. Per route: text in `main` (a page without a `main` landmark is read from its body: the phase-0 spike page `dev/math-check/` has none; reported); every KaTeX formula carries MathML (`.katex` count equals `.katex:has(math)` count; plan addition beyond 7.4, a check of A-12); every `<table>` of the page's own HTML is present; every `[data-prediction]` holds or is followed by a `<details>`. Per `[data-tool]`: an `svg` with content (or a snapshot `img`), a text alternative (the svg's `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `<title>`, the img's `alt`, the enclosing `<figure>`'s label or `<figcaption>`, or a labelled `role="group"`/`role="img"` inside the tool), a non-empty caption (`data-out="caption"` or the figure's figcaption), every `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` disabled (an attribute or a disabled fieldset), and a shown `<noscript>` line; per `[data-frame]` also a `<details>` holding a table with at least one button and at least one button per distinct `data-node` label. Broken on purpose in a scratch build: a "Copy link" left enabled and a removed figure each made it fail; an eager `new Worker` on hydration made `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` fail.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A2: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: none needed beyond the plan's own choices recorded above.

# P1-A3 decisions

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a43e0fe9de85f8632`, branch `worktree-agent-a43e0fe9de85f8632`, fast-forwarded to the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` at `4dbf4f8` (G0 green) before any work; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. No dependency added to the site or the engine.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: DrawLayout and its fields
Decision: `nodePositions(board, xy?)` returns `DrawLayout`, P0-A5's `GridLayout` (`xy` by position, `columns`, `rows`, `kind`) plus `circle: boolean`. `columns` and `rows` are the span of x and of y plus one for every layout, circles included (on a circle they are not used by the size rule; `toPixels` uses the polygon radius). The `Pick` is `n`, `labels`, `name`, `cartan`; `kind` is dropped because `nodePositions` does not read it. What the specification left open: the representation (plan). Reason: the instruction, 5.1; `Board.svelte` keeps working on the same shape.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: polygon and circle representation
Decision: corners on the unit circle, corner k of m at (sin(2 pi k / m), cos(2 pi k / m)) in label order from the smallest label, which is clockwise from the top with y up; values below 1e-12 in magnitude are written as 0. A~n (n >= 2), `pentagon` (5 nodes) and `square` (4 nodes) are `kind: 'polygon'`; the circle fallback of custom boards starts with the smallest label at the top and runs clockwise like the polygons, `kind: 'custom'`, `circle: true`. Reason: the plan (5.2); A-1 needs the label order on the circle.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: A~1
Decision: node 0 at (0, 0), node 1 at (1, 0), `kind: 'row'`. What the specification left open: 6.1 says only "side by side". Reason: the plan (5.1, 5.2).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: B2~ and C2~ by matrix
Decision: for the names `B2~` and `C2~` (and for `D3^(2)`, which reverses `C2~`) the rule follows the matrix: node 0 joined to label 2 only takes the B~ rule (node 0 at (2, 1)), joined to label 1 only takes the C~ rule (node 0 at (0, 0)); otherwise the name decides. Reason: golden `affine.B2` is named `C2~` in the B2 numbering (instruction 5.2).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: twisted names
Decision: twisted names are parsed, never listed: `A{2n}^(2)` is the row (i, 0) for i = 0..n; `A{2n-1}^(2)` (n >= 3) takes the B~n layout; `D{m}^(2)` (m >= 3) the C~(m-1) layout; `E6^(2)` F~4; `D4^(3)` G~2; position k takes the coordinates of label k of the untwisted layout. Checked against the 10 records of golden `twisted_affine` (`reverse_of`) and all 23 twisted names of `affine_names`. Reason: SPEC 2.12 and 6.1.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: what counts as a custom board
Decision: a board whose `name` is undefined, unrecognised (`H3` before phase 7, `E9`), or whose labels do not match the node set its name implies (for example `A3` with labels 2, 3, 4) is a custom board; `nodePositions` never throws. A board from `transposeBoard` is laid out by whatever `name` it carries, as every other board; with `name` undefined it is custom. Custom `xy` must have one whole-number point per node, all different, each coordinate 0 to 11; anything else (missing, wrong length, non-integer, repeated, outside 0..11) gives the circle. The reader's y grows upward, like the table (plan; the editor is P2-A3's, and the orientation is named in my report's requests). Reason: SPEC 6.1, 6.6; instruction 5.2, 5.3.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: size rule in pixels
Decision: `toPixels(layout, W)`: row and grid layouts use u = `gridUnit(W, columns)`, 8 pixels of margin on each side (the 16 of the formula), half a unit around the outermost centres, box `columns * u + 16` by `rows * u + 16`, hit side `min(44, u)`; circle layouts use R = `polygonRadius(W)`, hit side 44, box `2R + 44 + 16` square. y is flipped here only. `boardBoxWidth(viewport, container)` returns the viewport below 400. Reason: SPEC 6.1, A-7; plan margin (5.4).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: road glyphs and elements
Decision: `roadGlyphs` lists joined pairs i < j in increasing order; `dynkin` gives `lines` (count max(m, m'), chevron towards the node receiving the extra roads) when m m' <= 3 and `thick` when m m' >= 4; `roads` gives one `arrow` per road, i to j first. The thick line writes beside each end the number of roads leaving that node (`countAtFrom` = A_ij beside node i): A~1 gives 2 and 2, `A2^(2)` gives 1 beside node 0 and 4 beside node 1. `roadElements(glyphs, centres, unit = 1)` returns `{ tag, attrs, text? }` descriptors (tags `line`, `path`, `polygon`, `text`) with `stroke` / `fill` equal to `var(--road)`, `var(--arrow)` or `var(--text)`, coordinates rounded to 4 decimals so server and client print the same text; each road of the `roads` style is a quadratic curve bent to the left of its direction by (index + 1) steps, so roads of one direction never coincide and the two directions bend to opposite sides. `Roads.svelte` renders the descriptors inside `<g aria-hidden="true">` and repeats the three colours in its CSS by class, so they hold even where a browser ignored `var()` in a presentation attribute (checked in Chromium: computed stroke `rgb(26, 26, 26)` light, roads `#E8E8E8` dark). The third argument `unit` is an addition to the plan's `roadElements(glyphs, centres)`: offsets of parallel lines, chevrons, counts and arrowheads are fractions of one grid unit. Reason: SPEC 6.2, 2.1; instruction 5.5.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: the engine import in draw/
Decision: `draw/roads.ts` and `draw/layouts.ts` import from `@two-games/engine` (the package the `@engine` alias names) rather than from `@engine`, because the `site-unit` project of the root Vitest configuration does not resolve the site's tsconfig aliases (a probe import of `@engine` failed there; the package name resolves in Vitest, Astro and svelte-check alike). Request to MERGE / P0-A1's successor: add the aliases of CONTRACTS 1 to the `site-unit` project, after which these imports may switch back. Reason: testability without editing a file I do not own.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: palette, tokens and base.css
Decision: `palette.ts` holds `LIGHT` and `DARK` (the sign table of 6.3 with page and text, plus P0-A1's `--focus-ring`, `--road`, `--arrow`), `EDGES_LIGHT`, `EDGES_DARK`, `ACCENTS` (same in both themes), `tokenValues(theme)`, `contrastRatio` exactly as verify.py (`0.03928`), `edgeColour(label)` returning `var(--edge-c)`, and the two icons as `{ viewBox: '0 0 16 16', d, stroke: var(--accent-...), fill: 'none' }` (a city of three buildings; a disc with the numeral 1). `tokens.css` carries every property of CONTRACTS 8 in the light `:root` block, the dark `@media` block for `:root:not([data-theme="light"])` and the manual `:root[data-theme="dark"]` block. `base.css` now starts with `@import './tokens.css';` but keeps its own copy of the token block, because P0-A1's `scaffold.test.ts > base.css tokens` reads the hex values from `base.css` and may not be edited; `contrast.test.ts` fails if any value repeated in `base.css` differs from `tokens.css`. Request to MERGE: point that scaffold test at `tokens.css`, then delete the duplicate block from `base.css`. Font stacks and the reduced-motion rule stay in `base.css`. Reason: instruction 5.6 and trap "base.css restructuring".

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: the theme switch
Decision: `ThemeSwitch.svelte` is one `<button type="button">` with the visible text "Dark theme" and `aria-pressed` true in the dark theme, at least 44 by 44 pixels, focus ring in `--focus-ring`, `disabled` in the server HTML and enabled in an `$effect` on hydration. It stores the choice in `localStorage` under the key `theme`; on hydration it reads the stored value and re-applies it; with nothing stored, CSS alone follows `prefers-color-scheme`. Side effects go through `readStoredTheme(storage)` and `applyTheme(theme, root, storage)` in `palette.ts`, each storage call inside `try`; `window.localStorage` itself is read inside `try`. Checked once in Chromium on a scratch page (not committed): enabled after hydration, box 115 by 44, Enter sets `data-theme="dark"` and the dark page colour, the choice survives a reload, a second press stores `light`. Not done: the switch is not placed on any page (see the next entry). Known limit: a stored choice opposite to the system's is applied only when the island hydrates (`client:idle`), so such a reader sees the system theme for a moment; an inline script in the head would avoid that but the shell ships no script (P0-A5). Reason: SPEC 8.3, CONTRACTS 8, instruction 5.7.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: the switch is not added to Base.astro (deviation from the launch brief)
Decision: I did not add the ThemeSwitch include line to `Base.astro`. Reason: two constraints of the brief conflict. Placed in the shell as a Svelte island, the switch adds a second `<astro-island>` to the index page, and P0-A5's `nojs-phase0.spec.ts > the island is hydrated with client:idle` asserts exactly one island there; the brief requires that spec to keep passing. My instruction (7, "must not touch") also assigns the placement to P1-A4 in `Nav.astro`. Measured on a scratch page: the island costs 595 bytes gzipped of its own, but on a page with no other island it pulls in the Svelte client runtime, 18.5 kB gzipped in all (client 17.3 kB, renderer 0.5 kB, switch 0.6 kB, version 0.1 kB), above the 15 kB limit of SPEC 7.6 for a page with no tool (`about`, `credits`). Proposal for P1-A4 and MERGE: in `Nav.astro`, render the same button as plain HTML (disabled) and wire it with a bundled `<script>` that imports `readStoredTheme`, `applyTheme`, `resolveTheme`, `nextTheme` from `draw/palette.ts` (well under 1 kB, no framework runtime), or keep `ThemeSwitch.svelte` only where an island already loads the runtime; and relax the island count of `nojs-phase0.spec.ts` to count `data-tool` islands.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A3: layered layout
Decision: `layeredLayout({ keys, rank, edges })` keeps `y = rank`; the lowest rank is sorted by key; each higher rank by the barycenter of the vertex's neighbours in lower ranks (the centred x already placed), compared exactly as integer fractions; ties, and vertices with no lower neighbour (placed after the others), by plain code-unit string comparison, never `localeCompare`. Edges are deduplicated as unordered pairs, may span several ranks, and edges inside one rank are ignored for ordering. `x` = index in the rank + `floor((widestRank - size) / 2)`. `rankCount` is the highest rank plus one; an unoccupied rank has an empty `order` entry. RangeError on a duplicate key, a rank that is not a non-negative safe integer, a length mismatch or an edge to a missing vertex. Timing on this machine (Node 22.22, 2000 vertices on 13 ranks, 3821 edges): first run 8.3 ms, median of nine 5.6 ms. Reason: SPEC 7.1, A-11, instruction 5.8.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A4: frontmatter schemas (content.config.ts)
Decision: two collections with the glob loader, ids are file names without extension. Both schemas are `z.strictObject` (an unknown field fails the build).
- `modules` (src/content/modules/*.mdx): `title` string; `module` integer 0 to 10; `question` string; `phase` integer 1 to 6; `released` boolean; `draft` boolean, default `true` (D17); `outcomes` array of `LO1` to `LO10`; `sources` array of strings, each checked with `hasKey` (an unknown key fails the build with `unknown source key "<k>"`).
- `pages` (src/content/pages/*.mdx): `title` string; `phase` integer 0 to 7; `released` boolean; optional `description` string; optional `generated: 'dependencies'`. Later page agents (P2-A7, P5-A3, P6-A6) write to this schema.
What the specification left open: the schemas (plan, CONTRACTS 7). Reason: the plan.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A4: routes and collisions
Decision: `[page].astro` gives `/<slug>/`, `modules/[slug].astro` gives `/modules/<slug>/`; both build every entry, released or not. Reserved slugs that no page may take: sources, open-items, modules, tools, atlas, embed, index, dev; `[page].astro` throws on a collision, and `released.test.ts` checks that every static name under src/pages is reserved. Sitemap: none is installed and SPEC names none, so the rule "excluded from the sitemap" is met vacuously.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A4: how `released` is decided (lib/pages/released.ts)
Decision: one module, pure functions over plain lists plus build-time wrappers. A module or page is released when its file exists and its frontmatter says `released: true` (read from the raw MDX with `import.meta.glob(..., { query: '?raw', eager: true })` and `parseFrontmatter` of `@astrojs/markdown-remark`, so it is synchronous and usable in MDX). A tool is released when `site/src/tools/registry/T{nn}.ts` exists and its raw text does not say `released: false`. An Atlas page is released when `src/pages/atlas/[board].astro` exists. `sources` always; `open-items` only when `isReleaseBuild()` is false. `hrefFor(target)` and `linkIfReleased(target)` join `import.meta.env.BASE_URL` and the route with exactly one slash; targets: `{kind:'module', slug}` or `{kind:'module', number}`, `{kind:'page', slug}`, `{kind:'tool', id, query?}`, `{kind:'atlas', board}`, `{kind:'static', name: 'index'|'modules'|'sources'|'open-items'}`. `nextModule(n)` is module n + 1 only, never a later one.
MDX pattern for released-aware prose links (P1-A8 asked for it):
```
import { linkIfReleased } from '@lib/pages/released';
export const m = (n) => linkIfReleased({ kind: 'module', number: n });
{m(1) ? <a href={m(1)}>the mutation game</a> : <>the mutation game</>}
```
about.mdx uses exactly this for the seven statements, mapped (plan, [D]) 1 to M7, 2 to M1, 3 to M4, 4 to M5, 5 to M3, 6 to M8, 7 to M9.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A4: the theme switch (orchestrator decision)
Decision: a Svelte island ships about 17.3 kB of runtime on a page with no other island (P1-A3's measurement), over the 15 kB no-tool row of SPEC 7.6, so Nav.astro carries the switch as a plain `<button data-theme-switch>` "Dark theme", rendered `disabled` with `aria-pressed="false"`, at least 44 by 44 pixels, wired by a small bundled `<script>` in Nav.astro: it reads localStorage key `theme` (`light` | `dark`, every access in `try`), applies `data-theme` on `<html>` (removed for the system default), toggles on click, stores the choice, updates `aria-pressed` and enables the button. Astro inlines it: 732 bytes, 371 bytes gzip, no script file fetched. P1-A3's `ThemeSwitch.svelte` and `palette.ts` are not used. Consequence: P0-A5's `a11y.spec.ts` assertion `page.locator('script')` count 0 on about and credits no longer holds (the network assertion still does); see the request in my report.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A4: the sources page
Decision: Markdown renderer route (1): `createMarkdownProcessor({ syntaxHighlight: false, smartypants: false })` from `@astrojs/markdown-remark` 7.3.2 (MIT), a direct dependency; no plugin, no smart quotes, so the text prints as written. Pipeline (lib/pages/sourcesMarkdown.ts): slice from `### 11.1 ` to before `### 11.7 `; inline `$...$` to placeholders `MATHPLACEHOLDER<n>END`; labels spelled out; `**[KEY]**` to `<strong class="source-key" id="KEY">[KEY]</strong>`; plain `[KEY]` mentions of defined keys linked to `#KEY`; `###` to `##`; render; placeholders replaced by `renderTex(tex, false)`. Status words: [S] "read in the source", [S2] "reported by a later source", [V] "checked by computation", [U] "unverified", [D] "design decision". Label rule extended from the plan: the token may also be followed by a space (`[U unless marked]`, `[V for the corrections]`), and a further token after a comma or semicolon in the same bracket is spelled out (`[S for the lemma, S2 for Bourbaki]`); brackets become round brackets; nested Markdown links kept. A key defined outside 11.1 to 11.6 fails the build naming key and section (none today). Below 480 pixels the page sets formulas at text size, because two inline formulas of the file are wider than 288 pixels at KaTeX's default size.

Each item: `id="<id>"`, `data-item`, `data-status`, fields `data-field="status|statement|where|gate|pages"`; a closed item shows "closed on <closedOn>, settled by <closedBy>". ## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-ae4b903777d23b376`, branch `worktree-agent-ae4b903777d23b376`, fast-forwarded to the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` at `279150f` before any work; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. Another agent's `astro preview` held port 4321 during this session, so browser runs used a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 4391 (`reports/playwright.p1a5.config.ts`, ignored by git, identical except for the port and `reuseExistingServer: false`), and `npm run size` ran with `--port 4392`. No dependency added.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: the default state of /tools/board
Decision: `board: 'A2'`, `view: 'linked'`, `mode: 'free'`, `coords: 'populations'`, `start: [1, 0]`, no moves. The caption (registry `presets.default.caption`) states that one Martian in city 1 gives the numbers 2 and −1, computed with `toNumbers` in `T01.ts`, never typed, and that each number is the amount by which a mutation would lower its city. What the specification left open: the default of the tool's own route (4.0 "Starts meaningful"). Reason: the instruction (5.1); it shows both views, the bridge sentence and a negative and a positive number at once, and needs no worker. The component's own defaults when a lesson omits a property are the same view, mode and coords; `start` then comes from `defaultStart` (6.6).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: no presets, but a default entry
Decision: "T1 and T14 have no presets" (CONTRACTS 6). `ToolRegistryEntry` requires `presets.default` with a caption, so `T01.ts` holds the default state of the previous entry under `default` and nothing else. The `preset` property is accepted and ignored. Reason: P1-A2's registry rules.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: the two boards of the linked view
Decision: the populations board above, the line a = Cp and the bridge sentence between, the numbers board below, stacked at every width. Each board is its own group (one tab stop each), named "Populations board on <name>" and "Numbers board on <name>". Acting on node i of either board mutates city i and fires node i; both records are recomputed by the engine (mutate then toNumbers from a population start; fire then toPopulation from a numbers start). Reason: SPEC 4 T1 says only "one above the other" (plan order).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: the a = Cp line and the bridge sentence
Decision: the line is MathML, `<math aria-label="a equals C p"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>p</mi></math>` in `data-out="aCp"`; the aria-label is the fallback for SPEC 7.3's [U] on MathML in every engine (not settled here: phase 3 checks it). The bridge sentence is SPEC 2.4(a) verbatim, "The number on node <i>i</i> is the amount by which a mutation would lower city <i>i</i>.", in `data-out="bridgeSentence"`, rendered only in the linked view (the only place both views are shown), also without JavaScript. The roster paraphrase ("The number on a node ...") was not used: SPEC wins. D7 (a = Cp, the drop) and D8 (no reader-facing switch to the fire-negative convention) relied on.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: view fallbacks and the singular board
Decision: with `coords: numbers`, the start's population is `toPopulation`. When it is null (det C = 0; in phase 1 only a custom singular matrix such as the triangle, since affine names cannot reach T1) only the numbers view is drawn whatever view was asked, and a mode that the numbers view does not allow (raising) falls back to free play (plan). When the populations view is asked and some entry has a denominator other than 1, the linked view is drawn. The state and the link keep the view that was asked; only the drawing falls back, with no notice. The level-zero population of an affine board (its population up to multiples of the frozen population) is phase 2 work and is not computed. Reason: SPEC 4 T1 "Coordinates of the start"; traps 6 and 7.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: affine boards absent from the picker; what boards: 'all' means
Decision: in phase 1 the picker offers the 32 named types, grouped by letter A to G (one `<optgroup>` per letter, ranks increasing within a letter), and no affine or twisted name; any custom Cartan matrix of at most 12 nodes reaches T1 through a link (`g=c:...`). The name shown is the one chosen (D19: C2 shows `C2`; the group and the figure label carry it). A picker change keeps view, mode and coords, sets `start` to `defaultStart` for the new board and empties `moves`. Reason: 4.0 Board pickers; roster; P1-A2's codec offers 'all'.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: the SafeInt-to-Big switch
Decision: `t01/model.ts` uses P1-A2's `bigBoard` (state.ts): a start entry that is not a safe integer builds the game on `bigBoard(board)` at once; an `EngineOverflow` during any engine call switches to `bigBoard`, recomputes every position from the start, and repeats the call. No message, no notice; the board keeps its name and labels. No request for another helper is needed. Reason: SPEC 4 T1 last sentence of "Coordinates of the start"; trap 8.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: long numbers and data-value
Decision: more than 12 digits, counted without the sign, is long. A node draws "1.2345... x 10^83 (84 digits)" (a true minus sign before a negative one; a fraction is shortened part by part); the button's tooltip (`title`), the table view, `data-value` and Download data keep the exact digits. Reason: 4.0 Long numbers; `data-value` is "the value shown, as decimal text", read as the exact text (plan).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: fractions
Decision: populations from a numbers start are `ratToString` texts (`2/3`, `-1/3`, an integer as `p`). On a node they are drawn as an SVG stacked fraction; in the table through `Frac.svelte` (plain markup, `role="img"` with an aria-label). Spoken as "2 over 3", "minus 1 over 3" in node names, announcements and the table. A fraction's sign is its numerator's. Reason: SPEC 6.3, 7.3; A-3 has no template for fractions (plan).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: data-sign and data-ring
Decision: every node button carries `data-sign` (`positive`, `negative`, `zero`, from the value's sign) and `data-ring` (`true` exactly when the second "may act here" ring is drawn, which is when `data-can-act` is true). Reason: A-5 test hooks (plan).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: the figure's text alternative
Decision: ToolFrame's `figureLabel` reads "Game Board on <name>, <view> view, <mode text>: populations 1, 0; numbers 2, minus 1." (values spoken, in label order; a single view names only its record). Each board group has its own name (above). Reason: A-10 (plan wording).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: mode text, game over, refusals and the linked sentences
Decision: `data-out="mode"` reads `free play`, `legal play` or `raising moves`. In legal mode `data-out="gameOver"` reads `Game over after N moves.` (`1 move` in the singular, as P0-A5's template) when no number is positive, and is an empty reserved line otherwise. A move that ends a legal game appends `Game over after N moves.` to its announcement; a play to the end is one sentence (`Played N moves to the end. ...`, populations named for a raising play) and does not append it. A refused action in raising mode: `City 2 cannot be raised: its number is 1.` (plan; SPEC has no template). Linked view above four nodes: `Mutated city 2 and fired node 2. City 2 now 1; node 1 now 1, node 2 now 1, node 3 now minus 1. Move 1.` (plan). Undo in the linked view: `Undid move 4. Populations now ...; numbers now ....` (plan). A play stopped by its cap: `Still running after 20000 moves. Numbers now ...` in the live region and `still running after 20000 moves` in `data-out="limit"`; the words "not of finite type" never appear. Reason: A-4, 4.0 Stated limits.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: step, play to the end, the worker
Decision: Step and Play to the end appear in legal and raising modes only. Step makes the move at the lowest allowed position; play to the end calls `play` (legal; and raising from a numbers start, on -a, which makes the same moves because firing is linear) or `raisingPlay` (raising from a population), with no `choose` and the cap `CAPS.playMoves`, appends the word and keeps every position. `whereToRun({ board, kind: 'play', finiteKnown: isNamedFinite(board) })` is asked before each play; custom boards go to the worker through `engineCall`, named finite boards run on the main thread. "play on" and `playFate` are phase 2: on a custom board the control stays "Play to the end" and reports the cap. A link's move list is replayed on the main thread when the tool loads it, as `decodeState` itself does under P1-A2's accepted `LONG_LIST_REPLAY = 'sync'` reading (deviation from E-9 rule 2 for lists above 2000 moves, consistent with MERGE's stage 1.1 decision). Reason: 4.0, 5.6, E-9.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: history, the state, view and mode choosers
Decision: History.svelte is composed in the figure (after the boards, before the controls) and ToolFrame's own Undo/Redo/Reset are not used. `state.moves` holds the moves up to the cursor, so `data-moves` and the link follow undo and scrubbing; the redo tail stays in memory only. On the tool's own route (instance id empty; the `settings` property overrides) the controls row also holds the board picker and choosers for the view and the mode (`data-control="picker"`, `"view"`, `"mode"`); a view change keeps the moves (a mode the new view does not allow becomes free play); a mode change keeps the moves when `firstForbiddenMove` allows them all and otherwise restarts from the start (plan). Reason: 4.0 History; 6.6.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: Download data
Decision: `derived` is `{ view, mode, labels, positions }`, `positions` one entry per position of the current play up to the cursor: `{ move, populations?, numbers? }` in label order, safe integers as numbers, longer ones as decimal text, fractions as `p/q`; the linked view holds both. The state is the ToolState of the current point. Reason: 6.6 Data export (plan).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: properties, shapes and the predicate list
Decision: beyond the common ones: `view`, `mode`, `script: { move: label; caption }[]`, `goal` (`{ kind: 'reach', position, game?, text? }`, `{ kind: 'no-positive', text? }`, `{ kind: 'no-unhappy', text? }`, `{ kind: 'predicate', name: 'mixed-signs', giveUpAfter?, giveUpText?, text? }`), `prediction` (CONTRACTS 5.2 exactly, no field added), `hints: boolean`, `panels: ('form'|'level'|'colours'|'lengths')[]`, `roadStyle`, `rounds: number`, `settings: boolean`, `onChange({ state, populations, numbers })`. `linkTo`, `layers` and `preset` are accepted and ignored. The predicate list is `mixed-signs` (a population with a negative and a positive city; M1 beat 2). A reach goal compares values as text in label order, in the game of `coords` unless `game` says otherwise. `goalMet` shows `Goal reached.` (announced once, appended to the move's sentence); `goalNote` shows the give-up text once `giveUpAfter` moves are made and the goal is not met. Script: Next makes the next scripted move through the same path as a click; `scriptCaption` shows the caption of the next scripted move, `End of the script.` at the end, and `The script has ended: a move was made that it does not contain.` while the moves made differ from the script (undoing back onto it resumes it). A script move the mode forbids, or a view and mode outside the table, throws at build time naming the instance. `rounds`: `Round k` in `data-out="roundCounter"`, k = floor(moves made / rounds), following undo and scrubbing, not part of the state. Reason: SPEC 4 T1 Configuration; 3 M6 row 2 (plan).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: prediction check
Decision: `Predict.svelte` takes the `Prediction` of CONTRACTS 5.2 unchanged and checks `value` (number, position) or `correct` (choice); no `check` field was added, so there is no deviation of CONTRACTS 5.2. The engine check required by the plan is in `T01.test.ts > M0 figure configurations` (A3 from (1,1,0), city 2 gives 0). While a prediction is unanswered every node is `data-can-act="false"` and disabled, History and the step controls are disabled, and there are no hint ghosts. Without JavaScript the question is followed by `<details>` holding the answer and the reveal.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: hints
Decision: with `hints`, hovering or focusing a node draws below it (aria-hidden, `data-ghost`) the value it would take after acting there, from the engine (mutate or fire; both boards in the linked view), only where the mode allows, never during a prediction.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: panels
Decision: `panels` is accepted and filtered by `PANELS_AVAILABLE`, empty in phase 1: form value, level and long and short nodes arrive with their engine functions in phase 2 (formValue, level, symmetrizer), the colour classes in phase 3 (bipartition). No panel and no message in phase 1.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: linkTo and the highlight
Decision: on main P1-A2's `publishToolState` and `followToolState` (state.ts) exist, so no `t01/link.ts` was written. T1 publishes its ToolState under its instance id after every change (never positions) and follows the same id: a published state different from its own, on the same board, coords and start, is applied as a new move list from its own start (T2's jump). Highlight: hovering or focusing a node sets `{ kind: 'node', board: <name or c:matrix>, value: label }` and clears it on leaving; a `position` or `root` value equal to a board's current values (label order, same board name) marks that board (`data-highlighted`, a dashed outline).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: Board family extensions (deviation record: optional props only)
Decision: `Board.svelte` now lays out by `nodePositions(board, xy)` and `toPixels` (grid units = toPixels at u = 64 divided by 64; the CSS rule u = min(64, floor((W - 16)/k)) scales the box; hit side min(44, u)); a polygon or circle layout is a square box of side min(W, 360) with 44-pixel hit areas, the drawing scaled with the box, so the polygon radius is 150/360 of the side (at W = 320 the radius is 133 rather than the formula's 130; plan, recorded). W is the figure's width, which ToolFrame runs from edge to edge below 400 pixels (boardBoxWidth). New optional props: `xy`, `states`, `spoken`, `ghosts`, `disabled`, `highlighted`, `onFocusNode`; new hooks `data-sign`, `data-ring`. `Node.svelte` draws stacked fractions, shortened long numbers and the ghost; `History.svelte` gains `frozen` and the hooks `data-control="undo|redo|reset|historySlider"` and `data-step`. CONTRACTS 5.1 props unchanged; BoardSeed and P0-A5's tests unchanged and green. The Board family and T1 import the engine as `@two-games/engine` and lib by relative path (as lib/ does), because a Vite build without the site's aliases (P1-A2's toolframe-hydration fixture) reaches T1 through the registry's lazy import.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: additive edits granted by MERGE
Decision: `data-instance={instanceId}` on ToolFrame's root (for P1-A4's Challenge); `released?: boolean` on `ToolRegistryEntry`; in `pages/tools/[tool].astro` the static import of T01GameBoard, its `client:idle` line and `'board'` in `HYDRATED`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A5: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D7 (a = Cp), D8 (no convention switch), D19 (the picker shows the name chosen). D17 does not apply (no lesson). No [U] item settled.

# P1-A6 decisions (T2, the Orbit Garden)

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a39695ff4de568dc8`, branch `worktree-agent-a39695ff4de568dc8`, fast-forwarded to the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` at `279150f` before any work; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. No dependency added. The cap-9 check of the instruction (triangle, three single Martians, cap 9: 9 items, truncated, completeLayers 1) holds and is a unit test.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: properties and presets
Decision: properties beyond the common ones: `grown` (SPEC), `grows: 'roots' | 'orbit'`, `growth: 'follow' | 'explore'`, `positiveOnly`, `vertexLabels`, `boardChoices`. Presets (state part / options): `default` and `explore`: `A3`, populations, start (1, 0, 0), roots, explore, grown 0, labels on; `positive-roots`: as default with `grown` 40 and `positiveOnly`; `follow`: `A2` from (1, 0), growth follow, roots (orbit when the coords are numbers, the `grows` property overrides); `triangle-40`: custom `{ cartan: [[2,-1,-1],[-1,2,-1],[-1,-1,2]] }`, grown 40, layers 40. Options live in `components/tools/t02/presets.ts` (the registry's `PresetFields` type cannot hold them); the registry entry carries the state part and the caption. Captions take their numbers from the engine at module load (12, 6, 6, 243, 6), never typed. What the specification left open: names and option values (4.0 Configuration). Reason: the instruction's plan table.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: M6 row 1 configuration
Decision: M6 row 1 uses `grows: 'roots'`, `growth: 'explore'`, `grown: 2`, `board: 'B2'`, with `linkTo` naming the T1; `found` reads 8 at build time (unit test). `follow` cannot show 8 there: from (1, 0) mutation reaches only the 4 positions of its orbit (unit test feeds every reachable state). Reason: instruction 5.1 and trap 6.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: hooks
Decision: `data-out`: found, total, stopMessage, verdict (phase 2), layersGrown, layerChart (bars with data-layer, data-count, data-complete), vertex (data-position, data-layer), edge (data-label, data-from, data-to, data-rise), rise, tableRow (data-position, data-layer), chartRow (data-layer, data-count; addition, so that the chart's numbers in the table view are testable), strip. `data-control`: grow, showTotal, growth, grows, positiveOnly, vertexLabels, picker, scrub (strip buttons with data-step, or the slider), jumpRow (addition: the jump button of a table row); undo, redo, reset, copyLink, downloadData from ToolFrame. data-position and data-value use the ASCII hyphen; the drawn text uses U+2212.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: undo, redo, reset and the strip (deviation from "History.svelte does this")
Decision: the Undo, Redo and Reset buttons are ToolFrame's (hooked `data-control`), and the strip of past positions is drawn by T2 itself (buttons `data-control="scrub"` with `data-step`, a slider with a counter above 50 positions, as 4.0 says). `History.svelte` is not used because it renders its own unhooked Undo, Redo and Reset, which would duplicate ToolFrame's row. Request to P0-A5/P1-A5: a strip-only mode of History.svelte, after which T2 can use it. The `u` and `r` shortcuts are wired through `shortcutOf` while focus is inside the board.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: the seed of a numbers link
Decision: a link (or property) with `coords: numbers` names a numbers position, which only `orbit` mode can grow: T2 then grows that orbit in the firing game and the `grows` control is disabled. Reason: instruction 5.2.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: reset
Decision: reset empties the move list, returns `explore` to the configured `grown` layers (clamped to the layers bound) and `follow` to the current position alone (the start, since the moves are emptied). Reason: plan, instruction 5.2.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: follow mechanics and ranking
Decision: the garden starts with the position the board stands on when follow begins; a move adds the position it reaches when new, and the edge it travelled (labelled by the node) when that edge is new; undo, redo, scrubs and revisits add no vertex. (Plan said a revisit adds nothing; a revisit by a new road adds that road's edge, since every move is an edge; no vertex either way.) Each vertex is ranked by its depth in the bounded `orbitGraph` of the followed start, computed once; a position outside it goes one rank below the position it came from. Jumps in follow take their word from that bounded graph. With `linkTo`, a change of the followed state counts as a move when `moveMade` says the new list is a prefix of the old one plus one move; anything else adds the position only. `grown` is ignored. Positive roots only is available in explore only (the switch is disabled in follow and in orbit mode).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: stop messages
Decision: `stopMessage(graph, maxDepth, layersBound, cap)` in `t02/limits.ts`: the two sentences of 5.5 exactly; none for a completed search; none for a pause in explore below the bound, where `layersGrown` shows "N layers grown". In follow at 2000 visited positions: "stopped after 2000 positions" without the layers clause. The message is announced when a grow press brings it.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: edges
Decision: every move is applied once more to every item; each involutive pair is drawn once; a move that changes nothing (a self-loop) is not drawn; two nodes joining the same two positions give two edges, the second offset by 7 pixels. Colour from `edgeColour(label)` (tokens), the label as SVG text beside the line. In the positive view on a named finite-type board a rise of 2 or 3 is drawn as 2 or 3 parallel lines; on every other board the rise is written as `+k` at 58 percent of the edge's length on the opposite side from the label (at 42 percent), on every edge, 1 included.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: finite test
Decision: `isNamedFinite` in `t02/garden.ts` wraps state.ts's `isNamedFinite` (named finite types; every custom board counts as not finite, 4.0, E-9 rule 2). P2-A7 switches this one function to `classify`'s verdict. The layers bound is the `layers` property, else 40 off the finite types and none on a named finite board.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: counter without a total
Decision: when there is no total the counter shows `no total on this board`. An orbit search that stopped at the layers bound below 2000 positions gives no total, never "more than 2000" (a reading of SPEC 4 T2 Interactions: the SPEC names only the complete case and the 2000 case). The positive view does not change the counter. `totalOf` is the one function phase 3 changes for `orbitSize`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: highlight
Decision: on hover or focus of a vertex the highlight atom gets `{ kind: 'root' | 'position', board: name or the codec's c: text, value: entries in label order }`; cleared on leave or blur; skipped (null) when an entry is not a safe integer. T2 does not mark vertices highlighted by other tools (optional).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: vertex keyboard model, elements, names
Decision: vertices are real `<button>` elements over the SVG of edges, in a group named "Garden of N positions", one in the tab order (roving tabindex). Left and Right move within a layer in drawn order, Up and Down to the nearest vertex (by x) of the nearest occupied layer, Home and End to the first and last in items order, Enter or Space jumps, the focused vertex is scrolled into view. Names: `Root population 1, minus 1, 0, layer 2` (orbit mode: `Position ...`; positive view: `total` instead of `layer`). Labelled vertices are at least 44 by 44 pixels, dots 24 by 24, spaced so none overlap; the box scrolls (A-7, A-11).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: announcements
Decision: moves on T2's board use `moveAnnouncement` and `announceTemplates.undid` unchanged. Others (plan): `Layer 3 grown: 3 new, 12 found so far.` (followed by the stop message as a sentence when one appears), `Total: 12.`, `Jumped to root population 1, 1, 0 by 2 moves.`, `Reset. The garden holds 3 positions.`, `Board D4. ...`, `Follow mode: ...`, `Explore mode: ...`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: linkTo
Decision: no new mechanism: T2 uses P1-A2's `followToolState(linkTo, onChange)` from state.ts. It mirrors the followed state (board, coords, start, moves) as its own and shows no board of its own. A jump publishes `{ ...followedState, moves: word }` under the T1's id with `publishToolState`, the word being a shortest one from the T1's own start (`wordFromStart`). In orbit mode the seed is the followed start (the orbit of the current position is the same set). Requests: P1-A5 to publish T1's state on every change and to apply a state published under its own id. Protocol for P2-A2 and later tools: follow with `followToolState`, drive with `publishToolState` under the leader's id.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: long numbers
Decision: `t02/numbers.ts` `shortNumber`: `1.2345... x 10^16 (17 digits)`, a true minus sign in front when negative; values up to 12 digits unchanged. Exact digits in the table and the tooltip (`title`) and in the export. Request to MERGE: keep one copy with T1's, if P1-A5 wrote one.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: picker
Decision: the picker is a `<select>` with one `<optgroup>` per letter and plain-text names (an `<option>` cannot hold KaTeX HTML, and shipping names.json's HTML would cost bundle size); `boardChoices` limits it. A change resets start and moves to the defaults for the new board and regrows to `grown`. A custom board shows a disabled "Custom board" entry. (Deviation from "typeset from names.json".)

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: server-render test
Decision: `T02.test.ts` renders the component with `svelte/server` through its own Vite server (the Svelte plugin and the aliases of CONTRACTS 1, which `_svelteSsr.ts`'s `tsconfigPaths` does not apply to `.svelte` importers such as Board.svelte). The triangle-40 preset renders 243 vertices, found 243 and `stopped after 40 layers`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: prediction
Decision: the `prediction` property is accepted and not wired; Predict.svelte (P1-A5) is not on the integration branch. M1 places no prediction on T2. Request to MERGE.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A6: defaults of SPEC 10 relied on
Decision: D7 (the number on a node is the drop, a = Cp; the rise is -a_i) and D19 (the name the reader chose is shown in the picker). No [U] item touched.

# P1-A7 decisions (T9, Kostant's Game)

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a1e73d9add301f41e`, branch `worktree-agent-a1e73d9add301f41e`, fast-forwarded to the integration branch `claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` at `728c602` (T1 and T2 merged) before any work; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. Browser runs used a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 4401 (`reports/playwright.p1a7.config.ts`, ignored by git: port, `reuseExistingServer: false`, paths relative to `reports/`, `cwd: '..'`), and `npm run size` ran with `--port 4402`. No dependency added. The check of the instruction holds: `kostantPlay(boardFromName('A3'), 0)` is `ended`, 2 moves, word `[1, 2]`, position `[1, 1, 1]` (unit test).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: built on T1's parts and T1's model, not on T01GameBoard.svelte
Decision: T9 composes ToolFrame (P1-A2), Board, History and Predict (P0-A5, P1-A5) and drives them with T1's own model, `T1Game` of `t01/model.ts`, configured with view `populations`, mode `raising`, coords `populations`; it also reuses T1's announcement helpers (`t01/announce.ts`) and long-number helper (`t01/longNumber.ts`, through Board and the tooltip). It does not render `T01GameBoard.svelte`. What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T9 says "T1 in the populations view and raising mode"; the orchestrator's handoff suggested composing T01GameBoard with props. Reason: T01GameBoard renders its own ToolFrame with `data-tool="board"`, title "Game Board", `toolId: 'board'` in Download data, and reads the URL as tool `board` (all boards offered, every view and mode); T9 needs `data-tool="kostant"`, the codec's refusals for tool `kostant`, its own refusals (another view or mode, a fractional numbers start), its moods and end panel inside the figure, and its own export. No second ToolFrame may be nested and my instruction forbids editing T1's files. Request to MERGE (and P1-A5): to make T9 a thin wrapper, T01GameBoard would need the props `toolId`, `title`, a decode tool id with a hook for a tool's own refusals, a snippet inside the figure after the board (and in the table), a decoration hook on Board for per-city glyphs, and an export hook; until then T9 stays on the shared parts, which keeps one game model.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: the pure functions live in t09/
Decision: `site/src/components/tools/t09/kostant.ts` (moods, MOOD_STATE, MOOD_SHAPE, moodOfText, endPanel, kostantSummary, oneMartian, stepPosition, recipeMatrix, isNamedFinite, playControl, startFromLink, playOutcome, pickerGroups, sentences) and `t09/boards.ts` (the 32 names, no import). Reason: the site-unit Vitest project compiles no `.svelte` file (instruction 5.11 fallback). `boards.ts` is separate because the registry entry is loaded on every tool page through the codec and must not pull in the end panel.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: the default preset
Decision: `{ board: 'A3', coords: 'populations', start: [1, 0, 0], moves: [] }` (M1 row 3's first board); caption "Start with one Martian in city 1 of A3 and mutate only where a city rises: the game stops after 2 moves at (1, 1, 1), the highest root, whatever the order of the moves.", the numbers from `kostantPlay` at module load (test against `kostant.A3`). The words "the highest root" are written, not computed, in the registry (F13 on a simply laced board; a unit test compares them with `kostantSummary`), to keep the registry light.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: moods, shapes and the marking
Decision: words `unhappy`, `happy`, `manic` (SPEC 2.16; "excited" not used). Shapes: unhappy a chevron pointing down, happy a level bar, manic a chevron pointing up, drawn in `--text` on the board beside each city (lower right; `data-glyph`) and in a list under the board (`City k`, the glyph, the word in `data-out="mood"` with `data-label` and `data-shape`), plus a legend sentence. Accessible names use `nodeName` through Board's `states` with the A-3 strings. Marked cities: a dashed ring around the city and a small "+" at its upper left (`data-mark`), drawn in `--text`. The board glyphs are an aria-hidden SVG overlay laid over Board.svelte with the same box rule (6.1), because Board has no decoration hook (CONTRACTS 5.1 `decoration` is not read by Board). Colour is never the only carrier: shape and word carry the meaning.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: links with a start other than one Martian; numbers starts
Decision: a link may carry any integer population; it is played as given ("raisingPlay starts from any population"). The end panel then names the population only when it equals `highestRoot` or `highestShortRoot`, and gives no recipe caption or return counts when it is unnamed (the recipe of 2.12 is about Kostant ends); the marked cities are still shown. A link with `c=n` is converted through `startFromLink` (`toPopulation` in Big arithmetic) when every entry is integral and written back as `c=p`; a fractional population loads the default state with the codec's `LINK_NOTICE`. A property `coords: 'numbers'` with a fractional population throws at build time naming the instance (authoring error).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: the refusal of other views and modes (no codec hook needed)
Decision: the codec already refuses a view and mode outside the table of T1 (rule 11: `v=n`, `m=l` with populations), a board not in the registry's list (rule 8: custom, affine), and a move the raising mode forbids (rule 6). T9 refuses in addition, after `decodeState`, a state whose view is not `populations` or whose mode is not `raising` (`v=l`, `m=f`), and a fractional numbers start, with the same `LINK_NOTICE` and the default state; the same check runs on popstate. No change to the codec was needed.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: announcements
Decision: a move: T1's `moveSentence` for the populations view (A-4 template; above four nodes `changedOnly` with `Mutated`/`city`). A refused city: T1's raising sentence `City 1 cannot be raised: its number is 2.`, so the site has one wording. Play to the end: `announceTemplates.playedToEnd(moves, populations, 'populations')` (P1-A2's noun extension; no request needed). When the goal becomes met, the sentence `No city is unhappy. This is the highest root.` (or `the highest short root`; `No city is unhappy.` for an unnamed end) is appended. Board and start changes: `Board D4. One Martian in city 1.`, `One Martian in city 3.`

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: the end panel and the return counts without coroot
Decision: the end panel shows `endName` ("The game stops at the highest root, (1, 1, 1)."), `moveCount`, `hMinus2` only when `highestShortRoot` is null, `finalNumbers` (`toNumbers` of the end), `marked`, `affineCaption` and `returnCounts`. The return counts are `b = C^T beta^vee` with `beta^vee` the end itself on a simply laced board, `highestShortRoot(transposeBoard(board))` for the highest root and `highestRoot(transposeBoard(board))` for the highest short root (2.5, F11). The recipe matrix built from them equals `affine[T].cartan_node0_first` for all 125 long starts (unit test). P2-A7 may switch to `coroot`. The caption: "Join a new city to cities 1 and 3. It sends a_j roads to each marked city j (1 to city 1, 1 to city 3) and receives b_j roads back (1 from city 1, 1 from city 3). On a simply laced board the two counts are equal. The board with the new city is an affine board." No affine name in phase 1; the words "not of finite type" and "never ends" are not used.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: inline MathML
Decision: the `i` of the bridge sentence, `h − 2`, `a_j`, `b_j` and `j` are inline MathML written in the component (`<math><mi>i</mi></math>`, `<math><mi>h</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></math>`, `<math><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></math>`), so A-12 holds without KaTeX at runtime. The bridge sentence's text content is SPEC 2.4(a)'s first sentence character for character. A comment marks that it follows P3-A5's outcome of SPEC 7.3's [U] check (fallback: HTML spans with an aria-label). Note: T1's `Bridge.svelte` writes the `i` as `<i>i</i>`, not MathML; same text content.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: the stated-limit guard
Decision: `playOutcome(result, cap)` gives `still running after <cap> moves` for `status: 'cap'`, shown in `data-out="limit"` with no end panel; unreachable on a named finite type (a raising play takes at most N moves: unit test on random starts from -6 to 6, the longest found above 28), tested with a cap of 5.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: step, play to the end, the main thread
Decision: step is `stepPosition(board, p)` = the word of `raisingPlay(board, p, undefined, 1)`, so the lowest-position rule lives in the engine; play to the end is `T1Game.playToEnd(CAPS.playMoves)` (raisingPlay, lowest position first). Both run on the main thread; T9 imports nothing from `lib/worker` (unit test reads the source).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: picker, start city and "the tool's defaults for that board"
Decision: the picker is a `<select>` (`data-control="picker"`) with one `<optgroup>` per letter A to G from `pickerGroups(boardChoices)`, plain names (an `<option>` cannot hold KaTeX markup; as P1-A5 and P1-A6 did); D19 is met since the reader's choice `B2` or `C2` is shown as chosen. Choosing a board sets one Martian in the city with the lowest label and empties the moves. The start-city chooser (`data-control="startCity"`) lists the labels and shows "other start" when the start is not one Martian. Both are shown on every instance (M1 needs the picker on a lesson page).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A7: long numbers
Decision: `shortNumber` was not written: Board/Node use P1-A5's `t01/longNumber.ts` (`1.0000... x 10^20 (21 digits)`), and T9's tooltip is the node button's `title` (exact digits, as T1). Request to MERGE if a `<title>` on the numeral is wanted.

Decision: Predict.svelte is on the integration branch and is wired: while unanswered the board, step, play to the end, the start city and the picker are disabled. Decision: the tool writes no credit of its own and carries its title "Kostant's Game" (SPEC 8.4, Appendix E item 13 open).

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: predictions (8.5 "Predict a number")
Decision: two predictions. b2 (beat 2): "City 2 holds 1 and its neighbours hold 1 and 0. What will it hold after it mutates?", answer number 0, reveal the arithmetic in words. b4 (beat 4): "City 2 holds 1, and its number is 2. What will city 2 hold after it mutates?", answer number -1, reveal "a mutation lowers a city by its number". The second teaches the bridge sentence before the prose states it (P5); the prose then states every result (P2). The engine check of the answers: P1-A5's `T01.test.ts > M0 figure configurations` covers b2's answer; M0.spec.ts checks both answers against the engine through the board (after answering, the move gives (1, 0, 0) on b2 and (0, -1, 0) on b4). Predict.svelte resolves the prediction on any checked answer, right or wrong, so a wrong answer also unfreezes the board (P1-A5's decision); the test asserts the freeze before any answer and the release after "Check" and after "Show me".

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: challenges (8.5)
Decision: three challenges, each with its own T1 instance (b5 to b7, `client:visible`) and P1-A4's `Challenge` (`client:visible`):
1. "Reach a target": from one Martian in city 2 reach the highest root population, populations view, free play. Check `{ kind: 'reach', board: 'populations', values: theta }`, where `theta = resolveGoldenPath('types.A3.highest_root')` at build time; the goal text and the caption use `formatGoldenValue(theta)`.
2. "Reach a target" in the Numbers view: from the numbers (1, 1, 1), which is rho of 2.6 and not the highest root, reach (-1, -1, -1) by firing in free play. Check `reach` on `numbers`. The least number of moves is not stated (M4, M5). The explanation suggests firing positive numbers until none is and claims nothing about the end.
3. Linked view: from one Martian in city 2 reach a population with no positive number. T1's goal `{ kind: 'no-positive' }` on b7 and the check `{ kind: 'out', name: 'goalMet', equals: 'Goal reached.' }`, since P1-A4's kinds have no "no positive number" kind and T1 already evaluates it. The lesson does not claim how many root populations meet it.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: the seven statements and their links
Decision: beat 5 is a static `<figure>` holding an `<ol aria-label="The seven statements of the site">` with seven items in the site's words (shortened from SPEC 1.4, no claim added) and a figcaption. Each item ends with "Developed in <module title>" for the modules that develop it, rendered by a small MDX component `Developed` that calls P1-A4's `linkIfReleased({ kind: 'module', number })` and takes the title from `siteInventory()`; an unreleased or absent module gives no link and no text, so M2 to M10 gain their links when their phases release them with no edit to this file. Mapping (1.4 gives none): 1 to M6 and M7; 2 to M1 and M4; 3 to M4; 4 to M5; 5 to M2 and M3; 6 to M8; 7 to M9. M10 is the reference hub and has no statement. Every href comes from `linkIfReleased`, so it carries the base path. In this worktree no module other than M0 exists, so the list shows no link; once M1 (`m1-mutation.mdx`, P1-A9) is merged with `released: true`, statement 2 links to it.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: glossary links
Decision: first uses are in bold in the beats; later uses (Lie theory paragraph, beats 3 and 4) link to the glossary anchors of P1-A4 (`board`, `population`, `mutate`, `numbers`, `fire`, `the-translator`) through an MDX component `Term` that builds the link from `linkIfReleased({ kind: 'page', slug: 'glossary' })`.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: the sandbox board and ModuleLinks
Decision: `<ModuleLinks module={0} board="A3" />`. In phase 1 neither the sandbox (T16) nor the Atlas exists, and the next module M1 is not merged in this worktree, so the block is the empty wrapper of P1-A4 (`data-count="0"`). No link is hand-written.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: credit, conventions and sources
Decision: the mutation game and its Mars story are credited to Wildberger ([W20], section two); the Venus story to [W-MS], named only through its key (its Sources entry says "unpublished manuscript, undated"; no year is written anywhere). The numbers game is credited to Mozes [Moz90], with Proctor's earlier use [Pro84] and Eriksson's development; the Eriksson key chosen is [Eri92] ("Convergence of Mozes's game of numbers"), the same key as the credits page. Nothing is said about the content of Mozes' paper (E2). The sign convention is stated as the site's own (fires positive, as Proctor, Donnelly and Wildberger do). Wildberger's opposite sign ($q=-Cp$, the gain) is stated with this site's drop ($a=Cp$, D7) and the remark that linear moves make the two choices give the same games. Frontmatter `sources`: W20, W-MS, Moz90, Pro84, Eri92; the Sources note gives the locators in words.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: "What we have seen" and provenance
Decision: one `Seen` box with three statements, each carrying its provenance in its own sentence (a `<Cite>` or the words "verified by computation on every board of finite type of rank at most eight"), and the box's `provenance` prop `['W20', 'W-MS', 'verified by computation on every board of finite type of rank at most eight']`. The range comes from the per-type check `C.S_i = R_i.C` of verify.py, run on every type of golden_data.json (rank at most eight). The identity $CS_i=R_iC$ in beat 4 carries the same provenance in its sentence.

## 2026-10-10 P1-A8: Unverified items
Decision: no `<Unverified>` is used and no item is proposed. The lesson keeps to SPEC 2.2 to 2.4; it says nothing about Mozes' text (E2), links no video (E5), and quotes no theorem or journal detail marked [U] (E10).
