It is display only (D7: the state stays a = Cp in the site's convention): the nodes show x = -a; `data-value`, the numeral, the sign encoding (fill and outline follow the value shown), the A-3 names and the A-4 announcements use the shown values; the legal nodes are the same set (x negative exactly where a is positive); start, moves, links and Download data stay in the site's convention (the export adds `convention`, `shown` and `olympiadSum`). In the Olympiad form the meter adds the Olympiad's sum (`data-out="sum"`) with the sentence that "the sum is positive" there is "the level is negative" here. The switch is a toggle button "Olympiad's form" with `aria-pressed`, and a line naming the form in force; it is not part of a link (6.6).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: D19 for B2~ and C2~
Decision: the picker and `data-out="boardName"` show the name chosen (`B2~` or `C2~`); the verdict, a recognition from the shape by `classify` (which names both `C2~`), reads "affine, recognised as B2~ = C2~" for both choices. Plan reading: D19 is written for the finite pair B2 = C2; carrying it to the affine pair is this agent's reading.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: the default preset and the per-preset defaults
Decision: the registry has five presets: `default` (deep-equal to `frozen`, not a fifth tab; what the tool shows with no `preset` or with `preset="default"`) and the four ids of CONTRACTS 6. `frozen`: `{ board: 'square', coords: 'populations', start: [1, 1, 1, 1], moves: [] }` (the frozen population of a cycle, all ones, SPEC 3 M3 beat 3; a unit test checks it equals marks(square)); `marks-comarks`: `{ board: 'G2~' }` (no play; the two vectors come from the engine); `pentagon`: `{ board: 'pentagon', coords: 'numbers', start: [1, -2, 3, -4, 1], moves: [] }`, Olympiad form on; `three-fates`: the same state, site's convention. The captions state the result without a golden number (14 and the level are never typed). A picker change resets start and moves to the tool's defaults for that board: the marks in `frozen`, all ones in `three-fates`; the picker is not offered in `pentagon`. Switching tab loads that preset's state.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: the tab a link opens
Decision: the tab and the switch are not part of a link (6.6). A link read by an instance opens `frozen` when `c=p`, `three-fates` when `c=n` (or `pentagon` on an instance placed with `preset="pentagon"` when the board is the pentagon), and `marks-comarks` only when the state has no coords (an instance placed with that preset). So a Copy link from the `pentagon` tab on the tool route opens `three-fates` in the site's convention, and one from `marks-comarks` opens `frozen` on the same board: a limit of schema 1, not a bug. A link that names a board for `frozen` without `s` gets the marks as its start (the codec gives one Martian, which is 6.6's default for populations; T15's default for the board is the marks, 4.0 Board pickers). A link in the numbers tabs whose move list legal play forbids (a move at a node whose number is not positive) loads the defaults with the codec's notice, because hand play there is legal play and the state of 6.6 carries no mode for T15 (rule 6 read for T15's play).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: the Martian control and the root test
Decision: in `frozen`, a city chooser (`data-control="martianCity"`, default the lowest label) and "Add one Martian" (`data-control="martian"`) set `start` to δ + e_x and empty the moves; "Frozen population" puts the marks back. After a Martian the tool shows `isRootPopulation` of the start in `data-out="rootTest"` ("The start is a root population." / "... is not ..." / `too large to test here` on null, 5.6). Moods (`data-out="moods"`) come from the signs of `toNumbers`: "Every city is happy: a mutation anywhere would change nothing." or the counts of unhappy, happy and manic cities. The numbers are not printed beside the populations, so the bridge sentence of 2.4(a) is not required in this tab.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: the start editor
Decision: in `three-fates`, one text field per node (`data-control="startField"`, labelled "Node k"), label order; a complete integer of at most 40 digits with an optional minus sign is applied at once (start set, moves emptied); an empty field or a lone minus waits; anything else, a 41st digit included, is refused by restoring what the field held. Not offered in `pentagon` (the puzzle keeps its example; the three-fates tab starts from the same example on the pentagon so that M4 beat 7 works without configuration).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: data-out names
Decision: `boardName`, `verdict`, `moods`, `rootTest`, `marks`, `comarks`, `coincide`, `level` (data-value), `levelSign`, `sum` (data-value), `convention` (data-convention), `fate`, `firstVisit`, `fateSentence`, `provenance`, `gameOver`, `busy`, and from History, Predict and ToolFrame `moveCounter`, `historyStrip`, `historyCounter`, `predictResult`, `predictReveal`, `caption`, `notice`, `noscript`, `exportNotice`. Listed in the registry's `outs` and at the head of T15.spec.ts.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: hand play, game over and the table
Decision: in the numbers tabs hand play is legal play (a node may be fired when its number is positive in the site's convention); `data-out="gameOver"` shows `Game over after k moves.` whenever no node is legal (also after play on has ended, beside the report). In `frozen` every city may be mutated. The A-10 table has one button per node that acts as the board does, the exact values, and the level row; in `marks-comarks` it is the two-row table of marks and comarks with a button per node that reads both values.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: tests: PRNG, seed, the cap of the sweep, run times
Decision: mulberry32 with seed 20261010 (the seed of P0-A2, P1-A1, P2-A1), offset per test. The positive-level sweep runs at the cap of SPEC 5.6, `CAPS.playMoves` = 20000, on all 44 boards with 10 starts each: 24.6 s on this machine; the whole of T15.test.ts takes about 33 s, under the 120 s of the instruction, so it stays in one file. The negative-level test also checks that a second random order gives the same length and end (2.12); the root-population test checks the 2N bound on the untwisted boards and the two polygons (N of A3 and A4). One run of the full site-unit project failed 2 tests that passed on the two runs after it (not T15's; the machine was shared); recorded for MERGE.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A4: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D7 (a = Cp), D8 (the switch in T15 only), D19 (the name chosen; the B2~ = C2~ form for a recognition). No other default needed. No [U] item settled: E6 stays open and is wrapped.

# Decisions of P2-A5 (T16, the Sandbox)

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-aae6a30bf0b40ffc1`, branch `worktree-agent-aae6a30bf0b40ffc1` (the plan's `agent/P2-A5`), fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` from `5f439e7` to `3b0df02` before any file was written; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once. Browser runs used a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 4431 (`reports/playwright.p2a5.config.ts`, ignored by git, with `cwd: '..'` and `reuseExistingServer: false`), `npm run size` port 4432. No dependency added. `BoardEditor.svelte` was present on the integration branch (P2-A3 merged), so it is imported statically; the `import.meta.glob` device of the plan (section 8 step 3) is not in the code.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: T1 composed from its model and the board family, not T01GameBoard (deviation)
Decision: T16Sandbox.svelte composes ToolFrame, Board, Bridge and History with T1's model `t01/model.ts` (`T1Game`, `effectiveView`, `boardForStart`, `startVector`, `inLabelOrder`), T1's sentences `t01/announce.ts` and T1's long-number helper, exactly as T01GameBoard draws them, in free play only. Moves, undo, redo, reset and scrubbing go to the one `T1Game` the island owns; the island's ToolState is `T1Game.state()` with `tool: 'sandbox'`. What the specification left open: the plan's first choice was a frameless T1. Reason: T01GameBoard renders its own ToolFrame with `data-tool="board"` and reads links as tool `board`, and T1's files are not mine; two frames would double the root, the live region and the noscript line. The plan's fallback; T9 and T15 made the same choice.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the default state and the caption
Decision: `board: { cartan: [[2, -3], [-3, 2]], xy: [[0, 0], [1, 0]] }` (the board of bigint_test), `view: 'populations'`, `mode: 'free'`, `coords: 'populations'`, `start: [1, 0]`, `moves: []`. Caption (registry `presets.default.caption`): "Two cities joined by three roads each way, a board the verdict calls indefinite. Both games are played freely here, with exact integers of any size: on this board the entries pass 2^53 within a few dozen moves, and every digit stays exact." No golden number (39, 84) is typed. What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T16 names no default. Reason: the plan (5.2); the verdict `indefinite` shows at once.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: a link that names a mode other than free (case (b))
Decision: the sandbox offers free play only (SPEC 4 T16). `decodeState` accepts `m=l` with `v=n` or `v=l` and `m=r` with `v=l` (T1's table), and exports its sentence `LINK_NOTICE`, so case (b) of the plan applies: after decoding, a state whose mode is not `free` loads the defaults and shows `LINK_NOTICE` unchanged ("The link could not be read, so the tool shows its default state."). `v=p&m=l` fails the codec's own rule 11 first. A page author who passes `mode` other than `free` gets a build error naming the instance. Extension by analogy with 6.6 ("a board that the tool does not offer").

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the view control, the fallback and the restart controls
Decision: a radio group "View" (Populations, Numbers, Linked; `data-control="view"`). A choice is disabled when T1's rules refuse it for the current board and coords: with a numbers start, populations needs an integral C^{-1}a (else T1 falls back to linked), and on a singular board only numbers is offered. The state then reads the view drawn: a link `v=p&c=n&s=1,0` on the default board loads with `view: 'linked'` (written back as `v=l`), with no notice (SPEC says only "falls back"; T1 draws the fallback silently too). A view switch keeps coords, start and moves. Plan addition: two buttons, "Mutation game from one Martian" (`restartPopulations`: coords populations, the 6.6 default start, moves emptied; the numbers view becomes populations) and "Numbers game from all ones" (`restartNumbers`: coords numbers, all ones; the populations view becomes numbers, then normalised), so both games are playable from their 6.6 defaults without a link. The reader never types a start.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: board changes and the play
Decision: a preset, an added or removed node, a change of roads (any edit whose Cartan matrix differs), an import and a link with another board reset `start` to the 6.6 default for the current coords on the new board and empty `moves` (4.0 Board pickers). Moving a node changes only `xy`, so the board and the play stay. This is stricter than the editor's `resetsPlay` for roads (false), because the matrix changes; the editor contract is unchanged. Edits have no undo of their own in the sandbox: Undo, Redo and Reset act on moves (4.0 History). A preset load calls `exampleLoaded` (pushState, 7.4); every other change goes through ToolFrame's throttled replaceState.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: E-2 for the sandbox's boards
Decision: every custom spec resolves to `Big` (`resolveBoard({ cartan: A3 })` and the default board both give `Big` on the integration branch; no deviation). A named finite board loaded by link (`g=A3`, the module links) is `SafeInt` by E-2; T1Game moves it to `Big` silently when a start entry is not a safe integer or a move overflows (SPEC 4 T1 "reloaded with Big arithmetic, with no message"), tested with a 30-digit start on A2. A link's move list of more than 2000 moves is replayed on the main thread when the island loads it, as T1 does under the accepted `LONG_LIST_REPLAY = 'sync'` reading (MERGE stage 1.1, decisions/P1-A5.md); the default state needs no worker (worker-on-demand.spec.ts passed on /tools/sandbox/).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: JSON import and export of boards
Decision: the format is the `board` value of 6.6 in object form, `{"cartan": [[2, -3], [-3, 2]], "xy": [[0, 0], [1, 0]]}`, `xy` optional. Import also accepts a bare name (`"A3"`), a state object (its `board`) and a whole data export (its `state.board`); starts and moves inside are ignored. Refused, with the board left as it was: text that is not JSON, an unknown name, a `cartan` that is not a square list of whole numbers, an empty matrix, more than 12 nodes, a not-a-board matrix (boardKind, the oracle's board_kind), an `xy` whose length is not the number of nodes or whose points are not pairs of numbers. Coordinates that break 6.1 are kept and spoil only the layout. Wording (plan): "The board could not be read: <reason>. The board was left as it was." and "Board imported: N nodes. The play starts again from the default start." in `data-out="importStatus"` (role status, ordinary text, no announcement of my own). Export: "Export board" downloads `board.json` and the same text is shown in `data-out="boardJson"` (a `<pre>`, so no control is left enabled before hydration). A named board is exported as its matrix with whole grid points (the editor's `gridOf`), so it reads back as a custom board with labels 1..n (E-7). Controls: a labelled textarea, "Import board", a file input, "Export board", all disabled until hydration.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: long numbers and fractions (P1-A5's, recorded)
Decision: none of my own. The shortening is P1-A5's `shortValue` of `site/src/components/tools/t01/longNumber.ts`, drawn by Node.svelte: "4.6083... x 10^83 (84 digits)", a true minus before a negative value, more than 12 digits counted without the sign. The exact digits are in `data-value`, the node's `title` (the sandbox passes T1's tooltip rule as `hintFor`), the A-10 table (each per-node button also carries `data-value`, plan), Download data (T1's `derived()`, decimal strings above the safe range) and the SVG export (P2-A6's `exportSVG` adds a `<title>` with the exact digits to every shortened node and a note of exact digits; the e2e reads both 84-digit strings in the file). A long fraction is shortened part by part on the node (T1's rule); `data-value` keeps the exact `p/q`.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the verdict, the T4 items left out, D19
Decision: the verdict is T4's model, `t04/verdict.ts` `buildVerdict` on one board object per board load (one classify call, cached), shown by T16Verdict.svelte: "Verdict: " then, per component, `finite`, `affine`, `indefinite` or "has a one-way road: the theory does not apply", the name after it, and "nodes 1, 2: " before it when the board has several components (`data-out="verdict"`, names in `data-out="verdictName"` with `data-name`). Finite: `data-out="verdictBourbaki"`, "Your nodes and the Bourbaki labels: node 1 is Bourbaki 1, node 3 is Bourbaki 2, ...", with `data-order` the reader's labels in Bourbaki order. Affine: `data-out="verdictMarks"` and `"verdictComarks"`, "Marks (the frozen population): node 0: 1, ..." with `data-nodes` and `data-values`. T4's "Also shown" items (balance and weights, spectral radius, a smallest non-finite sub-board), the root counter and the hunter are not shown (SPEC T16 "No verdicts beyond those of T4"; the roster asks only for the verdict). D19: a component that classify names B2 reads B2 = C2, as MathML (A-12), when the board was recognised from its shape (any custom matrix); by name (`g=B2`, `g=C2`, a preset) it reads the name chosen. `data-tex` on the verdict element holds the TeX of the names shown (`B_2=C_2`, `A_{3}`, `\tilde{A}_{2}`). Names are plain markup: the letter, `<sub>` for the rank, then `~` or `<sup>(2)</sup>`, so the text reads `A2~` as in 6.6.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: data-out names
Decision: `verdict`, `verdictName`, `verdictBourbaki`, `verdictMarks`, `verdictComarks`, `mode`, `importStatus`, `boardJson`; from T1's Bridge `aCp`, `bridgeSentence`; from History `moveCounter`, `historyStrip`, `historyCounter`; from the editor `editorSubject`, `editorReason`; from ToolFrame `caption`, `notice`, `noscript`, `exportNotice`. Listed in the registry's `outs` (`T16_OUTS`) and at the head of T16.spec.ts; an e2e test checks that every name rendered is listed. The game boards sit in `[data-game]`, so tests tell their node buttons from the editor's (which carry the same hooks).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the released flag
Decision: `released: true` on the registry entry `site/src/tools/registry/T16.ts`, the form P1-A4 settled (`lib/pages/released.ts` reads the raw registry file; a tool is released unless it says `released: false`). The built M0 and M1 pages carry `<a data-link="sandbox" href="/tools/sandbox/?g=A3">`, with no lesson edited. Side effect: P1-A4's `released.test.ts > lesson anatomy fixture` expects `data-count="[01]"` on the fixture's links and now sees 2 (sandbox and next); request to MERGE to widen it.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the BoardEditor props built against
Decision: `editor/types.ts` `BoardEditorProps` as P2-A3 wrote it: `value`, `onChange(next, change)`, `name: "Board editor"`, `disabled: !hydrated`, `presets` (namedPresets() plus the test boards passed by the route as `boardPresets`, as T4 does). `values` is not passed: the game boards show the position, and values on the editor's nodes would duplicate the hooks of the populations board. `roadStyle` is left at its default; a one-way board is drawn in `roads` style by the editor and the board family on their own.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: properties of the island
Decision: `id`, `board`, `coords`, `start`, `moves`, `linkTo` (accepted, unused), `preset` (only `default`), `caption`, T1's `view` and `mode` (only `free`), and the plan property `boardPresets`. `prediction` and `layers` are not accepted. The properties over the sandbox's default state are the `defaults` of `decodeState`; a key a link names overrides them, and a link naming `g` resets start and moves for that board. Listed at the top of T16.test.ts.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: the roster's one-flow e2e split (deviation)
Decision: "load the bigint_test board by JSON import, play the 200 moves via a link, read the exact end in the table view" is three tests: `JSON import loads the bigint_test board`, `200 mutations by link end at the recorded population` (with the table, tooltip and shortened numeral) and `200 firings by link end at the recorded numbers`. Reason: a link carries its own board (`g=c:`), so a link opened after an import replaces the imported board; the two golden sequences also need two single-view links (they start at [1, 0] as a population and as numbers).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: measured size and checks
Decision: none; record. `npm run size -- --port 4432`: `/tools/sandbox/` 78.5 kB gzip over 16 scripts, within 90 kB. nojs.spec.ts, worker-on-demand.spec.ts and a11y.spec.ts passed on every route including /tools/sandbox/.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A5: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D19 only (the name chosen in pickers and links; "B2 = C2" where a board is recognised from its shape). D7 (a = Cp) through T1's Bridge, unchanged. No [U] item settled.

# P2-A7 decisions (phase-2 additions to T1, T2, T9; classify on load; the open-problem page)

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a9c0bc5bea30bc6c6`, branch `worktree-agent-a9c0bc5bea30bc6c6` (the plan's `agent/P2-A7`), fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only` from `5f439e7` to the integration head `03dc851` (stage 2.1 merged) before any file was written; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0; engine suite green first (38 files, 1444 tests). Browser runs used a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 4471 (`reports/playwright.p2a7.config.ts`, ignored by git) and `npm run size` ran with `--port 4472`. No dependency added.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the verdict cache in state.ts; boardVerdict, isFiniteType
Decision: `state.ts` holds a module-level `WeakMap<AnyBoard, readonly ClassifyRecord[]>`. `resolveBoard(spec)` builds the board exactly as before (now in a private `buildBoard`), calls `classify` once, stores the records deep-frozen, and returns the same object; signature unchanged. New exports (plan names): `boardVerdict(board)` (the cached records; a board that did not come through `resolveBoard`, one built by `boardFromName` directly, is classified on first request and cached), `isFiniteType(board)` (true exactly when every record has `class: 'finite'`), and `boardVerdictInLabels(board)` (plan addition: the records with node lists in labels, for "Download data"). `bigBoard(board)` copies the verdict of the board it copies (same matrix), so the SafeInt-to-Big switch of T1 and T9 never classifies again. What the specification left open: the place and shape of the site's cache (E-11 names the engine's). Default used: the plan's (P2-A7 instruction 5.1). Reason: SPEC 5.6 last sentence; the engine's own cache in `guards.ts` is private (P2-A1).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the finite rule on disconnected boards
Decision: `isFiniteType` is false when any component is affine, indefinite or one-way (`class: null`), and true for a board in several finite pieces. This is the same rule as P2-A1's `requireFinite` (which throws `NeedsTwoWayRoads` on a one-way component and `NotFiniteType` unless every class is finite); `guards.ts` exports no boolean predicate, so there is nothing else to compare. Tested in `T09-phase2.test.ts > isFiniteType from classify` (A2 beside A1 is finite; A2 beside the triangle is not). No disagreement found.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: finiteKnown from the verdict (E-9)
Decision: every `CallHint` this task builds passes `finiteKnown: isFiniteType(board)`: T1's play to the end and "play on", T9's "play on" (`t09/playOn.ts`). A custom board that `classify` calls finite (a renumbered E6) now plays on the main thread. `worker.ts` is not changed: its default (`isNamedFinite`) is still the phase-1 rule for callers that pass no `finiteKnown`. `engineCall` sends `playFate` to the worker whatever the hint (P1-A2), which is what E-9 rule 2 names.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: how 5.9 was applied
Decision: (a) T2: `t02/garden.ts`'s `isNamedFinite` is called inside that file by `layersBound`, `totalOf` and `poset.ts` (case 3). The component now calls new functions in a new file `t02/phase2.ts`: `gardenLayersBound` (the `layers` property, else 40 exactly when `isFiniteType` is false) and `gardenTotal` (roots on a board `classify` calls finite: the completed enumeration; otherwise `totalOf`), so the bound and the total follow `classify` without editing `garden.ts`. Left as it was: `positiveView`'s rise style (`poset.ts`), which writes the rise as a numeral on a custom board that `classify` calls finite instead of drawing double lines; nothing false is shown. Request to MERGE: the one-line switch of `garden.ts`'s `isNamedFinite` to `return isFiniteType(board)` (from `../../../lib/state`), together with `T02.test.ts > the rise is written on edges off the finite types`, whose first assertion (`isNamedFinite` of the custom B2 is false) would then change. (b) T9: `t09/kostant.ts`'s `isNamedFinite` and `playControl` are called only by the component (case 2): the component calls `isFiniteType` from `state.ts`, and `playControlPhase2(board)` in a new file `t09/phase2.ts`, which is `isFiniteType(board) ? playControl(board) : 'play on'`. Both old functions stay exported and unused by the component. (c) T1: `t01/model.ts`'s `PANELS_AVAILABLE` and `panelsToDraw` stay empty (P1-A5's file); the component calls `phase2Panels` in a new file `t01/phase2.ts`. Request to MERGE: fill `PANELS_AVAILABLE` with `['form', 'level', 'lengths']` if one source of the list is wanted.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: T2 verdict wording
Decision: `data-out="verdict"` beside the stop message, from `boardVerdict`: one text per component in the order of the records, separated by `; `: the class word `finite`, `affine` or `indefinite` (never a name), and for a one-way component T4's sentence `has a one-way road: the theory does not apply`. The triangle gives `affine`; "two components: A2 and the triangle" gives `finite; affine`. It is rendered only when a stop message is shown, which never happens on a board `classify` calls finite. "Download data" adds `verdict`. D19 does not arise (no name is written).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: T1 panels, wordings and hooks
Decision: each listed panel renders in P1-A5's `<div class="panel extra" data-panel="...">`. `form`: `data-out="formValue"` (with `data-value`, the exact `p/q` or `p`), text `Form value (p, p): <value>` from `formValue`, the value with a true minus sign and shortened above 12 digits (exact digits in the tooltip). Notes in words: `no invariant form: the board is not balanced` (formValue null), `the form value is shown for whole populations only` (a fractional C^{-1}a; formValue takes integers), `the form value needs a population, and this view shows the numbers only` (numbers view). `level`: `data-out="level"` (with `data-value`), `Level: <value>`, from `level` on the current numbers, rendered only when the verdict is one affine component. `lengths`: `data-out="lengths"`, from `symmetrizer`, one sentence per component (`Long nodes 1 and 2; short node 3.`, `All nodes have the same length.`, or for more than two values `More than two lengths; the squared lengths 2d are 2 at node 0, ...`; with several components each sentence starts `Nodes 1 and 2: `), and one `li` per node with `data-label` and `data-length` (`long`, `short`, `equal`, `value`); not balanced: `The board is not balanced, so its nodes have no lengths.` `colours` is never rendered (phase 3). Panels reflow at 320 pixels. "Download data" adds `verdict`, `formValue`, `level`, `lengths`, `fate`.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the affine rule of T1
Decision: P1-A5's fallback already draws only the numbers view for a numbers start whose `toPopulation` is null (every affine board). Added: on the tool's own route the view chooser then lists `numbers` alone and the mode chooser the modes of that view, so only the numbers view is offered (SPEC 4 T1). A population start on an affine board keeps all three views. The level of a numbers start is in the `level` panel when the page lists it.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the playToEnd hook kept, data-play, "Play on" in T1 and T9
Decision: the play control keeps its phase-1 hook `data-control="playToEnd"` on every board and gains `data-play="end"` (visible text `Play to the end`, the phase-1 string unchanged) on a board `classify` calls finite and `data-play="on"` (visible text `Play on`, in the phase-1 capitalisation) on every other board. In T1 this replaces P1-A5's phase-1 rule that a custom board keeps "play to the end": 4.0 assigns "play on" to phase 2. T1, legal mode, off the finite types: `playFate(board, a, undefined, cap)` through `engineCall` (the worker); raising mode: `raisingPlay` (or `play` on -a from a numbers start, as in phase 1) through the worker. T9 on an affine board: `raisingPlay(board, p, undefined, cap)` through `engineCall` with the verdict hint, from `t09/playOn.ts`, imported dynamically on the first "play on" so that the worker client is not in the route's first scripts.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: T1 report strings, and the game-over text kept for ended
Decision: from `playFate`'s status: `ended` shows P1-A5's existing `Game over after N moves.` in `data-out="gameOver"` and not T15's "ended after k moves" (a deliberate deviation from T15's wording: trap 17 forbids rewording a phase-1 string, and SPEC 4 T1's acceptance reads "game over after 3 moves"); `looped` shows `returned to an earlier position after k moves` in a new `data-out="fate"` line (rendered in legal and raising modes off the finite types), k the play's moves; `cap` shows exactly `still running after 20000 moves` in `data-out="limit"`, as before. Announcements: ended and cap as in phase 1 (`playSentence`), looped `Returned to an earlier position after k moves. Numbers now ...` (plan). No sentence about the level is added in T1 (it rests on Appendix E item 6; T15's).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the cap of a "play on" and the disabled second press
Decision: the cap of one "play on" is `min(20000, 20000 - moves already made)`; when that is 0 the control is disabled. So the moves list never passes the link maximum of 6.6 and "Copy link" never writes a list the codec refuses. From the start (the tested cases) the cap is 20000 and the report reads `still running after 20000 moves`. Applied in T1 and T9 (the instruction names T9; T1 has the same link limit). Finite boards keep phase 1's play to the end.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: T9 affine board, caption, link, verdict, announcement
Decision: `affineBoardName(board, end)` in `t09/phase2.ts`: `short` exactly when `end` equals `highestShortRoot(board)`; the name is `identifyAffine(affineExtension(board, short).board)`. It is computed only when the end panel shows a named end (the highest root or the highest short root), so it never appears in the static default (the default is not at the end) nor on an affine board. `data-out="affineBoard"` shows the data spelling (`D4~`, `C2~` for the board of B2, `D4^(2)`) with `data-name` holding the same: `names.json` has no affine names (request to the owner of `names.mjs`); D19 does not apply to affine names. The button is a link, `data-control="openLab"`, text `add the city`, to `${BASE_URL}tools/lab?g=<name>` with `~`, `^`, `(` and `)` left readable (the brackets are a plan addition; 6.6 lists commas, semicolons, dots, `~` and `^`); it is rendered only once the play has ended and never while a prediction is unanswered; 44-pixel target. "play on" on an affine board reports `still running after 20000 moves` in `data-out="limit"` and the verdict in `data-out="verdict"` (plan text: the class word and the name, `affine D4~`; a finite B2 would read `B2=C2`, D19), rendered only off the finite types. One announcement for a play that reached the cap: `Still running after 20000 moves.` (plan; A-4 has no template). On an affine board the end panel and its finite-theory calls are never made (`isFiniteType` first). "Download data" adds `verdict` and `affineBoard`.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: T9 and the worker, and P1-A7's source test
Decision: E-9 rule 2 sends a play to the end on a board that is not of finite type to the worker, so T9 needs `engineCall` for "play on". The call lives in `t09/playOn.ts`, imported dynamically, and the component source names neither `engineCall` nor `lib/worker`, which keeps `T09.test.ts > every call stays on the main thread` (a source scan) green; that test's intent ("every call stays on the main thread") holds for every board T9 offers in phase 1. Recorded so that MERGE can decide whether the scan should name `t09/playOn.ts` as the one allowed route.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: phase-1 tests that assert the absence of a phase-2 slot
Decision: five phase-1 tests assert that a slot SPEC 4 fills from phase 2 is empty; they fail by design once the slot is filled, and none was edited or skipped: `T01.test.ts > T1 server render > no panel in phase 1` (expected by the instruction); `T09.test.ts > T9 server render > the end panel renders at build time when the state is at the end` (its lines `outs(b3, 'affineBoard')` empty and no `openLab`; SPEC 4 T9: "From phase 2 the caption names that board ... and a button opens it in T4"); `T09.spec.ts > A3 from city 1 ends at (1,1,1) after 2 moves with final numbers (1,0,1)` (`affineBoard` and `openLab` counted 0); `T02.spec.ts > the triangle grows to 243 and says stopped after 40 layers` (`verdict` counted 0; SPEC 4 T2: "From phase 2 the verdict "affine" from classify is shown beside the message"); `M1.spec.ts > no forbidden statement` (beat 5 may not contain the word "affine"; T2's figure b8 in beat 5 now shows the verdict `affine` from classify beside "stopped after 40 layers", which 4.0 allows because it is not inferred from the cap). Replacements in my files: `T09-phase2.test.ts > panel filter in phase 2 renders form, level and lengths and never colours`, `> the end panel names the affine board and links to T4 (replaces the phase-1 absence check)`, `phase2-additions.spec.ts > T2 on the triangle stops after 40 layers with the verdict affine`, `> T9 on D4 names D4~ after play to the end`. Requests to MERGE to retire or narrow each (M1: let beat 5 show the classify verdict, or give T2 a property that hides it on that figure, which the lesson owner would pass).

## 2026-10-10 P2-A7: the open-problem page
Decision: `site/src/content/pages/open-problem.mdx`, route `/open-problem/`, front matter `title: "The Mutation Conjecture"`, `phase: 2`, `released: true`, a description; no tool island (0 kB of JavaScript measured). Decision: T1's panels and T9 on `D4~` or with a prediction cannot be reached through a link (panels and predictions are properties; `T09.ts` offers only the 32 finite names). `phase2-additions.spec.ts` bundles T01GameBoard and T09Kostant with Vite, renders the server HTML with `ssrLoader`, and serves both from a local `node:http` server (as `T09.spec.ts` does with page routes; a real server so that the engine worker is fetched normally). The fixture page gives `<main>` Base.astro's side padding, which ToolFrame's edge-to-edge figure expects below 400 pixels. Observed hazard (not fixed, not mine): `ssrLoader`'s cache directory is shared between the Playwright process and the Vitest project; after a Playwright run that used it, `toolframe.test.ts` and `history.test.ts` failed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'Symbol(filename)')` until `/tmp/two-games-ssr-test-cache-<key>` was deleted. Request to MERGE: separate cache directories per runner.

- Row 5: b8 T9 `board="D4"`, `start={[1, 0, 0, 0]}` (one Martian in city 1), `boardChoices={['D4']}`, with the second prediction; the default is the start (not the end), so without JavaScript the prose and the caption state the end, the final numbers and the new board.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A9: challenges (8.5) and their checks
Decision: four, each a `Challenge` (`client:visible`) on its own tool instance, after the Lie theory paragraph:
1. "Classify", b10 T4 on `A5`: close the path into a ring of six cities. Check `{ kind: 'out', name: 'verdictName', equals: 'Ã5' }` (the textContent of the typeset name, built from `affine.A5.name` with a combining tilde). The plan's goal "six cities, single roads both ways, affine" admits both A5~ and D5~, and Challenge's `out` check reads one hook with one value, so the goal names the ring (request below).
2. "Find a counterexample", b11 T4 on `A2` with `hunter={true}`: take away the road from city 2 back to city 1 and let the hunter search. Check `{ kind: 'out', name: 'hunterResult', equals: <the hunter sentence built from one_way_counterexample> }`. The hunter writes "none among ..." when it finds nothing, so a non-empty check would be met at load; the exact sentence is the only `out` reading that means "found". A test confirms that the reverse one-way road (also a counterexample, from city 2) does not meet this goal, which names the road to remove.
3. "Reach a target", b12 T1 on `D4~` from one Martian in city 0: reach (2, 1, 2, 1, 1). Check `{ kind: 'reach', board: 'populations', values }` with the values computed from the golden marks. The test plays 2, 0, 1, 3, 4, 2, 0 (found by a breadth-first search in a scratch script).
4. "Classify", b13 T4 on `E6`: draw the affine board of E6 with the recipe of beat five. Check `verdictName` equal to `affine.E6.name` with the combining tilde.
The optional fifth (a smallest non-finite sub-board) was dropped: T4 reports the sub-board but lets the reader mark none.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A9: Unverified items and fallbacks
Decision: two wraps.
- E4 (beat 6): "For such boards the statement is true: a root population is a real root of the Kac-Moody algebra of the board, and in Kac's theory every root of such an algebra is a combination of simple roots with coefficients all non-negative or all non-positive [Kac90]." Fallback: "On every board with two-way roads that the script checking this site has tried, no root population mixes Martians and anti-Martians: every board of finite type, and the first 2500 or so root populations of every untwisted affine board of rank at most 8 and of ten twisted affine boards." (H2 to H4 dropped; the reader has not met them.) [Kac90] is cited only inside this wrap and is not in the Sources list; it stays in the frontmatter's `sources` because every cited key must be there.
- E10 (beat 7): "the theorem behind that list is due to J. H. Smith (1970)." Fallback: "their book names the original source of that list, which this site has not yet checked." The [BrH] citation stands outside the wrap ([S] for the section title).
- E6: not wrapped in the lesson's prose (the fate table is M4's), but `T15Route.astro` registers the page as a user of E6, so the release report lists `/modules/m3-classification/` under E4, E6 and E10. The frozen preset shows no fate row.
- E14: not used; the lesson says nothing about Kac's tables. No new item proposed.

## 2026-10-10 P2-A9: provenance and credit
Decision: the three-way theorem and the A-D-E list for simple graphs: [W20] Theorems 4.1 and 8.8 (beat 2 and the Seen box), with "simple graph" in every sentence that gives the list, and one sentence that boards with multiple roads add B_n, C_n, F_4, G_2, whose forms are positive definite too (Appendix F). Affine boards as Wildberger's list: [W20] Theorem 4.1. Frozen population fixed by every mutation, and "every city is suitable" on the boards of the lesson: verified by computation (2.12 computed refinement); one Martian plus the frozen population: [W20] Proposition 6.1. Kostant's recipe: verified by computation for every board of finite type and every starting city; Kostant's game "attributed to Kostant". One-way counterexample: verified by computation; the bidirected reading is stated as "this site's interpretation". [Pro93] is cited for classification puzzles on the same condition (S2, nothing more). [DE08] Theorem 2.1 for the numbers game. [HHSV77]: a survey on the ubiquity of these diagrams that quotes Arnold's Problem VIII; no appearance of the list is named from memory. [W-MS] is "an unpublished manuscript, undated (circulated c. 2001 to 2004)" in the Sources note. Seen box provenance prop: `['W20', 'verified by computation on every untwisted affine board of rank at most eight', 'verified by computation for every board of finite type and every starting city', 'verified by computation']`.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: the functions are written once, generically, with AnyBoard overloads
Decision: every function of `weyl.ts` is written once over `Arith<T>` (E-2), with the overload pattern of P2-A1 (`<T>(board: Board<T>, ...)` first, then `(board: AnyBoard, ...)`) so that tools can pass the union type. Products that can exceed 2^53 (`weylDimension`, `countCompleteGames`) are `bigint` through `arith.toBig`; group orders and orbit sizes are numbers multiplied in `SafeInt`, so an impossible overflow throws `EngineOverflow` instead of returning a wrong number. What the specification left open: E-2 allows writing a core function twice [D]. Default used: once. Reason: generics proved no harder than in phases 1 and 2; the guards test exercises the `Big` path on a renumbered A3.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: the wave's bound and what it returns there
Decision: `wavePlay` stops after the round that makes `rounds` exceed 10000 (the module constant `WAVE_ROUNDS_CAP`, the oracle's `cap=10_000` tested after each round, so the oracle raises after round 10001) and returns the `WaveResult` reached there: `rounds` 10001, `legal` as accumulated, the position and word so far. From a start that is not dominant the wave can run for ever on a finite board (A3 from `(2, -4, -3)` with either colour first, checked against the oracle). What the specification left open: neither SPEC.md nor CONTRACTS.md says what to return at the bound, and `WaveResult` has no `status` field; E-10 forbids throwing at a cap. Default used: the plan's (P3-A1 instruction 5.4). Reason: a wave that ends within 10001 rounds behaves exactly as the oracle's; the missing `status` field is raised under `openQuestions` as a contract amendment, and no field was added to `WaveResult` on my own.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: weylDimension throws RangeError on a lambda that is not dominant or of the wrong length
Decision: `lambda` must have one entry per node, each a non-negative safe integer; anything else is a `RangeError` before the guards run. The replay from `lambda + rho` uses `fire` in the board's own arithmetic (never re-derived) and multiplies the numbers met into a `bigint`; a remainder from `M / D`, impossible for a dominant `lambda`, throws an `Error` naming the board rather than returning a rounded value. What the specification left open: the behaviour on bad input (the oracle asserts). Default used: `RangeError`, the choice of `toPositions` and of P1-A1 and P2-A1 for malformed arguments. Reason: 4 T5 says the calculator takes a dominant lambda; raised under `openQuestions`.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: countCompleteGames tests |W| > cap before building a layer
Decision: `countCompleteGames(board, cap = CAPS.countGamesGroup)` returns `null` exactly when `weylOrder(board) > cap`, decided from the game from rho before the dynamic programme starts, so no layer is built for a group that is too large; a cap equal to |W| still returns the count (D6 at 23040). `cap` must be a non-negative integer (`RangeError`). What the specification left open: the oracle's limit is 70000 positions found; SPEC.md 5.5 and 5.6 offer the count for |W| <= 60000. Default used: the plan's reading (instruction 5.8). Reason: the two rules give the same presence on all 32 types (the smallest order above 60000 is D7's 322560), and the engine accepts any cap the caller passes (5.5).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: the roster's E6 trap corrected
Decision: E6 is counted: |W(E6)| = 51840 is below 60000, `types.E6.n_reduced_words_w0` is present with the value 1266633313578528, and `countCompleteGames(E6)` returns `1266633313578528n` (tested). The types that return `null` at the default cap are A8, B7, B8, C7, C8, D7, D8, E7, E8. The reason the count is `bigint` is E-4, not a golden value above 2^53 (every present value is below it). What the specification left open: nothing; the roster's trap text ("E6 returns null") contradicts the golden file and 5.6. Default used: the golden file and SPEC.md (COMMON.md 2: the oracle wins). Reason: reported under `openQuestions` so that the roster can be corrected.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: the order of bipartition's classes
Decision: `bipartition` returns `[class0, class1]`, each a fresh array of positions in increasing order; the least position of each component (the oracle's walk starts there) has colour 0. A1 gives `[[0], []]`; `square` gives `[[0, 2], [1, 3]]`; an odd cycle anywhere (A2~, pentagon, a triangle beside an A2) gives `null`. No guard: it works on one-way boards too (the one-way road gives `[[0], [1]]`). The golden mapping is `colour[i] = class0.includes(i) ? 0 : 1`. What the specification left open: the oracle returns one colour per node; the contract type `[number[], number[]] | null` does not say which class comes first. Default used: the plan's reading (instruction 5.3). Reason: the colour-per-node form is recovered in one line, and T1's colour-class panel and T5's wave need the classes as lists.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: the unguarded helpers for |W| and |W_J|
Decision: `weyl.ts` has two private helpers: `groupOrder(board)` (the game from rho, the exponents of the tally, the product in `SafeInt`; 1 on no nodes; `NotFiniteType` if the play reaches its cap, which cannot happen after the guard) and `parabolicOrder(board, J)` (1 for the empty set, otherwise the product over the connected components of `subBoard(board, J)` of `groupOrder` of each component's induced board). `orbitSize` uses both after one `play` of the given position; the public `weylOrder` is `groupOrder` behind `requireGcm` and `requireFinite`. What the specification left open: the oracle calls `weyl_order(sub_matrix(C, J))`, which on a disconnected sub-matrix equals this product, and an empty J cannot be a board. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.6, trap 11). Reason: an empty sub-board never meets `requireFinite`, no sub-board pays for a `classify`, and E-9 item 1 wants `orbitSize` cheap (one play of at most N moves plus small plays).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: separatingRoots uses the internal corootOf
Decision: `separatingRoots` pairs `a` with `corootOf(board, beta)` of `form.ts` (P2-A1's internal, unguarded coroot as plain integers) for each `beta` of `positiveRoots(board)` with its default cap, computing the pairing in `bigint`, and returns the roots themselves (fresh arrays from the search) in the order of `positiveRoots`. What the specification left open: whether to call the guarded `coroot` once per root. Default used: the internal helper after this function's own guard. Reason: one guard per call instead of N; the result is identical (the B2 hand cases and F5 on every type with |W| <= 2000 are tested).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: legalLayers with a cap of 0, and the RangeError cases
Decision: `legalLayers(board, start?, cap = CAPS.legalLayersDefault)` returns `null` for `cap` 0 (the start itself exceeds it; `orbitGraph` would refuse a cap below the number of seeds) and otherwise `null` exactly when `orbitGraph(..., legalOnly)` reports `truncated`. `RangeError` on: a vector of the wrong length (`wavePlay`, `orbitSize`, `legalLayers`, `separatingRoots`, `weylDimension`), a `firstColour` other than 0 or 1, a cap that is not a non-negative integer, an `n` that is not a non-negative integer in `exponentsFromFired`. The order of checks is P2-A1's: argument shape, then `requireGcm`, then `requireFinite`. What the specification left open: invalid arguments. Default used: `RangeError`, as the earlier phases did. Reason: E-10 reserves the engine's classes for the theory; a cap is never thrown.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A1: WaveResult is declared in weyl.ts
Decision: `WaveResult<T>` is declared in `weyl.ts` verbatim from CONTRACTS.md 2.2 and re-exported as a type from `index.ts` inside the phase-3 block. What the specification left open: the file. Default used: the plan's placement, as P1-A1 and P2-A1 did for their record types. Reason: the type lives with the function that returns it.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: the heapKey text format
Decision: `heapKey(board, word)` is the multiset of (node, level) pairs of `heap`, sorted by node and then by level, each pair written `node:level` over positions, joined by commas; the empty word gives `""`. On A3 the play `[0, 1, 0]` gives `0:1,0:3,1:2`. Two words have equal keys exactly when the oracle's `tuple(sorted(zip(word, heap(C, word))))` are equal. What the specification left open: the text format ("a key that is equal for plays with the same heap"). Default used: the plan's suggestion (P3-A2 instruction 5.3). Reason: T8 (P3-A6) groups games by this string; a deterministic text over positions is enough and is cheap to compute.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: cap semantics of allCompleteGames and the bound for plays that do not end
Decision: `allCompleteGames(board, start, cap)` enumerates depth first with the positive nodes in increasing position (the oracle's order, so the items are in lexicographic order and the first is `play`'s word). When the search is about to record game number `cap + 1` it stops at once with exactly `cap` items and `truncated: true`; otherwise `truncated` is `false` (a cap of 0 gives `truncated: true` with no items as soon as one complete game exists). Plan rule for plays that do not end: when a single play reaches `CAPS.playMoves` (20000) moves without ending, the search stops at once with `truncated: true` and the complete games found so far. On a finite-type board every play from one start has the same length (F3; from rho it is N, F5), so there the bound binds only when that length exceeds 20000; on a one-way, affine, indefinite or reader-drawn board SPEC.md 2.7 gives no such guarantee, so the bound can cut a search in which some plays end and others do not (plan assumption): such a result carries `truncated: true` and is never presented as the complete list. A cap that is not a non-negative integer or a start of the wrong length is a `RangeError`. What the specification left open: the oracle raises `OverflowError` at `limit + 1` games and has no bound on the length of a play. Default used: the plan's (5.5, from E-6, E-10 and 5.4 "stops at the cap when the plays do not end"). Reason: a cap is reported, never thrown; "No function loops without a bound".

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: explicit stack in allCompleteGames
Decision: the search keeps its own stack of frames (the position, its positive nodes in increasing order, and the index of the next one to try) and the current word; no recursion. What the specification left open: the oracle is recursive. Default used: the plan's (5.5). Reason: a 20000-move play on an affine board from all ones would overflow the JavaScript call stack; with the explicit stack `allCompleteGames(boardFromName('A2~'), [1n, 1n, 1n], 100000)` returns `{ items: [], truncated: true }` in well under a second.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: bigint masks in commutationClassSize
Decision: the set of bricks already laid is a `bigint` mask (`1n << BigInt(k)` per brick, precomputed once), `below[k]` is a `bigint` mask of the earlier bricks at the same or a joined node, the memo is a `Map<bigint, bigint>` keyed by the mask, and the count is the oracle's memoised recursion (`count(full) = 1`; `count(mask)` sums over the bricks not in the mask whose `below` is inside it; the answer is `count(0n)`); the empty word gives `1n`. Tested with 40 bricks (two unjoined nodes fired 20 times each give C(40, 20) = 137846528820). What the specification left open: the representation of the bit mask ("a bit mask"). Default used: `bigint` (P3-A2 instruction trap 7). Reason: JavaScript's `1 << k` and `&` work on 32-bit signed integers and words longer than 30 moves occur.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: "joined" in heap and commutationClassSize
Decision: a brick rests on an earlier brick when the nodes are equal or `C[i][j]` or `C[j][i]` is nonzero, tested through `arith.sign` (never `!== 0`, since `0n !== 0` is true); so on the one-way board `[[2, 0], [-1, 2]]` the two nodes are joined and `heap([0, 1])` is `[1, 2]`. What the specification left open: nothing; this is the oracle's test. Reason: the port must match on `Board<bigint>` boards as well as on named finite boards.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: the overflow rule of symbolicPlay
Decision: `symbolicPlay` computes the running matrix exactly in `bigint` (the Cartan entries through `arith.toBig`), records row `i` before each move, then replaces every row `j` by `row j - C[j][i] * row i`, and converts each recorded entry to a `number`, throwing `EngineOverflow` when an entry is not a safe integer. No legality test: any word is played. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 2.4 says `number[][]` without saying what happens when a coefficient passes 2^53. Default used: the plan's reading (5.6). Reason: on every finite type the entries are root coefficients and small; on a `Board<bigint>` board with a long word they can grow, and E-10 names `EngineOverflow` for exactly this.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: how choleskyBasis converts rationals to floats, and its guards
Decision: `choleskyBasis` calls `requireGcm` then `requireFinite` before any arithmetic (so a one-way board throws `NeedsTwoWayRoads` and `A2~` or `pentagon` throws `NotFiniteType`), then takes `gram(board)` and converts each entry as `Number(num) / Number(den)`; the result is the oracle's upper triangular `U` as a list of rows with entries below the diagonal exactly `0`. The branch for `gram` returning `null` after the guards cannot be reached (a finite-type board is balanced) and throws `NotFiniteType` rather than using a non-null assertion. What the specification left open: the conversion (2.14 says only that floating point enters here). Default used: the plan's (5.7). Reason: `form.ts` and `classify.ts` are not mine to change; the numerators and denominators of the symmetrizer of a board with at most 12 nodes are tiny, so the quotient is exact to the last bit of a double.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: AnyBoard overloads and RangeError cases
Decision: `heap`, `heapKey`, `commutationClassSize`, `symbolicPlay`, `allCompleteGames` and `choleskyBasis` each have the generic overload `<T>(board: Board<T>, ...)` first and an `AnyBoard` overload second (`allCompleteGames` with `Vec<number | bigint>`), the pattern of P1-A1's `transposeBoard` and P2-A1's phase-2 functions. A word entry that is not a position 0..n-1 is a `RangeError` in the four functions of words (as `mutate` and `fire` do). What the specification left open: the contract writes `board` as "`Board<number>` or `Board<bigint>`". Default used: the plan's pattern. Reason: `boardFromName` and the site's `resolveBoard` return `AnyBoard`; without the overloads `tsc` rejects every tool call on one.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: the benchmark command, the measured median, and the request for the script
Decision: `packages/engine/vitest.config.ts` includes only `test/golden`, `test/properties` and `test/unit`, Vitest 5.0.3's default include pattern (`**/*.{test,spec}.?(c|m)[jt]s?(x)`) does not collect `*.bench.ts`, `vitest bench` runs benchmark mode (which reports timings and does not fail on a threshold), and the CLI has no top-level `--include` option (only `--dir`, `--exclude`, `--project`). The benchmark is therefore written with `describe`, `it` and `expect`, and run alone, from the repository root, by

    node_modules/.bin/vitest run --root packages/engine --config <scratch>/vitest.bench.config.ts

where the scratch configuration is a plain object (no import, so that it resolves nothing from its own directory): `{ test: { name: 'engine-bench', root: '<worktree>/packages/engine', include: ['test/bench/**/*.bench.ts'], fileParallelism: false } }`. Requested from MERGE (pre-approved by the orchestrator): a script `"bench"` in `packages/engine/package.json` that runs only this file, kept out of `npm test` because timing under the parallel suite is noisy; the one-line form `"bench": "vitest run --config test/bench/vitest.config.ts"` with that same object committed as `packages/engine/test/bench/vitest.config.ts` (a file outside my ownership list, hence the request) is the simplest. Measured on this cloud container (4 processors, shared with P3-A1 and P3-A7; checked quiet with `uptime` and `ps` first, load average 0.90, no other vitest, astro or chromium process): the run that printed its numbers gave a median of 25.79 ms over the seven runs [33.91, 28.34, 28.84, 25.79, 24.33, 22.21, 23.27] ms, with 2000 positions and `truncated: true`, so the test FAILED the 25 ms line by 0.79 ms. An earlier run of the same command, before the print line was changed (see the next entry), PASSED but printed no numbers; it is not counted as the measurement. No retry was made (COMMON.md 4.4, P3-A2 instruction 5.15). For scale, `node reference/bench_orbit.mjs` run alone on the same machine right after gave 21.0 ms in `Big` for the same case, against the 9 ms that SPEC.md 5.5 records for the specification's server processor: this container is about 2.3 times slower than the processor the 25 ms line was sized for, and the engine's `orbitGraph` is about 23 percent slower than the reference script here (the reference skips zero Cartan entries in its mutation and the engine's `mutate` multiplies all twelve, through the `Arith` interface, and copies the vector). `search.ts` and `moves.ts` are P1-A1's and were not changed; the item is reported `partial` with a request through MERGE. What the specification left open: how the file is wired (7.6 names the Vitest benchmark; CONTRACTS.md 3 only its path). Default used: the plan's (5.15). Reason: a benchmark that no command runs fails the G3 item; the threshold is fixed by SPEC.md 7.6 and may not be raised.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: the benchmark prints its line with process.stdout.write
Decision: the median line is written with `process.stdout.write`, not `console.info`. What the specification left open: the instruction suggests `console.info` "for example". Reason: with the installed Vitest 5.0.3 and its default reporter, `console.info` and `console.log` from a passing test did not reach the terminal in this environment (checked with a scratch test printing through all three channels: only the direct write appeared; `silent` is unset, whose default is `false`), and GATE needs the line to quote the median.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A2: fast-check seed and run count of property 12
Decision: `p12-heaps.test.ts` records `SEED = 20261010` (the seed of P0-A2, P1-A1 and P2-A1) and `NUM_RUNS = 600`; the arbitrary is a type name, a word of 0 to 16 positions and an index `k`; the run counts the cases where a swap happened and asserts the count is positive; the swapped bricks are also shown to exchange levels while every other brick keeps its level (a stronger statement than the key equality, true because the two bricks are unjoined); the fixed counter-example A3 `[0, 1, 0]` against `[1, 0, 1]` shows the property is not vacuous. What the specification left open: the seed and count. Default used: the plan's minimum of 200 runs, raised to 600 because only a fraction of random cases swap. Reason: CONTRACTS.md 3, "a fixed seed recorded in the file".

Decision: golden files `test/golden/p3b-heaps.test.ts` (registers `heaps`), `p3b-pentagon-games.test.ts` (registers `pentagon_example.complete_games`), `p3b-complete-games.test.ts`, `p3b-symbolic.test.ts`, `p3b-cholesky.test.ts`, `p3b-kinds.test.ts` (no key); the property file `test/properties/p12-heaps.test.ts`; the benchmark `test/bench/orbit.bench.ts`. ## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: the lab tool is Lighthouse CI (Branch A)
Decision: `npm run lab` measures the lab timings of SPEC.md 7.6 with Lighthouse CI, `@lhci/cli` 0.15.1 (which runs Lighthouse 12.6.1), not with the Playwright fallback. What the specification left open: 7.6 [D] names Lighthouse CI as the intended tool, with its current version and its behaviour under Node 22 unchecked [U], and gives a Playwright fallback if it "cannot be made to run within an hour"; 7.1 [U] leaves the version to "the version current when phase 3 starts". Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10 is involved. Reason: the time-boxed trial of the instruction (5.2) succeeded in about two minutes, and 7.6 names Lighthouse CI as the intended tool, so it is chosen even though the fallback would need no dependency.

Trial (wall-clock, UTC, from the first install command to the first good measurement):
- Start 2026-10-10T20:31:22Z (`npm --prefix <tmp> install --save-exact @lhci/cli@0.15.1`, 330 packages in 17 s, in a scratch directory outside the repository). End 2026-10-10T20:32:58Z. About 2 minutes of the hour.
- Registry, read on 2026-10-10: `npm view @lhci/cli version` 0.15.1; `npm view @lhci/cli engines` printed nothing (the package declares no engines); `npm view @lhci/cli license` Apache-2.0; `npm view @lhci/cli dependencies` lists `lighthouse: '12.6.1'` exactly, together with `chrome-launcher ^0.13.4`, `express`, `yargs` and others. `lighthouse` 12.6.1 declares `engines.node >=18.20`. The newest `lighthouse` on the registry is 13.5.0 (engines `>=22.19`), which `@lhci/cli` 0.15.1 does not use. The package name `@lhci/cli` that the plan remembered is the published one.
- What ran: `astro preview` of the preview build on port 4324, then `lhci collect` with a configuration file in the scratch directory and its working directory there (run files in `<tmp>/.lighthouseci/`), `chromePath` set to Playwright's Chromium (`require('@playwright/test').chromium.executablePath()`), one run each of `/` and `/modules/m0-start/`, with the settings `formFactor: 'mobile'`, `screenEmulation { mobile: true, width: 360, height: 800, deviceScaleFactor: 1, disabled: false }`, `throttlingMethod: 'devtools'`, `throttling { cpuSlowdownMultiplier: 4, requestLatencyMs: 0, downloadThroughputKbps: 0, uploadThroughputKbps: 0, rttMs: 0, throughputKbps: 0 }`, `onlyCategories: ['performance']`, Chrome flag `--no-sandbox`.
- Result: both reports carry `configSettings.throttlingMethod` `devtools`, `configSettings.throttling.cpuSlowdownMultiplier` 4 and the screen 360 by 800, no `runtimeError` and no run warning, and numeric values for the audits `largest-contentful-paint`, `total-blocking-time` and `cumulative-layout-shift` (`/`: 393 ms, 106.6 ms, 0.0005; `/modules/m0-start/`: 406 ms, 640.3 ms, 0; one unrepeated run each on a shared machine, functional evidence only).
- Read in the installed Lighthouse 12.6.1 (`core/lib/emulation.js`): with `throttlingMethod: 'devtools'` it sends `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` with `rate` equal to `cpuSlowdownMultiplier`, before navigation, through the DevTools Protocol, which is what 7.6 asks of the fallback ("the processor slowed through a DevTools Protocol session"). With `simulate`, its default, it sends no such call and only clears network throttling, so a simulated run never counts (roster trap 2).
- Settlement of the [U] items (Appendix E item 17): 7.1, the version current when phase 3 starts, is `@lhci/cli` 0.15.1 (Lighthouse 12.6.1), read from the registry on 2026-10-10. 7.6, the behaviour under Node 22: it installs and runs under Node 22.22.0 with npm 10.9.4 on this machine without error, launches Playwright's Chromium (Chromium 141 here), applies DevTools throttling at 4 and yields the three numbers.
- One incident to record: the plan's step `npm --prefix <tmp> init -y` does not honour `--prefix` in npm 10.9.4. It merged its defaults into the package.json of the current directory, which was the worktree root (it added `description`, `main`, `keywords`, `author` and a `dependencies` list built from `node_modules`). The lockfile was not touched. The change was reverted at once with `git checkout -- package.json` and `git status --porcelain package.json package-lock.json` then printed nothing; the later `npm --prefix <tmp> install` did write only into `<tmp>`. A future trial should create `<tmp>/package.json` with the Write tool instead of `npm init`.
- The scratch directory was deleted by its absolute path after that check; nothing of the trial is committed.

Decision: one devDependency in the root `package.json`, `"@lhci/cli": "0.15.1"` (exact), installed with the single command `npm install --save-dev --save-exact @lhci/cli@0.15.1` at the repository root. It is a build-time tool and is never shipped to the reader, so no size. The lockfile gains 311 entries; no entry that was there before changed version or flags (checked by comparing the `packages` maps of the two lockfiles). `npm audit` reports 21 vulnerabilities after the install (6 low, 4 moderate, 11 high) against 4 low before; the new ones are all in the tree of `@lhci/cli` (`lighthouse`, `puppeteer-core`, `@puppeteer/browsers`, `proxy-agent`, `get-uri`, `basic-ftp`, `tmp`, `uuid`, `js-yaml`, `inquirer`), which runs only on the builder's machine against the local preview server. ## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: versions
Decision: the lab runs with Node 22.22.0, npm 10.9.4, `@playwright/test` 1.64.0, Playwright's Chromium (reported by the binary as Chromium 141.0.7390.37 in this container, whose Chromium is linked in under the build number Playwright expects), `@lhci/cli` 0.15.1 and Lighthouse 12.6.1. What the specification left open: 7.6 [U]. Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: these are what the trial and the live runs used; the first output line of every run prints them.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: the profile as implemented
Decision: every Lighthouse run uses `formFactor: 'mobile'`, `screenEmulation { mobile: true, width: 360, height: 800, deviceScaleFactor: 1, disabled: false }`, `throttlingMethod: 'devtools'` with `throttling.cpuSlowdownMultiplier: 4` (Lighthouse then sends `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` with rate 4 through the DevTools Protocol before the page loads), and `requestLatencyMs`, `downloadThroughputKbps`, `uploadThroughputKbps`, `rttMs`, `throughputKbps` all 0, which Lighthouse 12.6.1 sends as `Network.emulateNetworkConditions` with latency 0 and throughput 0, the same values it uses to clear network throttling. Also `onlyCategories: ['performance']`, `locale: 'en-US'`, `maxWaitForLoad: 30000`, the Chrome flag `--no-sandbox` (Playwright launches Chromium without the sandbox as well; the container runs as root), and Lighthouse's own mobile user agent. Lighthouse CI adds `--headless=new`; each run is a new Chromium process with a new profile, so every run is a first visit with an empty cache. The page is never touched: Lighthouse navigates and records, it does not scroll, so `client:visible` islands below the first view stay unhydrated. What the specification left open: 7.6 [D] names only "360 by 800 pixels, processor slowed four times". Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: the plan reads the parenthesis as the whole profile, so the network is not throttled; Lighthouse's default mobile profile (a 412 by 823 screen, a simulated slow 4G network and a simulated processor) is replaced explicitly. Whether the owner wants the network throttled as Lighthouse's own preset does is an open question.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: the three numbers and the end of the measurement
Decision: LCP, TBT and CLS are the `numericValue` of Lighthouse's audits `largest-contentful-paint`, `total-blocking-time` and `cumulative-layout-shift` (confirmed in the installed version). A missing or errored value makes the run an error, never zero; zero TBT and zero CLS are legitimate. The end of the measurement is Lighthouse's: under devtools throttling it raises its network quiet window, its processor quiet window and its pauses after FCP and after load to at least 5250 ms (`overrideThrottlingWindows` in `core/config/config.js`), so the plan's quiet window of five seconds is met; `maxWaitForLoad: 30000` is the ceiling, and a run whose report carries Lighthouse's warning "The page loaded too slowly to finish within the time limit" is an error ("did not settle within 30 s"), as is any `runtimeError`. TBT is Lighthouse's: the blocking parts (beyond 50 ms) of the long tasks between FCP and Time to Interactive. CLS is Lighthouse's largest session window (1 s gap, 5 s cap) of shifts without recent input. What the specification left open: 7.6 names the metrics, not their computation; the instruction's 5.6 gives plan definitions "after web-vitals and Lighthouse" for the Playwright fallback. Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: on Branch A the tool's own definitions are the measurement; the plan's raw-entry functions (TBT over recorded long tasks, CLS over recorded shifts, the quiet window over a simulated timeline) have no input on this branch, so their unit tests are replaced by tests of the report readers, of the timeout and runtime-error rule and of the settings (a deviation, reported). The long tasks Lighthouse lists (audit `long-tasks`) are kept in the `--json` output as evidence for an OVER on TBT.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: runs, median, thresholds, statuses, exit codes, output
Decision: three runs per route by default (`--runs`), each metric's median taken separately (the mean of the two middle values for an even count); a run in error makes the route an error and is never dropped. `THRESHOLDS = { lcpMs: 2500, tbtMs: 200, cls: 0.1 }` and `PROFILE = { width: 360, height: 800, cpuSlowdown: 4 }` are exported constants; a median passes when it is at most its threshold, compared unrounded. A route is `ok`, `OVER` (naming each failing metric), `missing` or `error` (with its reason). Exit 0 when every route is ok, the canary failed all three thresholds and the processor check passed; exit 1 for any other row, no route, no build (`lab: no build found at <dir>; run npm run build first`), a server that does not start, a canary that passes a threshold or a failed processor check; exit 2 for a usage error. Output: a first line with the tool and its versions, Chromium, the profile, the runs, the built directory, the base and the processor check; the table (LCP in s to three decimals, TBT in ms to one, CLS to four, under the headers `LCP (<= 2.5 s)`, `TBT (<= 200 ms)`, `CLS (<= 0.1)`); the canary line; `lab: N routes measured, M over threshold, E missing or in error`. Progress, and Lighthouse CI's own output prefixed `lhci:`, go to standard error. `--json <file>` writes the tool and versions, the profile, the settings, every run's numbers and long tasks, the medians, the statuses, the canary and the settings read from every report; files under `reports/` are never committed. Lighthouse CI itself retries a Lighthouse process that crashes, up to three attempts (`runUntilSuccess`); that is the tool's handling of a crashed process, not a retry of a measured result, and the script never repeats a run. What the specification left open: all of this is plan. Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: the instruction's 5.7 and 5.8.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: route lists, base, serving, the non-200 rule
Decision: `npm run lab` measures the core path: `/` and, for k from 0 to 5, the one lesson whose slug begins with `m<k>-` (the hyphen keeps `m1-` from matching `m10-`; `/modules/` is not a lesson); no match is a `missing` row and fails the run, two matches an `error` row. `npm run lab -- <route> ...` measures exactly those routes in order, normalising a missing trailing slash to the built form; a route with no HTML file is `missing`. `npm run lab -- --all` measures every route of `routesOf` imported from `scripts/size.mjs`, the same list `npm run size` measures (Astro's `build.format` is the default `directory` in the merged configuration; the `.html` form is handled too). Options `--url`, `--dist`, `--port` (default 4324, beside 4323 of `size.mjs` and 4321 of `playwright.config.ts`), `--runs`, `--json`, `--help`; `--all` with routes, an unknown option or a bad value prints the usage and exits 2. The base is `siteAndBase(process.env).base` imported from `site/astro.config.mjs`, and route URLs come from `routeUrl` imported from `size.mjs`; with `--url` the URL already carries the base. The built directory is `--dist`, else `OUT_DIR` resolved by `outDirFrom` as Astro resolves it, else `site/dist`. `astro preview` is spawned from Astro's own binary in `site/` with `--ignore-lock` (as `size.mjs` does), in its own process group, with the environment passed through, after checking that the port is free; the script waits up to 60 s for a 200 at the base URL, refuses a server that reports another port, and kills the group in a `finally` block and on SIGINT and SIGTERM, together with the Lighthouse CI process group. Each route's status is read with a GET before Lighthouse runs: a status other than 200 (304 counts as 200) makes the route an `error`, except the route `/404` built from `404.html`, which may answer 404 by design and is then measured with Lighthouse's `ignoreStatusCode` (checked live on a copy of the build with a `404.html` added). What the specification left open: all plan, after `P0-A6` 5.1. Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: the instruction's 5.3 and 5.4; the index is the plan's addition to "the core path M0 to M5".

## 2026-10-10 P3-A7: the canary and the processor check
Decision: `CANARY_HTML`, exported, is served by a throwaway Node `http` server on a free port of 127.0.0.1 and measured once, first in every command, through Lighthouse CI with the same settings: a short first paint, a large block of text 3.5 s after load, a 600 ms busy loop, then a 320 pixel block inserted above the text; it fetches nothing. If it passes any threshold the script prints `lab: measurement check failed: the slow fixture passed <metric>` and exits 1 before any route. Live it read LCP about 3.6 s, TBT about 554 ms and CLS 0.373 in every command. The processor check on Branch A reads every report, the canary's included: `configSettings.throttlingMethod` must be `devtools`, `configSettings.throttling.cpuSlowdownMultiplier` 4 and the screen 360 by 800, otherwise `lab: the processor slowdown did not take effect (<setting found>)` and exit 1; the first line prints the method and multiplier in place of a ratio. What the specification left open: plan. Default used: none of SPEC.md section 10. Reason: zero TBT and CLS are legitimate, so a broken measurement would read as a perfect page, and a simulated run never slows the processor.
