## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the five presets and the default state
Decision: `default`: A3, coords numbers, start all ones, moves the lowest-node game (1, 2, 1, 3, 2, 1, computed by `play`), tab tally. `tally`: A3 from all ones, no moves, tab tally. `wave`: A3 from all ones, no moves, tab wave, colour class 0 first. `compared`: A3 from all ones with the lowest-node game, tab compared, game B preset to the highest-node game (3, 2, 3, 1, 2, 3, `play` with `choose` taking the last allowed position). `dimension`: A2 from all ones, no moves, `lambda` (1, 1), tab dimension. Every preset's state part also holds `coords`, `start` and `moves` (all ones and the moves above) so that it is a whole state object and a link. The registry (`T05.ts`) holds the state part and the caption; the options outside schema 1 (tab, first colour, game B) live in `t05/presets.ts`. What the specification left open: the presets' contents (4.0 Configuration, CONTRACTS 6). Default used: the plan's table (instruction 5.1). Reason: M4 row 2 and M5 rows 6 to 8 through properties alone.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: captions
Decision: the captions state the result in words and take their numbers from the engine (the compared caption's number of moves from `play`); the default caption says the numbers fired "are the heights of the positive coroots" (F6, SOURCES.md 11.5), never "heights of roots". No caption names a theorem number of [Ste59] (SOURCES.md 11.5: its item numbers were read through text recognition). Nothing T5 shows is [U], so no caption needs `<Unverified>`.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the board property overrides a preset's board
Decision: on a lesson page `board` replaces the preset's board and the preset's start and moves are recomputed for it: all ones, the lowest-node game where the preset has one, game B's highest-node game for `compared`; a preset `lambda` is kept only when its length fits the new board (otherwise zeros). So `preset="wave" board="E8"` and `preset="tally" board="E8"` work, and `preset="compared" board="B2"` has the moves 1, 2, 1, 2. When the author gives `start` without `moves`, the moves are empty. The caption without a `caption` property is the preset's, written for the board in force.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: properties
Decision: common properties `id`, `board`, `coords`, `start`, `moves`, `caption`, `preset`, `prediction`, `linkTo`, `layers`; T5's own `tab` ('tape' | 'tally' | 'wave' | 'compared' | 'dimension'), `lambda` (also a state field), `firstColour` (0 | 1), `secondMoves` (labels), `boardChoices` (limits the picker). `coords` other than numbers, a non-name `board`, or an invalid `lambda` throw at build time naming the instance. `linkTo` is accepted and ignored (T5 follows no tool). `layers` is accepted and ignored: 4.0 gives it to "Every tool that grows a set of positions (T2, T4)", and T5 grows none.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: legal play only, and the link rules
Decision: T5 plays only legal firings. The state written is exactly `{ schema, tool: 'numbers', board, coords: 'numbers', start, moves, lambda? }`. The codec checks a move against the mode in force only when the state carries a mode, and T5's state carries none, so `t05/link.ts` checks the decoded state itself with `firstForbiddenMove(board, { ...state, mode: 'legal' })`: a forbidden move loads the defaults with `LINK_NOTICE`. A link with `c=p`, or with `v` other than numbers or `m` other than legal, loads the defaults with the notice. A `d` with a negative entry or an entry of more than 6 digits keeps the rest of the link, loads the default `lambda` (the author's when the board is the author's, else zeros) and shows the notice. The tab, the first colour and game B's moves are never in a link (6.6). Note for links on the tool's own route: a link that names A3 without `w` keeps the default's six moves (the codec keeps an absent key), so a link to A3 from another start needs `w=`; links written by "Copy link" always carry `w`. What the specification left open: how the codec learns T5's mode. Default used: the tool checks (plan). Request to P1-A2 recorded in the report.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the start chooser
Decision: the chooser offers all ones, a fundamental weight (with a node picker, `omega_i = e_i`) and custom (one text field per node, an optional minus sign, ASCII or U+2212, and at most 40 digits; "Use this start" is enabled only when every field holds such an integer). The kind shown is derived from `start` (all ones; a unit vector; otherwise custom), never stored; choosing "custom" opens the fields until a start is applied. Changing the start resets the moves and game B and keeps `lambda`; a picker change resets start, moves, `lambda` (to zeros) and game B. A start that leaves the safe range is reloaded with `Big` arithmetic, with no message (SPEC 4 T1, read for T5), and an `EngineOverflow` during play switches to `Big` and recomputes every position.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: step, play to the end, the worker
Decision: step fires the lowest legal position (E-5); play to the end is one call of `play` with `CAPS.playMoves`, its word appended; the branch for `status: 'cap'` writes `still running after N moves` in `data-out="limit"`. Every play runs on the main thread and the component never imports `engineCall`: a game from an all-positive start has N <= 120 moves (F4), and the plan reading for a start with zeros or negative numbers is that each legal move lengthens the element of W reached (F5 for all-positive starts), so no legal game has more than N moves. The positions of the play are extended by one engine call per move (7.4).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the wave
Decision: the wave runs from all ones only. Its rounds come from one `wavePlay(board, rho, firstColour)`, split by the colour classes of `bipartition` (class 0 holds label 1). In the wave tab "Step" (labelled "Step one round") appends the moves of the next round and "Play to the end" the rest of the wave's word; both are disabled while the moves do not follow the wave from all ones, and then the panel says `The wave runs from all ones. Start it to fire one colour class per round.` with a button "Start the wave from all ones" (`data-control="startWave"`), which sets the start to all ones and empties the moves. This button is the confirmation the plan asks for before discarding moves (no dialog). A colour choice (`firstColour`, radio buttons, default 0) is shown on rank 2 and more. The round counter counts complete rounds made; beside it the Coxeter number from `coxeterNumber`. On the wave tab each node carries a mark over the board, a filled square for class 0 and an outlined triangle for class 1, and each class is listed in words.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the A1 sentence
Decision: `On A1 one colour class is empty: the wave is a single move.` (`data-out="waveNote"`); on A1 no round count and no colour choice are shown, and the step is announced `Fired node 1. The wave is a single move. Numbers now minus 1.`

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: two games compared
Decision: game B starts from game A's start with its own move list (outside the link), on a second board in legal play (accessible name `Game B on <board>`), with "Step B" and "Play B to the end" taking the highest legal position, and "Reset B". The overlay shows both tapes sorted numerically, cell by cell, and compares only when both games have ended: `the sorted tapes agree` (`data-out="tapesAgree"`, present only then) or `the sorted tapes differ`; otherwise `Game A is unfinished: play it to the end to compare.`, `Game B is unfinished: play it to the end to compare.` or `Both games are unfinished: play them to the end to compare.` (`data-out="compareStatus"`). The ends and the lengths of both games are shown (`endA`, `endB`, `movesA`, `movesB`). The tapes are never compared in move order. Game B's keys `u` and `r` do nothing (History belongs to game A).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: the dimension calculator, the lambda cap and the message
Decision: one text field per node; the field refuses a minus sign, anything but digits, and a seventh digit (the text returns to its last accepted value); at most 6 digits per entry, the same cap on a link. The word is the lowest-node game from rho; the tapes are computed in `Big` arithmetic (so no number on them can overflow), D and M are `bigint` products, and the quotient shown is `weylDimension(board, lambda)`, checked against M / D. Should the calculation ever fail the panel says `The dimension could not be computed for this lambda.` (`data-out="dimensionNote"`). The default `lambda` is zeros (dimension 1) unless a preset or a property sets it.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: long numbers
Decision: T1's formatter `t01/longNumber.ts` (`shortValue`, `isLong`), as P2-A2 reused it: `1.2389... x 10^106 (107 digits)` and `4.8010... x 10^109 (110 digits)` for the E8 products with lambda omega_1. Exact digits in `data-exact`, in the SVG `<title>` of each product (tooltip and export) and in the table view. No second formatter was written.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: MathML written inline
Decision: the two formulas T5 builds at run time are inline MathML in the component: `prod_i (m_i + 1) = 2 · 3 · 4 = 24` and `dim V(lambda) = M / D = 16 / 2 = 8` (the numeric fraction only when M has at most 12 digits). `LinearForm.svelte` (P3-A5, same stage) was not on the integration branch when this work started. Request to MERGE: move them to `LinearForm.svelte` if wanted. Plain lists of integers stay text.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: wordings of announcements and readouts
Decision: moves, refusals, undo, game over and a play to the end use the A-4 templates unchanged (through T1's `t01/announce.ts`). A move that ends the game appends `Game over after N moves.` and, from all ones, `Exponents 1, 2, 3; product 24; order of the group 24.` A wave round: `Fired nodes 1 and 3. Round 1. Numbers now minus 1, 3, minus 1.`; the whole wave: `Played the wave to the end: 4 rounds, 6 moves. Numbers now ...`. The dimension: `Dimension 8.` Game B: `Game B: fired node 3. Numbers now ... Move 1.`, `Game B: Played 6 moves to the end. ...`, then `Both games have ended. The sorted tapes agree.` A start: `Start all ones: 1, 1, 1.`, `Start the fundamental weight of node 2: 0, 1, 0.`, `Custom start: 2, minus 1, 3.`; a picker: `Board E8. Start all ones: ...`. The tally note: `The bars are read sideways when the game from all ones has ended.`

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: tabs and scroll boxes
Decision: the tabs are a WAI-ARIA tab list (role tab, aria-selected, roving tabindex, Left, Right, Home and End with automatic activation); only the current panel is rendered; the tape line, the tally reading and the colour classes of the current tab are on the page, and the "Show as table" view always lists the board, the tape, the tally with the exponents, the wave's rounds, both compared tapes and the dimension tapes with exact products. Scroll boxes (the tape line, the tape table, the tally chart, the sorted tapes, the dimension tapes, the formulas) are focusable groups with an accessible name (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`). Nothing in T5 is sticky. Tally bars and tapes use the neutral `--road` token, never a sign colour or a view accent (8.3); the bars are vertical so that the reading sideways is the rows of the chart, each row drawn as a dashed line with its count.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: names in the picker
Decision: the picker lists the 32 names as plain text in `<option>` elements grouped by letter (an option cannot hold markup), the name the reader chose kept (D19: C2 stays C2). `names.json` is not read (P2-A3 recorded the same choice for the same reason).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: browser fixtures for property-only configurations
Decision: `T05.spec.ts` bundles the component with Vite and serves a fixture page from a stub origin (as T09.spec.ts does) with three instances: `compared` on A3, `compared` on E8 (two stacked E8 boards at 320 pixels) and `tally` with a number prediction. So the prediction tests run in the browser in my own file; no page of this stage places T5 with a prediction. The fixture page links the site's `tokens.css` and `base.css`.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: hydration markup and the KaTeX stylesheet
Decision: the hydration test compares the tool's `outerHTML` with JavaScript off and after hydration, ignoring `disabled`, `data-hydrated` and `checked=""` (ToolFrame's export checkboxes: the server writes the attribute, the browser keeps a property). On `?g=A2&d=1,1` the static HTML is the default state (a link is applied by the island), which the test checks. The MathML test checks that no KaTeX script is fetched; the KaTeX stylesheet is linked by `Base.astro` on every page for the prose formulas and is not T5's request.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A3: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D8 (explanation only: no fire-negative switch in T5) and D19 (the picker shows the name chosen). No [U] item was settled; no dependency was added.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: environment and starting point
Decision: record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a238ec92b4d9531d6`, branch `worktree-agent-a238ec92b4d9531d6` (the plan's `agent/P3-A4`), fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` from `5f439e7` to `22b0abd` before any file was written; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. Playwright ran from a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 4350 with the prepared `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`; nothing was downloaded. No dependency was added.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: D19, B2 and C2 under their own names
Decision: the picker offers `A2`, `B2`, `C2`, `G2` (registry `boards`), each built with `boardFromName` (never `identify`) and drawn in its own numbering: on `B2` node 1 is long (|alpha_1| = 2, |alpha_2| = sqrt 2), on `C2` node 1 is short. `data-out="boardName"` carries the chosen name in `data-name`. Default used: SPEC 10 D19. Reason: SPEC 4 T6, 2.8.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: D7, a = Cp and the bridge sentence
Decision: the numbers are a = Cp ("the drop", D7, 2.4(e)); the line a = Cp is shown with the actual numbers, and the first sentence of 2.4(a) is printed verbatim beside it (`data-out="bridge"`, with i in italics, plain markup, in the server HTML). Default used: SPEC 10 D7.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: the reading of "the chambers"
Decision: the chambers are the |W| sectors (6, 8, 8, 12) cut out by the N mirror lines, one perpendicular to U q for each positive root q. All N lines are drawn (`data-out="mirror"`, `data-mirror` = 1-based index in `positiveRoots` order); the two simple mirrors are solid, thicker and labelled "mirror 1" and "mirror 2" (`data-simple`), the others dashed and thin. The dominant chamber (every simple root pairs positively with its bisector, 2.6) is filled with the neutral token at low opacity and labelled "dominant" in words (`data-dominant="true"`, on that chamber only). What the specification left open: what "the chambers" means in the drawing. Plan reading, flagged to the orchestrator.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: drawing conventions, the screen flip and the window
Decision: `choleskyBasis(board)` once per board; COLUMN j of U is the picture of alpha_(j+1). A root population q is drawn at U q, a position a at U C^{-1} a (`toPopulation`). Screen: an SVG box of 400 by 400 user units, the window [-R, R] squared filling it, screen y = -y so that alpha_2 is above the axis and the angle reads counterclockwise. R = 2 times the larger of the longest root and |U C^{-1}(1,1)|, rounded up to a half unit (A2: 3, B2 and C2: 4.5, G2: 7.5). Every coordinate in markup has 3 decimals, with -0.000 written 0.000. A point outside the window is not drawn; `data-out="offView"` says so in words. What the specification left open: the window and the scale. Plan.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: the snapping rule
Decision: on release of a drag, the real numbers a = C U^{-1} y of the pointer position are rounded and the 3 by 3 neighbourhood of the rounded pair is searched for the nearest weight-lattice point (U C^{-1} a'); ties (within 1e-12) go to the smaller a_1, then the smaller a_2. Pointer capture, release on pointerup, pointercancel and lostpointercapture; `touch-action: none` on the point's handle only (a 22-unit transparent circle), so the page scrolls elsewhere. Plan (5.6).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: placement by keys and buttons; placement sets the start
Decision: four visible buttons "a1 − 1", "a1 + 1", "a2 − 1", "a2 + 1" (`data-control` `a1-minus`, `a1-plus`, `a2-minus`, `a2-plus`; accessible names "Move the point: number at node 1 minus 1", and so on; 44 pixels high). The plane is one tab stop (`data-control="plane"`, an `<svg role="img">` whose `aria-label` is the text alternative); while it has focus, Right and Left change a_1 by plus and minus 1, Up and Down change a_2. Every placement path (keys, buttons, drag) sets `coords: 'numbers'`, `start` to the new numbers and `moves: []`; placement is not a move. Tab order: the plane and the placement buttons, then the node buttons and History, all inside ToolFrame's figure; the picker and the overlay switches come after, in ToolFrame's controls row (ToolFrame fixes that order; the plan's order put the picker first). Plan.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: announcements
Decision: a firing uses the A-4 `fired` template unchanged (`Fired node 1. Numbers now minus 1, 2. Move 1.` on the A2 default); undo and a scrub backwards use `undid` (`Undid move 3. Numbers now ...`), a scrub forwards `fired` with that move's number; reset `undid(1, ...)`. Composed sentences (A-4 has none): placement `Point placed at numbers 2, 0.`; picker `Board G2. Point placed at numbers 1, 1.`; overlays `All roots shown.` / `All roots hidden.`, `Orbit of the point shown.`, `Root lattice and weight lattice shown.`. Fractions are spoken "2 over 3". All go through ToolFrame's announcer (coalesced within half a second).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: links with c=p
Decision: a link with `c=p` gives an integer population p; T6 places the point at a = Cp (`toNumbers`, always integral) and writes the state back with `coords: 'numbers'`; moves are kept (mutating city i and firing node i correspond under a = Cp, 2.4(a)). `view` and `mode` in a link are dropped (T6 uses neither). No key was added to the codec.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: the reset rule
Decision: reset empties the move list and returns to the current start, the last placement, not to the preset's start. Plan (5.8).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: data-out and data-control names
Decision: data-out: `boardName`, `numbers`, `population`, `equation`, `bridge`, `point` (data-x, data-y), `distance` (data-node, data-value), `parallelogram`, `simpleRoot` (data-node), `mirror` (data-mirror, data-simple), `chamber` (data-dominant), `root` (data-value, data-marked), `orbitPoint` (data-value), `latticeDot` (data-lattice, data-x, data-y), `offView`, plus those of ToolFrame, History and Predict. data-control: `picker`, `plane`, `a1-minus`, `a1-plus`, `a2-minus`, `a2-plus`, `overlay-roots`, `overlay-orbit`, `overlay-lattices`, plus ToolFrame's and History's. The plan's names, plus `distance` and `parallelogram` (additions). Listed in the registry's `outs` and at the head of `site/tests/e2e/T06.spec.ts`.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: the overlays property and boardChoices
Decision: property `overlays: ('roots' | 'orbit' | 'lattices')[]`, default `[]`, sets the initial state of the three switches; the switches are not part of a link (6.6, schema 1). Property `boardChoices: string[]` limits the picker (default the four boards). Root-lattice dots are small filled discs, weight-lattice dots open diamonds, so on G2 every dot visibly carries both markers. Roots are drawn as arrows from the origin; a root equal to the page's `highlight` (kind `root`, this board, value in label order) is drawn thicker in the focus colour and carries `data-marked`. T6 does not set the highlight (optional in the plan).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: SafeInt overflow (5.8)
Decision: positions are kept as bigint; each engine call uses the SafeInt board while every entry is safe and its `bigBoard` copy otherwise (and retries with Big on `EngineOverflow`), so a 40-digit link replays exactly and never crashes the island. `choleskyBasis` is always called on the SafeInt board (the floats of the drawing do not depend on the arithmetic). Plan reading by analogy with SPEC 4 T1.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: colours of the figure (flag)
Decision: the Numbers panel (node buttons and the numbers) is bordered in `--accent-numbers`, the Population panel in `--accent-populations` (8.3: borders of panels). The perpendiculars and the parallelogram themselves are stroked in `--text` (solid and dotted), not in the accents, because 8.3 names only borders and icons and the orchestrator has not confirmed the stroke reading of 6.3. Every colour comes from the tokens; no hex value in the component.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: the line a = Cp in plain markup (deviation from A-12)
Decision: the line is built as 7.3 prescribes for mathematics produced while a tool runs: C as an HTML table with a caption and row and column headers, the population entries through `Frac.svelte`, the numbers as a vector of spans, the whole line in words as the `aria-label` of its `role="group"`. This is table markup, not MathML, so it does not meet the letter of A-12; deviation flagged. If P3-A5 reports that native MathML renders in all three engines, the line can move to MathML later.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: board names in plain markup, not names.json (deviation)
Decision: the board name is set as the letter with the rank in `<sub>` (`data-name` holds `A2`), and the picker's `<option>`s are plain text. names.json is not imported. Reason: the precedent of P1-A6 and P2-A3 (MERGE accepted): an `<option>` cannot hold KaTeX HTML, and names.json would ship all 32 entries and need KaTeX's stylesheet for four names.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: motion
Decision: the point is moved with a CSS transform with a 150 ms transition, replaced by an instant update under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (A-9); nothing moves on load.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: default caption of the preset
Decision: `default` = `{ board: 'A2', coords: 'numbers', start: [1, 1], moves: [] }`, overlays off, caption "Firing a node reflects the point in its mirror. The numbers are the scaled distances to the two mirrors, the population is the pair of coefficients along the two normals, and the two stay linked by a = Cp." No preset id was added.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A4: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D7 and D19 only (above). No [U] item was settled; nothing T6 shows rests on one.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: versions observed and the branch
Decision: built and tested against node v22.22.0, Astro 7.3.8, Svelte 5.57.2, TypeScript 6.0.3, Vitest 5.0.3 and Playwright 1.64.0, as installed by `npm ci --prefer-offline` from the committed lockfile. The worktree branch `worktree-agent-af9ea28dda41e0587` (the plan's `agent/P3-A5`) was fast-forwarded with `git merge --ff-only claude/session-6a181f60-hzzoud` from `5f439e7` to `22b0abd` (stage 3.1 merged) before any file was written; `npm test -w packages/engine` was green there. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: COMMON.md 3 and 4.1.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the MathML [U] of SPEC 7.3, settled for Chromium only on this machine
Decision: LinearForm renders MathML (`RENDER = 'mathml'`). `site/tests/e2e/mathml.spec.ts` ran here on Chromium: the launcher of Playwright 1.64.0 reported Chromium 141.0.7390.37 (the headless shell installed under PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH; `browsers.json` of playwright-core lists chromium revision 1248), and the test passed: the server-rendered forms x1, x2, x3 and the client-created form 2x1 + x2 are `<math>` elements in the MathML namespace with an `aria-label` and a box of non-zero size, and every subscript is set smaller than its base and lower than it. WebKit (playwright-core lists 27.2, revision 2370) and Firefox (157.0, revision 1555) are not installed in this cloud container and cannot be installed here, so their two tests failed at `launch()` with Playwright's "please run npx playwright install" message: not run, not skipped. The owner runs, on the Mac: `npx playwright install webkit firefox` then `npm run build` and `npx playwright test site/tests/e2e/mathml.spec.ts`. If either engine fails there, set `RENDER` in `site/src/components/math/LinearForm.svelte` to `'spans'` (the span path is implemented and unit-tested; the spec then reads the `<sub>` of each term) and record the engine and its version here. What the specification left open: [U] in 7.3 ("MathML Core in Chromium since version 109, from memory; check in phase 3"). Default used: the instruction's (5.9). Reason: Appendix E item 17, the builder settles a tool's [U] when the tool is first used; the item stays partly open (two engines) until the owner's run.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: how mathml.spec.ts launches the three engines (deviation from the roster)
Decision: the spec launches each browser itself with `chromium`, `webkit` and `firefox` as exported by `@playwright/test` (re-exported from playwright-core), one test per engine, each against the configured `baseURL`; the repository's single Chromium project runs the file. The roster says "Playwright projects added for this spec only", but `playwright.config.ts` belongs to P0-A6, then MERGE and GATE. Requested from MERGE: install Firefox and WebKit in `ci.yml` (`npx playwright install --with-deps chromium webkit firefox`), and, if MERGE prefers projects, three projects `mathml-chromium`, `mathml-webkit`, `mathml-firefox` with `testMatch: /mathml\.spec\.ts/` and `testIgnore` of that file in the main project (the spec would then use the project's browser instead of launching its own). Reason: COMMON 4.2.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: LinearForm rendering and spoken rules
Decision: terms in increasing label order; zero terms omitted; a coefficient 1 is not written, -1 is written as a minus sign; other coefficients as `<mn>`; between terms `<mo>+</mo>` or `<mo>−</mo>` (U+2212), a leading negative term with `<mo>−</mo>`; each variable `<msub><mi>x</mi><mn>label</mn></msub>`; the zero form `<mn>0</mn>`; no invisible times. The `aria-label` speaks coefficients through `speak` of a11y.ts, variables as "x 1", operators "plus" and "minus": "2 x 1 plus x 2", "minus x 1 minus 2 x 3", "0". Props: `coeffs` (by position, number or bigint), `labels` (board.labels, the subscripts, E-7), `letter` (default x), `render` (default the module constant `RENDER`, so that tests can render both paths). The span fallback: the same terms in `<span>` with `<i>` and `<sub>`, `role="math"` and the same `aria-label`. What the specification left open: the plan's rules (5.5). Reason: they reproduce the forms of A.1 and M5 beat 5. Svelte 5.57.2 creates `<math>` and its children in the MathML namespace on the client (checked by mathml.spec.ts after a move); no `<svelte:options namespace>` was needed.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: default board, caption and state
Decision: default board A3 (M5 beat 5), start all ones, no moves; the only preset is `default`. Caption: "Play the numbers game on the left. The right board makes the same moves with letters in place of numbers, so every number fired is a linear form. Over a complete game each positive root of the reversed board is fired exactly once." The state is `{ schema: 1, tool: 'symbolic', board, coords: 'numbers', start, moves }`; view and mode are fixed by the tool and never written into links (`?z=1&g=B2&c=n&s=1,1&w=1.2.1.2`). What the specification left open: the plan's (5.2). Reason: 4.0 "default is the state described in this section"; CONTRACTS 6.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: links T7 refuses beyond 6.6
Decision: besides the codec's rules (decodeState with the instance prefix), a link loads the default with the codec's notice when: `c=p` (coords other than numbers); a start with an entry that is not a positive integer (T7 needs "a start with all numbers positive"; F6 fills the table only then); a move that legal play forbids at its point of the play (6.6 rule 6 for the mode in force: the tool's mode is legal, but since links carry no `m`, the codec cannot see it, so T7 replays with `firstForbiddenMove` and mode legal itself). The same rules make an authoring error (a page passing such a start or moves) fail the build with a message naming the instance, as T1 does. What the specification left open: plan reading of 5.2. Reason: the left board must always be a legal play from an all-positive start.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the left board is T1's model; Big with no message
Decision: the left board is `T1Game` of `t01/model.ts` (numbers view, legal mode, numbers coordinates), imported and not changed: one engine call per move, step and play to the end lowest position first (E-5), and the silent switch to Big when a start or a play leaves the safe range (SPEC 4 T1), which also gives the long-number display of `Node.svelte` and `t01/longNumber.ts` (shortened on the node, exact in the tooltip, the table view and the export). The announcements are T1's (`t01/announce.ts`) in the numbers view. Every board T7 offers is finite with at most 120 moves, so play to the end runs on the main thread (E-9); the full recomputation after a move (symbolicPlay, n unit-start applyWord calls, positiveRoots of the transposed board) took a median of 3.2 ms on E8 along its 120-move longest word in Vitest here, against the plan's 25 ms bound. Reason: the instruction's "reuse T1's mechanism; do not write a second one".

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the right board is a T07 sub-component, with its own hooks
Decision: `Board.svelte` draws a numeral on each node and validates its value as a decimal (sign.ts throws on "2,1"), so the right board is `T07SymbolicBoard.svelte` (README 6: T07*.svelte), built from the board family's parts (draw/layouts.ts, the size rule of 6.1 in Board's CSS, Roads.svelte, keyboard.ts's arrow model). Each disc shows the sign of its entry by fill and a + or − glyph (6.3, A-5); the forms themselves are listed under the drawing, one line per node with LinearForm, so that a long E8 form never covers a neighbour. The node buttons are one tab stop with arrows, Home and End (A-1), `aria-disabled="true"`, `data-can-act="false"`; activating one changes nothing. Hooks: the left board is wrapped in `data-out="numbersBoard"`, the right in `data-out="symbolicBoard"`; the right board's buttons carry `data-board="numbers"` and `data-value` equal to the coefficients in label order joined by commas (for example `2,1`), plus `data-sign` (positive, negative, zero or mixed); each line of the list carries `data-entry` and `data-form`. Tests scope every node query by the wrapper. A request to P0-A5 through MERGE: a `content` snippet prop on Board.svelte would let a later tool draw non-numeric entries without a sub-component. What the specification left open: 4.0 names only `populations` and `numbers` for `data-board` and decimal or `p/q` for `data-value`; raised under openQuestions.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: A-3 wording on the right board
Decision: "Node 2, form x 1 plus x 2" (the LinearForm spoken text), with no "can be fired" state, since the right board does not act. The left board keeps the templates of A-3 ("Node 3, value 2, can be fired"). Reason: plan (5.6).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the stacking width and the layout boxes
Decision: the two boards stand side by side when the tool's figure (its inline-size container) is at least 600 pixels wide and are stacked, left board first, below; each board's panel is its own size container, so the size rule of 6.1 measures the panel. The fired list keeps a fixed height of 8em and scrolls inside its box; the side table renders all N rows at build time and scrolls inside a box of at most 24em; the figure's grid column is `minmax(0, 1fr)` so that a long form never widens the page at 320 pixels (A-11). The three scroll boxes (the fired list, the side table and the list of current forms) are keyboard stops (`tabindex="0"`, the first two as named regions), so that their content can be scrolled without a pointer; axe's `scrollable-region-focusable` flagged the side table before this. Reason: 4.0 Small screens and 7.6 (no layout shift on hydration; measured equal boxes and CLS within 0.1 in T07.spec.ts).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the side table and the fired list
Decision: the table's caption is "Positive roots of the reversed board" (the reversed board is not named, which avoids D19 questions); one row per root of `positiveRoots(transposeBoard(board))` in the engine's order, shown as a population in label order; an unused row reads "not yet fired"; a used row shows "move k", the form (LinearForm) and the number fired on the left at that move, read from the left board's play. Hooks: `data-out="rootTable"`; each row `data-out="rootRow"` with `data-root`, `data-used`, and `data-move` when used; the list `data-out="firedForms"` with items carrying `data-move` and `data-form`. Rows are matched by equal coefficient vectors (positions order equals label order on the 32 types). "Show as table" (A-10, `data-out="valuesTable"`) has one row per node with the label, the left number (exact digits), the right form and a "Fire node k" button, disabled when the number is not positive.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: the T1 colour-class panel
Decision: key `'colours'` in T1's `panels` (the key P1-A5 declared). Wording: "First class: nodes 1, 3; second class: node 2." ("node" for one label, "no node" for none), each class preceded by a glyph (a disc ● for the first, a square ■ for the second, `aria-hidden`) coloured `--edge-3` and `--edge-4`; node fill is untouched (it carries the sign, 6.3). On an odd cycle: "No two colour classes: the board has a cycle of odd length." Hooks: `data-out="colourClasses"` with `data-class-1` and `data-class-2` (labels joined by commas) or `data-none="true"`. The first class is bipartition's class 0, which holds the lowest position of each component. The edit of T01GameBoard.svelte is additive: a `<script module>` block with `colourClasses` and `classText`, one derived value, the panel's markup after the phase-2 panels and two style rules. The panel deliberately carries no `data-panel` attribute: P1-A5's unit test "no colours panel before phase 3 (MERGE, P2 stage 2.2)" asserts that no `data-panel="colours"` is rendered; that test is now obsolete and is reported to MERGE (not edited). Board.svelte's `decoration` prop is not read by Board (types.ts: "Board does not read it yet"), so no marker is drawn on the nodes; a request to P0-A5 through MERGE asks for a per-node marker. The browser test of the panel bundles T1 with `panels: ['colours']` (T07.spec.ts > `T1 colour-class panel`), since no route sets the property yet; once M5 places it, a lesson test can read the same hooks.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A5: golden values in the Playwright file
Decision: T07.spec.ts reads reference/golden_data.json with `node:fs` and `JSON.parse` (golden.ts was not imported into the Playwright process). The only vectors typed are those of Appendix A.1 and the first two A3 forms that M5 beat 5 quotes. Reason: COMMON 4.4.

Decision: D19 for the picker names (the board the reader chose, B2 or C2, stays as chosen; the table does not name the reversed board). No other default was needed.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: environment and starting point
Decision: none; record. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/agent-a69a500fb3cf91332`, branch `worktree-agent-a69a500fb3cf91332` (the plan's `agent/P3-A6`), fast-forwarded to the integration head `22b0abd` before any file was written; `npm ci --prefer-offline` once; Node v22.22.0. The engine suite was green (53 files, 1943 tests), `legalLayers(A3)` and `countCompleteGames(A3)` gave the expected values, and `npm run build` passed. No dependency added.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: boards offered
Decision: `offeredBoards()` (t08/graph.ts) is the 32 named types filtered by `weylOrder <= CAPS.weylExplorerGroup`, in picker order; the registry carries the same fourteen names as a literal, and `T08.test.ts` proves literal, function and golden file agree. The picker lists them grouped by letter as plain text in a `<select>` (an `<option>` cannot hold the typeset names of `names.json`; P1-A6 made the same choice), `B2` and `C2` both listed and each drawn in its own numbering (D19). What the specification left open: the order and the markup of T8's own list (4.0 "T6, T8, T11 and T15 list their own boards"). Default used: D19. Reason: instruction 5.1.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: presets, options and what a copied link restores
Decision: `default`: A3, numbers, start all ones, no moves, graph first. `counter`: the same state, counter first, `boardChoices: ['A2', 'A3', 'B3', 'D4']` (M5 row 3). `heap`: A3 with moves 1, 3, 2, heap view first, `plays: [[1, 3, 2], [3, 1, 2]]` offered as two `loadPlay` buttons that load the play with `pushState` (history.ts `exampleLoaded`). The state of every preset is `{ schema: 1, tool: 'weyl', board, view: 'numbers', mode: 'legal', coords: 'numbers', start: all ones, moves }` (view and mode set so that the codec applies 6.6 rule 6 to a link's moves; `defaultState('weyl', ...)` gives no mode, so T8 sets its own). The options (`panel`, `boardChoices`, `plays`) live in t08/presets.ts and are never written in a link: a link copied from the `counter` placement restores the board and moves only; opened on that page the placement keeps its options, opened at `/tools/weyl` it shows the default presentation (full picker, graph first, no play buttons). Tested in `a preset's link carries its state and none of its options`. The panels are never hidden: `panel` only puts one first, so nothing essential is behind a click and the page reads without JavaScript; the default preset therefore shows the counter and the heap view below the graph, open, rather than collapsed. What the specification left open: the option names and values (4.0 Configuration). Default used: the instruction's table. Reason: instruction 5.1.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: boardChoices against the 4.0 rule (deviation reported)
Decision: `boardChoices` is kept as a presentation option outside the state, although a reader cannot limit the picker by hand, because M5 row 3 names exactly four boards. It is marked as such in the comment block of `T08.spec.ts` and reported under `deviations` so that MERGE decides between keeping it and showing the full picker with the four boards named in the caption. The `counter` preset follows figure row 3 (A2, A3, B3, D4) while beat 3 also names A4 (768): A4 is offered by T8 and accepted in `boardChoices` when a page passes it (unit test: the picker lists it and the count equals the golden value); the choice between row and beat is left to P3-A9 or MERGE. Reason: instruction 5.1.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: refusal of a start other than all ones
Decision: after `decodeState`, `admitLink` (t08/link.ts) refuses a link whose coords are not the numbers, whose start is not all ones, whose view or mode is another, or whose moves are not a legal game from all ones; T8 then loads its default state and shows the codec's own sentence (`LINK_NOTICE`). A board T8 does not offer is refused by the codec (rule 8, from the registry's `boards`). The codec has no hook for a tool's own validation; none was requested because this reading after `decodeState` gives the same result. Properties: `coords` other than numbers or a `start` other than all ones throw an authoring error naming the instance. Reason: instruction 5.2; trap "start other than all ones has no place in the graph".

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: the graph, its edges and the stated limits
Decision: one `orbitGraph(board, [rho], 'firing', cap, undefined, true)` per board, cap `CAPS.weylExplorerGroup` (F4 completes with exactly 1152 items, tested), vertices keyed by the entries joined with commas (`arith.show`, natively for SafeInt and Big), layer = depth. Edges: every legal move applied once more to every item, drawn once, downward; `|W| n / 2` edges (tested). `buildGraph(board, cap?)` takes the cap so a test can force a cut: then only layers 0 to `completeLayers` are drawn and counted, the sentence of SPEC 5.5 "stopped after N positions; layers 0 to L are complete" (N the number of items, L `completeLayers`) is shown in `data-out="limit"` and in the figure's text alternative, and no path count is given. A `null` count reads "count not offered". T8 passes no `maxDepth` and takes no `layers` bound: 4.0 "Growth off the finite types" names T2 and T4, and every board T8 offers is finite, so "stopped after L layers" never arises; the `layers` property is accepted and ignored. T8 never writes "not of finite type" (tested over the component's source, the captions and the sentences). Reason: instruction 5.3.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: the edge rule above 192 elements and the canvas
Decision: at most 192 elements (D4 included, the boundary is strict) every edge is drawn, coloured by node through `var(--edge-k)` and carrying the node label as text; above 192 (A5, B4, C4, F4) only the edges of the current game and the edges at the focused vertex, both directions (into it from the layer above and out of it to the layer below), are drawn. `data-out="edgeMode"` reads `all` or `game-and-focus`. Above 192 the figure is a Canvas 2D drawing after hydration; the server render (and the no-JavaScript reader) gets the SVG snapshot that `svgScene.ts` writes from the same `Scene` (layout.ts) in Node, `data-out="snapshot"`, `role="img"` and an aria-label naming the board, the positions and the layers. After hydration the snapshot stays in place, hidden under the canvas once it is drawn, and is rewritten one task after each draw: it is the mirror of the drawn edges (`data-out="edge"`) and what ToolFrame's SVG and PNG export take (ToolFrame exports the figure's visible SVGs; a canvas cannot be exported, see requests). The canvas is wrapped in an element with `role="img"` and the same label (Svelte refuses `role="img"` on a canvas). Reason: instruction 5.7; 7.1; 7.4.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: no long task on F4
Decision: the graph, layout and counts are cached per board (t08/cache.ts); a pick or a link that names a board not yet prepared computes them in four tasks (board and verdict, graph, layout, counts) before the state changes, the canvas is drawn in a task of its own after the DOM update (dots stamped from one small sprite instead of a thousand arcs), the colour tokens are read once per theme, and the snapshot SVG is rewritten in a later task. Measured in Chromium headless: no long task from the pick of F4 to the draw (the first version, all in one task, made one of 96 ms). Reason: 7.6; roster acceptance.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: geometry, hit areas, keyboard model and names
Decision: layout from `layeredLayout` (rank = number of moves). Hit areas are squares of 44 pixels when the widest layer has at most 6 vertices (A1, A2, A3, B2, C2, G2: 6 x 48 + 32 = 320), otherwise 24, with columns 4 pixels wider than the hit area and rows of 72 pixels (all edges) or 44 (above 192), so no two hit areas overlap; the box has a fixed height (the drawing's, at most 480 pixels) and scrolls in both directions (A-11). Keyboard (plan reading of A-1): at most 192 elements the vertices are real buttons in one group with one tab stop (roving tabindex, the current vertex first); above 192 one focus button outside the canvas moves to the focused vertex. Left and Right move within a layer in drawn order, Up and Down to the vertex of the previous or next layer nearest in x, Home and End to the top and the bottom vertex, Enter or Space jumps; the focused vertex is scrolled into view. Hover on the canvas is a hit test within the hit area. Accessible name: `Position 2, minus 3, 2, layer 3`, with `, on the game` for a vertex of the path. The details panel shows the vertex pointed at, else the current position, so it is never empty and holds the information itself (P2). Reason: instruction 5.10.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: the jump, announcements, highlight
Decision: activating a vertex (click, Enter, Space, or a table row's jump button) sets the moves to its shortest word from `wordTo` in labels; with `linkTo` the word is published to the Game Board with `publishToolState`. Moves on T8's board use the A-4 templates through T1's announce.ts unchanged (a move that ends the game adds `Game over after N moves.`; play to the end is one `Played N moves to the end. ...` sentence). Sentences without a template (plan): `Jumped to position 2, minus 3, 2 by 3 moves.`, `Loaded the play 1, 3, 2. Plays with this stack: 2.`, `Board B3: 48 positions in 10 layers.` (numbers from the engine). Hover or focus of a vertex sets the highlight to `{ kind: 'position', board, value }` (label order), hover of a separating root to `{ kind: 'root', ... }`, cleared on leave; a position highlighted by another tool of the page marks its vertex (`data-highlighted`). Reason: instruction 5.4, 5.10.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: heap columns
Decision: one column per node, labels increasing from left to right; each brick shows its label and the node's edge colour as its outline; levels from the engine's `heap` only; equal stacks compared by `heapKey`; the count from `commutationClassSize` (bigint, printed with `toString`). Reason: instruction 5.6.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: the table view above 192 elements
Decision: the A-10 table lists every vertex (layer, position, shortest play, number of separating roots, a jump button) up to 192 elements; above 192 it lists the vertices of the current game and the focused vertex, with the layer sizes, the counts and the heap, because 1152 rows each with a `separatingRoots` call would make a long task at every change. What the specification left open: the rows of T8's table. Reason: 7.6.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: linkTo
Decision: T8 follows the Game Board named by `linkTo` with state.ts's `followToolState` (P1-A2's mechanism, which T1 publishes to on every change and follows for jumps; no second mechanism). It shows no board, no step and no play control of its own; it draws the path only when the followed state is a legal game from all ones in the numbers on a board T8 offers (`followedGame`), and otherwise draws no path and says "The linked board's position is not in this graph: the graph holds the positions reached from all ones by legal play." in `data-out="linkStatus"`. No page or fixture route on the integration branch places a Game Board and a T8 linked, so the browser item `A3 with a linked T1 highlights the path` is reported as a deviation; the Vitest test `a linked T1's state drives the path` drives the link module directly (no DOM needed: nanostores in Node). Reason: instruction 5.8.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: the server-render test and the F4 route
Decision: `T08.test.ts` renders the component with `svelte/server` through its own Vite server (the Svelte plugin and the aliases of CONTRACTS 1, as T02.test.ts and T09.test.ts do). The F4, A5, B4 and C4 renders hold the snapshot with every dot and no canvas; A3 and D4 hold the full SVG graph. There is no F4 route and no fixture route on the integration branch, so the no-JavaScript check of F4 is this unit test (request to MERGE below). Reason: instruction 5.14.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: worker
Decision: every call of T8 runs on the main thread: the search is capped at 1152 (E-9 rule 1, a cap of at most 2000), the counts are on groups of at most 1152 elements (rule 3), and a play has at most 24 moves (rule 2); a unit test checks `whereToRun` returns `main` for the hints of all fourteen boards. T8 imports nothing from lib/worker.ts. Reason: E-9; 7.6.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A6: defaults relied on
Decision: D19 (the name the reader chose: B2 and C2 both offered, each in its own numbering). No [U] item touched; T8 states no theorem and names no theorem number.

The stage check on f7699f5: 2489 unit tests, type checks, build, 481 browser tests passed with those two engines failing at launch before the fix; 25 routes within budget.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: the figures of the table made concrete
Decision: twelve tool instances in reading order. b1 T1 `A2`, numbers, legal, a = (1, 1), `client:idle` (row 1). b2 T1 `A3`, numbers, legal, a = (1, 1, 1), with a choice prediction (row 2). b3 T5 `preset="compared"` (row 2; the preset fixes A3, all ones, game A the lowest-node game and game B the highest-node game, both played at build time, so the sorted tapes and `tapesAgree` are in the static HTML). b4 T1 `A3`, linked, free, p = (1, 1, 0) (row 3). b5 T6 `preset="default"` (row 4). b6 T1 `A2`, linked, a = (1, 0), with a number prediction (row 5). b7 T2 `preset="follow"`, `linkTo="b6"`, `board="A2"`, `coords="numbers"`, `start={[1, 0]}`, `grows="orbit"` (row 5; the property exists, so it is passed explicitly). b8 T15 `preset="pentagon"` with a yes or no prediction (row 6). b9 T15 `preset="three-fates"` (row 7; the preset's default board is the pentagon with the example start). ## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: the mode of row 5
Decision: `mode="free"` on b6. What the specification left open: row 5 names no mode. Reason: free play lets the reader visit all three positions of the orbit in any order; legal play from (1, 0) would stop after one move. The `linked` view is required, because the `populations` view with `coords: numbers` falls back when the population is fractional (SPEC 4 T1).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: the prediction options of row 2, and no script in beat 3
Decision: b2's choice prediction has the options "Nothing: the same end after the same number of moves", "The same end, after a different number of moves", "A different end"; correct 0 (the plan's three options, reworded as answers to "what will a different order of moves change?"). The reveal states the length through `fmt('types.A3.n_positive_roots')` and the end (−1, −1, −1). Beat 3 has no `script`: the prose asks the reader to make three moves on either board and check the drop after each (the plan made the script optional; a script caption would have had to name a node and a city together, which P8 forbids).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: the sandbox board
Decision: `<ModuleLinks module={4} board="A3" />`, the board of beats 2 and 3 (plan). M5 and the Atlas are dropped by the build while unreleased; neither is linked in prose.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: predictions added (8.5)
Decision: three predictions. (1) b2, "Predict a yes or no" in the form of a choice (SPEC 3 M4 beat 2's own prediction). (2) b6, "Predict a number": "From the position (1, 0) on A2, firing any node in any order, how many different positions can you reach, the start included?", answer `types.A2.fundamental_orbit_sizes[0]` read with `resolveGoldenPath`. (3) b8, "Predict a yes or no": "Will the pentagon game stop, whatever order you fire in?", options Yes and No, the correct index computed from `pentagon_example` (complete games and moves are positive) rather than typed. Each freezes its board until answered or "show me"; without JavaScript each is followed by a `<details>` (Predict.svelte).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: challenges added (8.5) and their checks
Decision: three challenges, each a `Challenge` watching the tool below it. (1) b10, "Reach a target": on A3 in legal play from (1, 1, 1), reach (3, −1, −1), which the lowest-node game never passes, so meeting it proves a different order of play; check `{ kind: 'reach', board: 'numbers', values: [3, -1, -1] }`. (2) b11, "Reach a target" on the Kaleidoscope (`boardChoices={['A2']}`): move the point to the population (1, 0); check `{ kind: 'reach', board: 'numbers', values: [2, -1] }`, the numbers of that population (a = Cp is a bijection, so the check is the goal). (3) b12, "Reach a target" on the pentagon: a start that loops, with play on; check `{ kind: 'out', name: 'firstVisit' }`, which T15 renders only for a loop report. Deviation from the plan's challenge 2 ("find a position other than (0, 0) whose population is whole"): no check kind of `challengeCheck.ts` can test integrality of the populations board, and a T1 in a lesson has no start editor, so the goal was made a specific target on T6 (request below). The optional "still running" challenge was left out to keep the page light.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: Unverified items and fallbacks
Decision: two wraps, no new item. E2 (beat 6): "Mozes introduced his game in 1990 as a generalisation of this puzzle, firing negative numbers." Fallback: "Later authors, Donnelly among them, describe the game Mozes published in 1990 as a generalisation of this puzzle." Mozes' sign convention appears nowhere else on the page: beat 1's convention sentence names Eriksson, the Olympiad problem and Gashi, Schedler and Speyer as firing negative numbers and does not name Mozes. The bare credit "The numbers game is Mozes'" (8.4) and "attributed to Mozes and Eriksson" (F3, [S2], cited) are not wrapped. E6 (beat 7): "The same table holds on the affine boards with multiple roads, which arrive in module six." Fallback: "On boards with multiple roads the same table was computed on every affine board the site offers, and the Affine Playground gives an argument for it beside it." The release build printed `open item E2 used on /modules/m4-numbers/ (fallback shown)` and the same for E6 (E6 is also registered by T15Route). The Olympiad's proposer is not named (E11); Proctor's restricted game is not mentioned (E17); Kac's Tits cone is not cited (E4).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: provenance and credit
Decision: the Seen box has six statements with provenance [W03c] section two (the rules; first published definition, which credits the numbers game to Mozes), [Eri95b] Definition 1.1 and [Don08] Theorem 2.1 (F3, attributed to Mozes and Eriksson), "verified by computation for every board of finite type of rank at most eight" (F4), "verified by computation on every board of finite type of rank at most eight" (CS_i = R_i C, the bridge sentence, the det C classes and det C = 1 on E8, F4, G2), and "verified by computation on every affine board the site offers" with [GSS12] Theorem 1.2, signs reversed (the level and the fate table on boards with single roads). Credit (8.4): "The numbers game is Mozes' [Moz90], with Proctor's earlier use of the same process [Pro84] and Eriksson's development of it [Eri95b]". [W-MS] is cited for Proposition 0.3, pp. 31-32 (q = −Cp, the gain) and p. 30 (Venus), and in Sources as "an unpublished manuscript, undated (circulated c. 2001 to 2004)". Conventions stated where quoted: the Olympiad (fires negative, a = −x), [GSS12] (fires negative, c = C^T), Donnelly (M = C^T, fires positive), Bourbaki (transpose).

## 2026-10-10 P3-A8: numbers, typed numbers and the scanner
Decision: the numbers-in-prose report is empty in both sections for this file (the reading of P1-A8, P2-A9). In props and captions through `fmt`/`resolveGoldenPath`: the same paths plus pentagon_example values in the b8 and b12 captions. ## 2026-10-10 P3-A9: heights of coroots
Decision: beat 6 defines the coroots as the positive roots of the reversed board (2.5), the height as the sum of the coefficients, says that on A3 and E8 a coroot and its root have the same height, and that with double roads they can differ, "so the numbers fired are heights of coroots"; the Seen box and the B3 challenge say "heights of coroots". The theorem is cited to [Ste59] 9.1 for roots, [Kos59] and [Mac72] as reported by later authors, and the coroot version as verified by computation for every board of rank at most eight. Reason: F6, F7, SOURCES 11.5.

## 2026-10-10 P3-A9: the Lie theory paragraph
Decision: the graph of T8 is named the Hasse diagram of the left weak order: a move multiplies the element reached on the left (the play i_1, ..., i_k reaches w = s_{i_k} ... s_{i_1}, and the next move gives s_i w), and the separating roots of the position w rho are the positive roots made negative by w^{-1}, its inversion set ([Hum90] 1.7; checked on the example 1, 3, 2, whose roots (0,1,0), (0,1,1), (1,1,0) are those of the reduced word 1, 3, 2 of w^{-1}). The wave with class 0 fired first is the product ... c_1 c_0 of h factors, a power (c_1 c_0)^{h/2} only for even h. Reason: instruction 5.12.

## 2026-10-10 P3-X1: what the document tasks are (diagnosis)
Decision: record, from DevTools traces at 360 by 800 with the processor slowed 4 times (Playwright, CDP) and bounding experiments on copies of the built M3 page (not of record; single or triple runs, load 1 to 4):
- With everything after beat 1 removed from the HTML, TBT fell to about 26 ms; with everything after beat 1 kept but `display: none`, to about 460 ms (Style & Layout 339 ms against 1188 ms). The cost is the rendering of the content after the first view, not its parsing.
- The time is not proportional to the number of layout objects. While the parser streams the rest of the page, Chromium paints a frame after each chunk; each frame lays out again, and passes with 6 to 103 dirty objects out of 600 to 1500 took 30 to 220 ms each (4 times slowed). Hiding beat 1's tool, hiding every KaTeX formula, or switching off container queries did not change that much.
- Removing KaTeX's HTML layer (keeping MathML) did not help M3; removing every island after beat 1 halved TBT.
- The b1 hydration task (200 to 330 ms, the Svelte runtime) is by itself above the 200 ms line on this container (a 300 ms task alone contributes 250 ms of TBT). No layout or markup change can remove it; only the tools can (SPEC 7.6 fixes `client:idle` for the first tool).

## 2026-10-10 P3-X1: remedy (a), content-visibility: auto, tried and dropped
Decision: dropped. Tried as the instruction describes, through Lesson.astro only: `content-visibility: auto` with `contain-intrinsic-block-size: auto <typical height>` (beats 2200 px, Seen 1000, Lie theory 550, Sources 1300, challenges' tool frames 1100) on every beat after the first, Seen, LieTheory, Sources and the tool frames placed directly in the article; paint containment would have clipped the figures that break out of their beat, so each skipped beat was widened to the figure column by a negative margin and an equal padding (prose and figures stayed at the same pixels: checked at 360, 500, 800 and 1280 pixels on M0 to M3, no new horizontal scroll), with 8 pixels of room for focus rings elsewhere, and a print rule restoring `visible`.
Measured: alone (with the M2 swap built in, load 3.53 to 1.40) M0 420.1, M1 808.6, M2 933.7, M3 1253.6 ms TBT; together with remedy (c1) (load 0.69 to 1.37) M0 332.8, M1 680.3, M2 685.4, M3 1091.4 ms; LCP 0.44 to 0.62 s, CLS unchanged (at most 0.0017). Traces show why the gain is small: Chromium lays out a newly parsed block once before it decides the block is off screen; `content-visibility: hidden` on the same blocks was no cheaper.
Why dropped: the full browser suite failed 4 tests with it (M1 "no forbidden statement" and "writing rules", M2 "anatomy", M3 "no forbidden statement"): `innerText` of a skipped block is empty, and the lesson tests read the Lie theory paragraph, beat 5 and the whole article with `innerText`. Worse, checks that read `main.innerText` (for example "no em-dash in the text") would pass vacuously on the skipped blocks. Tests may not be loosened, and the lessons' tests are not mine, so the remedy goes. Lesson.astro keeps a comment saying so.

## 2026-10-10 P3-X1: remedy (c1), the render hint for the first view, kept
Decision: kept. Lesson.astro puts `<link rel="expect" href="#first-view-end" blocking="render">` into the head (through a new named slot `head` of Base.astro), and Beat.astro writes `<div id="first-view-end" hidden></div>` at the end of beat 1. A browser that knows the hint (Chromium 124 and later) paints once the end of beat 1 has been parsed instead of painting and laying out the first view again for every chunk of HTML; others ignore it; if the marker is missing the browser paints when the document has been parsed. Nothing changes without JavaScript (it is markup), nothing is per lesson, and M4 and M5 get it by using Beat as the anatomy says.
Measured: alone, without (a) (with the swap, load 0.90 to 1.02): M0 572.6, M1 677.8, M2 978.5, M3 1242.6 ms; the final state, twice (below): M0 518.7 and 530.7, M1 867.9 and 717.8, M3 963.4 and 1215.5 ms, against the baselines M0 636.6 and 562.1, M1 931.7 and 940.7, M3 1262.7 and 1459.2 ms. M2's own number is dominated by the swap (next entry). LCP stays between 0.43 and 0.73 s, CLS unchanged. The gain is modest, of the order of 50 to 250 ms, and inside the spread of single runs on M0; it is kept because every pair of runs on M1 and M3 moved the same way and it costs nothing measurable.
Tests that guard it: the whole browser suite (481 passed with it; the only failures were the two environment cases below and one timing flake that passed three times on rerun), `pages.spec.ts` geometry of beat 1, `nojs.spec.ts`, `a11y.spec.ts`, the M0 to M3 specs.

## 2026-10-10 P3-X1: remedy (b), KaTeX output, measured, route unchanged (request)
Decision: no change; request to the orchestrator with numbers. The route of decisions/P0-A3.md renders every formula as `htmlAndMathml`. In the built pages (before): M0 83 formulas, 1303 elements in `.katex-html` and 728 in `.katex-mathml` of 3222 elements, 58.3 kB and 21.0 kB of 232 kB; M1 955 and 570 of 6380 elements (43.2 kB and 17.4 kB of 564 kB); M2 1941 and 1123 of 5159 elements (87.2 kB and 32.6 kB of 337 kB); M3 951 and 526 of 6289 (43.0 kB and 16.4 kB of 514 kB). KaTeX's `output: 'mathml'` would drop the HTML layer (about 25 to 38 percent of a lesson's elements on M0 and M2) and keep MathML for assistive technology, so A-12 still holds; `output: 'html'` would drop MathML and break A-12, so it is not proposed. Effect, approximated by deleting the HTML layer from a copy of the built page: M2 (with (c1), five diagnostic runs, without hydration) TBT median 532 to 453 ms; M3 no measurable change. The cost: formulas would be drawn by the browser's MathML Core rendering with system fonts instead of KaTeX's layout, which looks different and needs the owner's eye in three engines (`mathml.spec.ts` checks MathML rendering, and WebKit and Firefox are not installed in this container), and the lesson tests that read `.katex` and `annotation` would have to be checked. Not worth changing the route for the lab timings alone.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: the folded board's labels and name (plan reading of E-7)
Decision: `fold` builds the folded board directly, not through `fromAdjacency`: `{ n: m, cartan: Cf, labels, kind, arith: SafeInt, name }`, frozen like every engine board. `cartan` stays in the order of the orbits, so `cartanNumbers(folded)` is the golden `folded_cartan`. With `id = identify(folded)`, `labels[id.order[k]] = k + 1` (position `id.order[k]` plays Bourbaki node `k + 1`) and `name = id.name`; `kind` is `boardKind(Cf)` (always `gcm` for a symmetry of a finite board). Fallback: when the folded board is in several pieces (possible because `requireFinite` admits a disconnected board whose pieces are finite, for instance three A1 nodes folded by the identity) or `identify` returns `null`, `identify` is not called or not used, the labels are `1..m` in the order of the orbits and `name` is absent. The eight rows that fold give: A3 flip `B2` labels `[1, 2]`; A5 flip `B3` `[1, 2, 3]`; A7 flip `B4` `[1, 2, 3, 4]`; D4 swap `C3` `[1, 2, 3]`; D5 swap `C4` `[1, 2, 3, 4]`; D6 swap `C5` `[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]`; E6 flip `F4` `[1, 4, 2, 3]`; D4 rotation `G2` `[2, 1]`. Listing the orbits in increasing label reproduces `folded_orbits_in_bourbaki_order` on all eight (tested). What the specification left open: E-7's row "folded board" says labels are in the Bourbaki order of the type `identify` names, without saying how the board object is built. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3, traps 18, 19). Reason: `fromAdjacency` would give labels `1..m` in position order.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: the reason text of a board that does not fold
Decision: `fold` returns `{ folded: null, orbits, reason: 'an orbit contains an edge' }` when some orbit contains an edge, the text of the golden row of A4; the test compares it as a plan check (rule 6 makes `reason` documentation). What the specification left open: the text. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3). Reason: T11's first tab shows it.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: guards of averagedSimpleCartan and applyWordFloat; no guard on symEig and classifyRestricted
Decision: `averagedSimpleCartan` and `applyWordFloat` call `requireGcm` then `requireFinite` (NeedsTwoWayRoads on a one-way board, NotFiniteType off the finite types), like `fold`, `averagedRootsType`, the geometry and the Wythoff functions. `symEig` (a matrix) and `classifyRestricted` (a set of vectors and a Gram matrix) have no guard; the board-taking functions that call them carry it. In every guarded function the argument shape is checked first (RangeError: a symmetry that is not a permutation of `0..n-1`, a vector of the wrong length, an unknown game, a position outside `0..n-1`, a bad `rings` or cap), then `requireGcm`, then `requireFinite`, the order P2-A1 and P3-A1 used. `symEig` throws RangeError on a matrix that is not square and does not check symmetry (as the oracle). What the specification left open: 5.6 names "fold, averagedRootsType, the Wythoff, Satake, representation and geometry functions" but not `averagedSimpleCartan`, and two of the functions take no board. Default used: the plan's readings (instruction 5.5, 5.6, 5.10, 5.13). Reason: `averagedSimpleCartan` belongs with `fold` and `averagedRootsType` in the 5.4 table; `applyWordFloat` stands in the Geometry table; finiteness is decided through `guards.ts` only (E-11).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: classifyRestricted's parameter type (a supertype of the contract)
Decision: `classifyRestricted(S, B, r)` in `src/restrictedType.ts` accepts vectors whose entries are `number`, `bigint` or `Rational` (exported type `RestrictedEntry`) and converts each entry to a `Rational` on entry; `B` is `readonly (readonly Rational[])[]`. S is treated as a set: a vector given twice counts once (the oracle's argument is a Python set; keyed by the rational text in lowest terms). It returns `RestrictedType = { name: string; classes: { class: string; roots: number; form_value: string }[] }`, classes in ascending squared length, `form_value` as `ratToString` text (rule 4). The oracle's decision table is ported in its order (non-reduced first; one norm A, D, E; two norms with the ratio 3 test for G2 before the rank 2 rule, which always says `B2`, and F4 before the B and C counts); every `assert` and `raise ValueError` becomes `throw new EngineError('classifyRestricted: ...')` with the oracle's message text (`unrecognised simply-laced system`, `unrecognised system`) or a sentence starting `not a root system` for the bare asserts, followed by the oracle's tuple values. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 2.4 phase 6 writes `S: number[][]`, but `averagedRootsType` passes rational averages. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.4): a supertype of the contracted parameter, so both callers type-check. Reason: reported under `openQuestions` as a contract amendment to confirm, not as a deviation.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: the types Symmetry, FoldResult, FaceType and three result types are declared in the phase-4 files
Decision: `Symmetry` and `FoldResult` (CONTRACTS.md 2.2, verbatim) are declared in `fold.ts`, `FaceType` (verbatim) in `wythoff.ts`, and the plan's result types `RestrictedType`, `RestrictedEntry` (`restrictedType.ts`) and `CoxeterPlaneRings` (`geometry.ts`, the golden field names `rings`, `distinct_points`, `h_points_on_every_ring`) beside their functions; all six are exported as types from `index.ts` in the block headed `// phase 4 (P4-A1)`. What the specification left open: the files. Default used: the placement P1-A1, P2-A1 and P3-A1 used for their record types. Reason: `SatakeDiagram` (phase 6) can import `Symmetry` from `fold.ts`.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: wythoffFVector returns n + 1 entries
Decision: `wythoffFVector(board, rings)` returns `n + 1` bigint entries as the oracle does: `f[0]` vertices, `f[1]` edges, ..., `f[n]` = `1n` when some node is ringed and `0n` when none is; with no ring the result is `[1n, 0n, ..., 0n]`. Tests compare `f.slice(0, n)` with the golden `f_vector`. What the specification left open: the length. Default used: the oracle's (instruction 5.11). Reason: a port keeps the oracle's shape.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: wythoffEdges items, order and truncation
Decision: each item is `[u, v]` with `u` before `v` in the lexicographic order of their entries compared as integers (through `arith.sign(arith.sub(...))`, never as strings), entries converted to `number`; the visited set is keyed by the decimal text of both ends in canonical order (`u|v`); items are in discovery order: the seeds `{lambda, R_i lambda}` for `i` from 0 to n - 1 with `lambda_i != 0`, then breadth first over the pairs in the order found, firing both ends at each node in increasing position through `fire`. The cap acts when the search is about to add item number `cap + 1` (seeds included): `{ items, truncated: true }` with exactly `cap` items; otherwise every edge with `truncated: false`; a cap that is not a non-negative integer is a RangeError; cap 0 with a ringed node gives `{ items: [], truncated: true }`. On E8 with rings `(0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0)` (1_42) and `CAPS.edgesAtRest` the result is truncated with exactly 70000 items. What the specification left open: the oracle's set has no order and raises at the cap. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.12, traps 11, 12). Reason: E-6, E-10.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: rings must be non-negative integers
Decision: `wythoffFaceTypes`, `wythoffFVector` and `wythoffEdges` throw RangeError unless `rings` has one entry per position and each entry is a non-negative safe integer. What the specification left open: the instruction names only the wrong length; the oracle does not check. Default used: plan. Reason: SPEC.md 2.14 takes "a dominant position lambda with numbers a_i >= 0" and T10 offers "a non-negative integer on each node"; the face rule and the polytope graph are meaningless on other input.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: the rounding helper and the minus-zero rule
Decision: `round6(x) = Math.round(x * 1e6) / 1e6`, then `-0` normalised to `0` (`r === 0 ? 0 : r`); radii are `round6(Math.hypot(x, y))`, grouped in a `Map` and sorted ascending; distinct points are counted on the text key `${round6(x)},${round6(y)}`. The body of `coxeterPlaneRings` is the helper `ringsOfPoints(points, h)`. Both `round6` and `ringsOfPoints` are exported from `geometry.ts` for the unit test of the rule and are not part of the public API (`index.ts` does not export them). The computed radii equal the golden radii exactly (not only within 1e-5) on all 31 types, checked once with a throwaway test. What the specification left open: the helper (5.4 says "rounded to 6 decimals and -0 counted as 0"). Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.8, traps 13, 14). Reason: `toFixed` would count `-0.000000` and `0.000000` apart.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: the order of a sub-board's Weyl group
Decision: `|W_K|` in `wythoffFaceTypes` is `1n` for the empty set and otherwise the product over the connected components of K (the oracle's `induced_components`, joined in either direction) of `BigInt(weylOrder(subBoard(board, component)))`, with the components' orders kept in a `Map` for the length of one call only (nothing is cached across calls, E-5). Counts are `W / (|W_K| |W_K'|)` in bigint with exact division (EngineError on a remainder, which cannot happen). What the specification left open: the oracle calls `weyl_order(sub_matrix(C, K))`, which equals this product on a disconnected K. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.11, trap 8). Reason: no `weylOrder` on an empty or disconnected board; `TypeError: Cannot mix BigInt and other types` avoided by converting first.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: geometry.ts imports placed after choleskyBasis
Decision: the phase-4 imports of `geometry.ts` (`cartanNumbers`, `CAPS`, `symmetrizer`, `EngineError`, `coxeterNumber`, `Game`, `Word`, `roots`, `bipartition`) stand at the top of the appended section, after `choleskyBasis`, and nothing P3-A2 wrote was changed. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: the instruction says to append and change nothing above; ES import declarations are hoisted, so their place in the module does not matter, and no linter is configured.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: coxeterWord and the colour classes
Decision: `coxeterWord` takes `[c0, c1] = bipartition(board)` (P3-A1, in `weyl.ts`, re-exported from the index) and returns the class containing position 0, sorted ascending, followed by the other class sorted ascending; if `bipartition` returned `null` after the guard it would throw EngineError. P3-A1's first class always holds position 0 (the least position of each component gets colour 0), so the swap never happens; it is kept as the defensive form of the oracle's `col[comp[0]] = 0`. The word equals the golden `bipartite_colouring` order on all 32 types (tested). What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: trap 17.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A1: enumerations inside the finite theory
Decision: `averagedRootsType` and `coxeterPlanePoints` enumerate all roots with `roots(board, CAPS.workerPositions)` after the guard and throw EngineError if the result is truncated (impossible on a finite board: at most 288 roots on twelve nodes). What the specification left open: the cap (instruction 5.4: "a cap above the largest root count"). Default used: the largest enumeration cap of 5.6. Reason: the enumeration must be exact; finiteness was decided by the guard, never by the cap.
