## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the depth axis of the Coxeter view and the three turns
Decision: the Coxeter view is coordinates 0 (right) and n-1 (up) of y with coordinate 1 as depth for n >= 3 (toward the viewer is positive), and none for n = 2. The turning angles of the j value act on the frame in the order horizontal axis u (turn[0]), vertical axis v (turn[1]), viewing axis w (turn[2]), each about the axes as they stand after the previous turn. Signs follow the right-hand rule for turning the picture, with u, v, w right-handed: a positive turn[0] brings the top toward the viewer, a positive turn[1] takes what faced the viewer to the right, a positive turn[2] turns the picture counterclockwise on the screen. Each step turns two orthonormal vectors in their own plane (angles reduced modulo 360 before conversion to radians; a multiple of 360 is skipped, so the frame stays bit-exact), which keeps the frame orthonormal to 1e-12 (tested for n = 2 to 8). What the specification left open: SPEC 6.6 names the three axes but not the order, the signs, or which coordinate is depth in the Coxeter view. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3). Reason: the round trip "a turn, then its negation in reverse order" is the identity, and the picture of the oracle's `coxeter_plane_points` is the view at zero turn.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: rank 2 and rank 1
Decision: on rank 2 `frameFor` returns `w: null` (never a zero vector) and `turnFrame` applies only turn[2]; turn[0] and turn[1] leave the frame unchanged, so the picture stays flat and u, v stay orthonormal. `parseProjection` still accepts three angles on rank 2 and `formatProjection` writes back what was parsed. Plan extension: rank 1 (A1, among the named types T10 offers) is a line: `frameFor(1, coxeter)` is u = (1), v = (0), w = null, no turn moves it, `parseProjection` accepts `coxeter` (with or without angles, as `urlCodec.ts` does) and never `xyz`, and `defaultProjection(1)` is `coxeter`. What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T10 says "rank 2 flat" and nothing about rank 1. Reason: a link `g=A1&j=coxeter` passes the codec, so the projector must read it.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the default projection per rank
Decision: `defaultProjection(n)` is `coxeter` for n = 1, 2 and n >= 4 and `xyz:1,2,3` for n = 3, all with zero turn. What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T10 fixes the kinds of view per rank, not the default. Default used: the plan's (5.3). P4-A3 may fix another default per board in `registry/T10.ts`, and the state field `projection`, when present, overrides.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: "projection preserves the invariant form on the chosen plane"
Decision: read as: in y-space the form is the dot product (y . y' = (p, p'), tested within 1e-9 against `formValue` on 50 random population pairs for A3, B3, G2 and E8 with E from `choleskyBasis`, and from `euclideanBasis` too once the engine exports it); rotations in coordinate planes and the turns are isometries (dot products unchanged within 1e-12); the frame is orthonormal, so a vector y = s u + t v of the chosen plane is drawn at (s, t) with length preserved, and on rank 3 with xyz:1,2,3 the three coordinates hold the whole length. What the specification left open: the roster's wording. Default used: the plan's interpretation (5.3).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the depth cue, its alpha floor, eight levels
Decision: `depthStyle(depth)` for depth 0 (far) to 1 (near) is width = lineWidth x (0.5 + d), radius = pointRadius x (0.6 + 0.8 d), alpha = 0.6 + 0.4 d, linear and so continuous and non-decreasing; `depthStyle(null)` (no depth axis) is the middle (d = 1/2). Base values: line width 1 CSS pixel, point radius 2.5 (plan). The alpha floor is 0.6: the road and text colours of both themes blended at 0.6 over their page keep 4.5 to 1 or more against it (light road 4.54, dark road 6.02 by the WCAG formula with sRGB blending; tested at 3 to 1, A-6), so a far segment is still a road. The cue works through width and radius first. Renderers quantise into eight levels (`DEPTH_LEVELS`), each drawn with `depthStyle` at the centre of the level, rounded to two decimals so that canvas and SVG carry identical numbers; a scene without depth uses the middle style for every level. What the specification left open: the mapping (T10 "points and segments with a depth cue"). Default used: the plan's (5.4, 5.6).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the scale of buildScene and the depth of a scene
Decision: `buildScene` puts the origin of y at the centre of the box, one scale for both directions, y flipped once (downwards on the screen); the scale is (min(width, height) / 2 - margin) / radius with radius by default the largest norm of the unprojected vectors, which `project` records in `Point3.norm` (plan extension of `Point3`), so that no rotation or turn changes it and a picture does not jump while it is turned; margin defaults to the radius of the nearest point plus 2 pixels. Depth is z normalised over the scene's z range; a scene has no depth axis when the cue is off or when the z range is below 1e-12 times the radius (rank 2: every z is 0), and then every depth is 1/2. Segments carry the mean depth of their ends and are sorted far to near, ties in the order given (stable). `Scene` also carries `scale` (pixels per unit of y) for T10's pointer mapping; the renderers do not read it. What the specification left open: everything here. Default used: the plan's (5.5).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the caps while turning, and above 70000 edges
Decision: `selectSegments(scene, turning)`: above `CAPS.edgesAtRest` segments nothing is drawn but the vertices, at rest and while turning alike (beyond that cap the edges are not enumerated at all, T10 "Limits"); at rest everything up to that cap; while turning everything up to `CAPS.edgesTurning`, otherwise exactly `CAPS.edgesTurning` chosen by the fixed stride floor(k m / cap), k = 0 .. cap - 1, through the far to near order, the same every frame. `drawScene` draws the first `CAPS.verticesDrawn` points in scene order. The caps are read from the engine's `CAPS` (exported again as `LIMITS` for the tests); a unit test scans the three modules for a retyped cap. What the specification left open: how the turning sample is chosen. Default used: the plan's (5.6).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: colours, background and the canvas context
Decision: segments use `--road`, points `--text`, the background `--page` (plan; CONTRACTS 8 lists the tokens but assigns none to T10's marks and SPEC 6.3 has no such mapping). `drawScene` receives the three concrete strings (T10 reads them with `getComputedStyle` at draw time and on a theme change); `renderSvg` writes `var(--road)`, `var(--text)` and `var(--page)` by default, or concrete strings for a file or an export. Neither module contains a colour literal (tested). The canvas fills its background with the page colour (`fillRect`, added to the plan's list of `Context2DLike` members) so that the picture is the same as the SVG's background rectangle; `strokeStyle` and `fillStyle` are typed `unknown` in `Context2DLike` so that a real `CanvasRenderingContext2D` fits. The device pixel ratio is applied once with `setTransform(dpr, 0, 0, dpr, 0, 0)` inside one `save`/`restore`. What the specification left open: the tokens of T10's marks. Default used: the plan's (5.6, 5.7).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the j value
Decision: `parseProjection(text, n)` uses the same regular expression as `site/src/lib/urlCodec.ts` (`coxeter` or `xyz:` and three digit strings, optionally `@` and three integers, negative allowed) and adds the rank rules: xyz axes distinct and in 1..n (so never on rank 1 or 2); it returns null otherwise. It agrees with the codec on every input the codec accepts except that the codec does not check rank (it accepts `coxeter` everywhere, as the projector does) and both accept leading zeros (`xyz:01,2,3`). `formatProjection` writes axes from 1, angles reduced to 0..359, and leaves out `@0,0,0`; `formatProjection(parseProjection(s, n)) === s` for every normalised s. Angles are kept as parsed (for example -30) and reduced only when written or turned. What the specification left open: normalisation. Default used: the plan's (5.3).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the SVG snapshot and its 200 kB guard
Decision: `renderSvg(scene, options)` returns one `<svg xmlns=... viewBox="0 0 W H" width height>` string: optional `<title>` and `<desc>` first (then `role="img"` and `aria-labelledby`), a background `<rect>`, one `<g>` of segments with one `<path stroke-width stroke-opacity d="Mx yLx y...">` per depth level far first, and one `<g>` of points with one `<g fill-opacity>` per level holding one `<circle cx cy r>` per point; every id is prefixed by `options.id` (default `projection`); coordinates rounded with `roundTo(x, decimals)` (default one decimal). It uses the same `selectSegments(scene, false)`, `levelStyles`, `segmentRuns` and `pointPlan` as the canvas, and a test checks that the canvas and the SVG receive the same rounded coordinates, widths, radii and opacities in the same order. Measured: the E8 root polytope (240 points, 6720 segments, depth cue off) in a 360 by 360 box is 135761 bytes at one decimal, under the 200 kB guard. Because P4-A1's `euclideanBasis` and the committed E8 file do not exist yet, that test builds the scene from the engine (`roots`, and the pairs at inner product 1 through the integral Gram matrix, a sample checked with `formValue`, every degree equal to the golden 56) with `choleskyBasis` in place of `euclideanBasis`; it switches to `euclideanBasis` by itself once the engine exports it, and the continuation may switch its source to the committed E8 file as the plan says. The size in the true Coxeter plane will differ by a few kilobytes and is to be re-measured then. What the specification left open: the markup (7.4). Default used: the plan's (5.7).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: distinct dots and rings
Decision: `roundTo(x, digits) = Math.round(x * 10^digits) / 10^digits + 0` (never -0); `countDistinct` keys on both coordinates rounded to 6 decimals; `ringsOf` keys on the radius `Math.hypot(x, y)` of the unrounded coordinates rounded to 6 decimals, sorted innermost first: the two different rules of the oracle's `coxeter_plane_rings`. This is not bit for bit Python's `round` (half to even on the exact binary value); the two can differ only within about 1e-12 of a rounding boundary. What the specification left open: the JavaScript rounding. Default used: the plan's (5.8).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2: the preset file format, key naming and the configurations
Decision: `PRESET_CONFIGS` holds the seven configurations of the M7 figure table in this order: A2 highest root (`root-polytope`, `two-graphs`), A3 highest root (`root-polytope`), D4 highest root (`root-polytope`, `coxeter-plane`), A3 (1,0,0) (`rings`), B3 (1,0,1) (`face-table`), B3 (0,1,1) (`make-uniform`), E8 highest root (`showpiece`); the rings of a root polytope are computed as `toNumbers(board, highestRoot(board))`, never typed. Keys `<board>-r<rings joined by '.'>` (A2-r1.1, A3-r1.0.1, D4-r0.1.0.0, A3-r1.0.0, B3-r1.0.1, B3-r0.1.1, E8-r0.0.0.0.0.0.0.1, checked by loading the module in Node). A file holds, in this order and nothing else: schema, tool, key, board, rings, presets, counts {vertices, edges, moveEdges}, edgesEnumerated, fVector (numbers when safe, else decimal strings), vertices, adjacency and moveAdjacency (for each vertex the sorted larger neighbours), tree {parent, node as labels}, shortRoots (null in all seven files: the four root polytope configurations, A2, A3, D4 and E8, are simply laced, and the other three are not root polytopes; B3 has two root lengths, but its two configurations are not root polytopes. Corrected 2026-10-17 in the repair entry below). `serializePreset` is `JSON.stringify` with no spaces and a final newline; the manifest `index.json` is `{schema, tool, files: [{key, board, rings, presets, file, counts}]}` with bare file names. A repeated pair, a loop, or an edge end that is not a vertex throws; a truncated orbit or edge enumeration throws `PresetRefused`. The default configuration of T10 is P4-A3's to fix (deviation, see the report). The measured sizes of adjacency lists against pairs, and the committed files themselves, wait for P4-A1 and the continuation. What the specification left open: the format (plan). Default used: the plan's (5.9).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2 (repair of wave 4.1): short roots of a root polytope, and the test that claimed every configuration is simply laced
Decision: the reviewer's finding is right. `presets.test.ts > the rings of every root polytope configuration equal numbers_of_highest_root` asserted that the golden `highest_short_root` is absent for every board of `PRESET_CONFIGS`, with the comment "Every shipped configuration is simply laced"; B3 is in `PRESET_CONFIGS` (face-table, make-uniform) and the golden file has `types.B3.highest_short_root = [1,1,1]`, `simply_laced = false`. Reproduced in a scratch tree (this branch with P4-A1's `packages/engine` from `worktree-wf_88a98043-0aa-1` laid over it, files written by this branch's `computePreset`; nothing of it committed): 10 of 11 preset tests passed and this one failed with "expected false to be true" at presets.test.ts:168. The sentence in section 5.9 of my instruction ("all seven configurations are simply laced") is wrong in the same way; the files hold `shortRoots: null` because the B3 configurations are not root polytopes. Fixes: (1) the golden check now applies only to the root polytope configurations (rings `'highest-root'`: A2, A3, D4, E8), and the check that every file has `shortRoots === null` stays for all seven, with the reason stated per kind of configuration; (2) the short roots branch of `computePreset` is now the exported function `presetShortRoots(config)` (the same code, moved; it needs only the engine of phases 0 to 3), and two new tests cover it without P4-A1: on every named type of the golden file but C2 (the board B2), a root polytope gets null on a simply laced board and otherwise the orbit of the highest short root, starting at golden `numbers_of_highest_short_root`, of size twice golden `n_short_positive`, each point an integer population of the length of the highest short root and, at rank 4 and below, plus or minus a golden positive root; and every configuration of `PRESET_CONFIGS` gets null, B3's included although B3 has short roots. In the scratch tree the files written after the move are byte for byte those written before it, and the preset tests pass 13 of 13 (projector, presets and presets-size together 41 of 41). Mutations, each run and restored in a scratch copy: removing the root polytope guard fails the second new test, the freshness test and, with the files regenerated, the kept `shortRoots === null` check of the corrected test; returning null always fails both new tests; starting from the highest root instead of the highest short root fails the first. What the specification left open: nothing (SPEC 2.14: a root polytope "with the short roots available as a second set of points"). Reason: the red-team gap of wave 4.1.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2 (continuation): scripts/presets.mjs and how it loads the engine
Decision: `node scripts/presets.mjs` (any directory; paths from `import.meta.url`) loads `site/src/components/draw/presetsT10.ts` with Node's own type stripping and one resolve hook, and installs nothing. The hook maps `@engine` to `packages/engine/src/index.ts` and an extensionless relative specifier from a file under `packages/engine/src/` or `site/src/components/draw/` to the `.ts` file (or `/index.ts`) that exists; it is registered with `module.registerHooks` (synchronous, Node 22.15 and later) and otherwise with `module.register` and a `data:` module holding the same rule (the function's own text). When `process.features.typescript` is falsy (Node 22.12 to 22.17 without the flag, or a run with `--no-experimental-strip-types`) the script runs itself again once with `--experimental-strip-types` placed after the inherited flags (a `--no-experimental-strip-types` of the parent is dropped), guarded by the variable `TWO_GAMES_PRESETS_STRIP_TYPES` against a loop. All three paths were run on Node v22.22.0: the normal one, the `module.register` fallback (with a scratch preload that hides `registerHooks`), and the re-execution (`node --no-experimental-strip-types scripts/presets.mjs --check`); each gave the same files. The script computes every configuration first and writes nothing when one is refused (`PresetRefused`, or any error) or a file gzips (level 9, `gzipSize` of `scripts/size.mjs`) above 30 x `KB` bytes, and exits 1; otherwise it writes the seven files and `index.json` and removes any other `.json` file in `site/public/presets/T10/`. `--check` writes nothing and exits 1 when a file on disk differs from the pipeline's output or is not produced by it (plan addition, so that MERGE or CI can check freshness without Vitest). It prints a table of file, vertices, edges, moveEdges, bytes and gzip bytes, and the Node version, the stripping mode and the hook used. The engine needed no change: every module loaded under stripping. What the specification left open: the mechanism. Default used: the plan's (5.9).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A2 (continuation): the drawing-time probe, its page and what is timed
Decision: `site/src/pages/dev/draw-time.astro` (layout `Page.astro`: `lang`, one `h1`, the navigation; linked from nowhere) mounts `DrawTimeProbe.svelte` with `client:load`. The probe fetches `import.meta.env.BASE_URL + 'presets/T10/' + 'index.json'`, finds the file whose presets include `showpiece`, fetches it, builds `boardFromName`, `euclideanBasis`, the populations of the vertices with `toPopulation` (floats, cached) and the edge list from `adjacency`, reads `--road`, `--text`, `--page` with `getComputedStyle`, sizes the canvas backing store to 360 x `devicePixelRatio`; all of that is outside the timed region. Then five times: `performance.now()`, the Coxeter frame, y = E p and `project` for every vertex, `buildScene` (360 by 360, depth cue off), `drawScene` at rest with the page's ratio, `performance.now()`. "The drawing time" is the median of the five (plan); the first is reported beside it (`firstDrawTime`), so a cold first draw is visible. Hooks: `data-tool="geometry"` and `data-moves="0"` on the root, `data-hydrated="true"` when the five draws are done or failed, `data-out` `drawTime` (median, one decimal), `drawTimes`, `firstDrawTime`, `segmentsDrawn`, `pointsDrawn`, `dpr`, `error` (empty unless something failed; the test requires it empty). Nothing animates (A-9); there is no control. Deviation from the plan's wording: the plan puts `role="img"` and the `aria-label` on the canvas; svelte-check reports `a11y_no_interactive_element_to_noninteractive_role` for that, so they sit on the box that holds the canvas (the canvas itself carries no role). Without JavaScript the probe shows the SVG snapshot of the same picture, which the page renders at build time with the projector and `renderSvg` from the committed manifest and E8 file (read with `import.meta.glob` and `?raw`, server side only) and passes as the island's default slot (rendered once, not serialised into the island's props); the probe hides it when the canvas is drawn. The plan said `nojs.spec.ts` would not visit the page; it does (every route of the built site is discovered), and the page passes it, `a11y.spec.ts` and `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` (run filtered to this route). What the specification left open: the probe (plan 5.10). Default used: the plan's, with the role moved as said.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: D19 for the folded and the averaged names
Decision: the names are printed exactly as the engine gives them (`folded.name` from `fold`, `averagedRootsType(...).name`), so the tested elements `data-out="folded-name"` and `data-out="averaged-name"` read `B2`, `C3`, `G2`, `BC2` and so on (set with `<sub>`, `data-name` holding the plain name). The D19 equality "B2 = C2" stands in a sibling element: `data-out="folded-name-note"` on the fold tab when the folded name is `B2` (the flip of A3), and `data-out="averaged-name-note"` on the average tab when the averaged name is `B2`. `G2` (rotation of D4) and `BC2` (flip of A4) carry no note. The captions write "B2 = C2" for a rank 2 name recognised from its shape. The board picker shows the name the reader chose (A2 to E6; no rank 2 B or C board is offered). What the specification left open: where the note goes. Default used: SPEC section 10 D19 ("B2=C2" wherever a board is recognised from its shape); the plan's placement (instruction 5.4, 5.7). Reason: the tested name stays `B2`.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the symmetry picker, the default symmetry, the identity
Decision: `symmetries(board)` is called once per board (default limit); the picker lists every symmetry but the identity, in the engine's order (lexicographic in the image tuples), each shown by its orbits in labels with its order (`{1, 3} {2}, order 2`; option value the images joined by dots, the `y` form). The default symmetry is the first one listed: the flip on A_n and E6, the swap of the last two nodes on D5 to D8, the swap `1.2.4.3` on D4. On D4 the five are `1.2.4.3`, `3.2.1.4` (swaps), `3.2.4.1`, `4.2.1.3` (rotations of order 3, folding to G2) and `4.2.3.1` (swap): three swaps fold to C3 in three orbit orders, two rotations to G2 (the instruction's sentence lists the two rotations last; the engine's order puts `4.2.1.3` fourth). A link whose `y` is the identity loads the default state with the notice (plan: a symmetry the tool does not offer, by analogy with 6.6 "a board that the tool does not offer"). `fold` does not check that its permutation preserves the board (as the oracle), so the tool checks `C[s(i)][s(j)] = C[i][j]` itself before folding (a RangeError, which an author sees as a build error naming the instance; a link never reaches it, the codec's rule 7 refuses first). The picker of boards, the symmetry picker and the view switch each reset the start and the moves; the board picker also resets the symmetry to the new board's default. What the specification left open: the order and default. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.2).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: one entry per symmetric move in `moves`
Decision: a symmetric move is recorded as ONE entry of `moves`, the smallest label of the orbit acted on; on replay each entry expands to the whole orbit in increasing position (E-5), and any label of an orbit in `w` names that orbit and is written back as its smallest label (`/tools/fold?g=A3&w=3` is written back as `w=1`; `?g=D4&y=3.2.4.1&w=4.2` holds two symmetric moves, on `{1, 3, 4}` and on `{2}`, and is written back as `w=1.2`). So `data-moves`, the History strip, undo, redo and reset count symmetric moves, and the folded board's word is the same list read through the orbit map (folded position s is orbit s). This is the one place where a T11 link differs in meaning from a T1 link. What the specification left open: 6.6 defines `w` for single moves only. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3), reported as a deviation for MERGE to confirm.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: views, coords, the default start, and starts that are not symmetric
Decision: T11 plays one game at a time: `view` is `populations` or `numbers` and `coords` always equals it (the start is a position of the game shown). Default state of `/tools/fold`: `{ schema: 1, tool: 'fold', board: 'A3', view: 'numbers', coords: 'numbers', start: [1, 1, 1], moves: [], symmetry: [3, 2, 1] }`, fold tab. The populations default start is one Martian in every city of the orbit of city 1 (the orbit sum, which is the folded board's "one Martian in the first city"): `(1, 0, 1)` on A3 with the flip, `(1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1)` on E6, `(1, 0, 0, 0)` on D4 with the swap `1.2.4.3`. Link rules (plan): `v` and `c` name one game; a link with only one of them sets both, a link with both that differ loads the defaults with the notice; a link without `s` takes the tool's default start for the board, view and symmetry in force (the codec's own fallback, one Martian in the first city, is not symmetric); a start that is not constant on the orbits of the symmetry loads the defaults with the notice (an author's such start fails the build naming the instance). What the specification left open: 6.6 lists the codec's failures; these are the tool's. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: links with `v=l` or `m` (plan rules, for MERGE to rule on)
Decision: `v=l` asks for the linked view, which T11 does not offer: the instance loads its default state with the notice that the link could not be read. An `m` whose value parses (`f`, `l` or `r`) is ignored: it is removed from the query before the codec reads the link (so neither T1's table of views and modes nor the replay of rule 6 can refuse it), the rest of the link is read, there is no notice, and the URL written back has no `m`. An `m` that does not parse (`m=z`) fails in the codec like any known key and gives the defaults with the notice. Tested in `T11.test.ts` and `T11.spec.ts`. What the specification left open: 6.6 says only "An unknown key is ignored", and `v` and `m` are known keys. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3); reported under deviations and open questions.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: no symmetric play when the board does not fold
Decision: on A2, A4, A6 and A8 (the flip is the only symmetry and its middle orbit contains an edge) the fold tab shows, in place of the folded board, `data-out="fold-message"`: `This board does not fold by this symmetry: an orbit contains an edge. The orbit {2, 3} contains an edge.` (the engine's `reason`, made sure to carry the phrase whatever the engine writes, then the orbits that contain an edge, in labels). The big board is drawn with its orbits coloured; every node has `data-can-act="false"`; a click makes no move and announces the same message. A link with moves on such a board loads the defaults with the notice; an author's moves there fail the build. `fold`'s NeedsTwoWayRoads and NotFiniteType are never caught. What the specification left open: what the first tab does there. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.8).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: announcements of a symmetric move (deviation for MERGE to confirm)
Decision: one announcement per symmetric move, built in `t11/model.ts` from `speak` and the punctuation of A-4. An orbit of one node uses the A-4 template unchanged (`Fired node 2. Numbers now 2, minus 3, 2. Move 2.`). Several nodes, on boards with at most four nodes: `Fired nodes 1 and 3. Numbers now minus 1, 3, minus 1. Move 1.`, `Fired nodes 1, 3 and 4. Numbers now minus 1, 4, minus 1, minus 1. Move 1.`, `Mutated cities 1 and 3. Populations now minus 1, 0, minus 1. Move 1.`; above four nodes the changed-nodes form (`Fired nodes 1 and 5. Node 1 now minus 1, node 2 now 2, node 4 now 2, node 5 now minus 1. Move 1.`). A move that changes nothing on a board above four nodes says `... Nothing changed. Move k.` (plan: A-4's changed-nodes form would print an empty list). Undo uses `undid` with the big board's values (`Undid move 1. Numbers now 1, 1, 1.`); a redo or a scrub forward reads the move again; reset reads like an undo to the start. Only the big board's values are read; the folded board shows the same numbers. What the specification left open: A-4 fixes templates for one node only. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.6).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the orbit colouring
Decision: decoration only, in an overlay drawn in the same box and grid units as `Board.svelte` (T9's overlay technique) and placed BEFORE the board in the page, so that the board's buttons and their focus ring are painted over it (A-2): an outer ring of radius 0.41 grid units around each node, stroked in `--edge-k`, and on the big board a badge (a small box outlined in the same colour) holding the folded label of the node's orbit as text, at the first of the corners bottom right, bottom left, top left that no road leaving the node points into (the top right holds the node's label). Colour k belongs to the orbit at folded label k, and folded node k has the same ring; when the board does not fold, colour k is the orbit at index k (orbit order, plan). Never the fill (6.3) and never an accent (8.3); no hex literal. The legend `data-out="orbits"` lists each orbit in labels with its folded node as text (`Orbit {1, 6}: folded node 1`), in folded label order. `Board.svelte`'s `decoration` prop is not read by Board (types.ts), hence the overlay. What the specification left open: ring, badge or decoration row. Default used: the plan's (either; ring plus badge chosen).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the tab is outside the link; the preset `average`
Decision: the tab list is data (`t11/config.ts`: `fold`, `average`, and `golden` with `released: false`, rendered nowhere in phase 4); a WAI-ARIA tab list with Left, Right, Home and End, disabled until hydration. The tab is not part of a link in schema 1 (6.6), so "Copy link" on the average tab gives a link that opens the fold tab; no key was added and nothing is stored in localStorage. The preset `average` (A5 with the flip, numbers, all ones, average tab) is therefore a state object but not a link: its state fields go in the link, its tab does not. This is a conflict between 4.0 ("every preset is also a state object and a link") and 6.6 (the tab of T11 is not part of a link), left for MERGE or the owner. The registry has `default` (required by the registry; equal to `fold`), `fold` and `average`, and no `golden` entry. What the specification left open: see above. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.3, 5.13).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the caption follows the state
Decision: a preset's own caption is shown for the preset's state (board, symmetry and tab); when the reader moves away from it on a route or lesson that passed no caption of its own (the tool route passes the registry's default caption), the caption states what the figure now shows, from the engine's names: `Folding D4 by this symmetry gives the board G2. One click acts ...`, `A4 does not fold by this symmetry: an orbit contains an edge. ...`, `Averaging the roots of A5 over this symmetry gives a root system of type C3, while folding the board by the same symmetry gives B3: the two constructions give dual answers.` An author's own caption never changes. What the specification left open: 4.0 "the surrounding prose states the result it demonstrates" for a state the reader reached. Default used: plan addition.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: data-out names
Decision: `symmetry`, `construction`, `big-board`, `folded-board`, `folded-name`, `folded-name-note`, `fold-message`, `orbits`, `averaged-name`, `averaged-name-note`, `averaged-classes`, plus ToolFrame's `caption`, `notice`, `noscript`, `exportNotice`, History's `moveCounter`, `historyStrip`, `historyCounter` and Predict's `predictResult`, `predictReveal`. Each tab names its construction in `data-out="construction"` with the words of 2.13: `Folding the board (orbit sums)` and `Restricting the roots (orbit averages)`. Other hooks: `data-tab`, `data-table-board` (`big`, `folded`) with `data-table-node`, `data-orbit` and `data-folded-label` on legend items, `data-class` on class rows, `data-ring-node` and `data-orbit-ring` on the overlay rings. Every name is in the registry's `outs` and in the comment block of `T11.spec.ts`.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: where the replays run (E-9), and the worker's board
Decision: everything T11 computes on load or on a move runs on the main thread (symmetries, fold, identify, averagedRootsType, one engine call per board per symmetric move), except a list of moves read from a link whose big-board word (the expanded word) or folded word exceeds 2000 moves (E-9 rule 2). Hints, built in `t11/model.ts`: `playHint(board, n) = { board, kind: 'play', moves: n }` with n the expanded length for the big board and the number of symmetric moves for the folded board; on A3 with 1001 symmetric moves the big hint says 2002 (worker), with 1000 it says 2000 (main). When either goes to the worker, both replays go through `engineCall('positionsOf', [state], hint)` (the site's replay, which P1-A2 placed in the worker API "so that a long link replay runs in the worker") instead of `applyWord`, because `positionsOf` returns every position of the play, which the History strip and undo need (deviation from the plan's `engineCall('applyWord', ...)`, same rule and hints). The folded board is sent as its Cartan matrix in orbit order (a custom spec, labels 1..m following its positions), not by its name: `workerWire.specOf` serialises a named board by name, and `resolveBoard('G2')` would rebuild G2 with its positions in Bourbaki order, exchanging the two nodes of the rotation of D4. While the worker runs, both boards keep the start, every node has `data-can-act="false"`, the History and the pickers are disabled and nothing is announced; errors are not caught. Moves the reader makes afterwards are played one at a time on the main thread from the positions kept. A configuration given by an author's properties is replayed at once (at build time, where E-9 does not apply, and again at hydration); no default needs the worker. The averaged roots: `whereToRun({ board, kind: 'enumeration', finiteKnown: true, expectedItems: <roots> })` with the number of roots known in advance (A_n: n(n + 1), D_n: 2n(n - 1), E6: 72), always `main` (instruction 5.7). What the specification left open: E-9 names the cases, not the calls. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.12), with the positionsOf deviation.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the table view and the exports
Decision: "Show as table" holds two tables: the big board (node, orbit, folded node, value as a button) and the folded board (node, orbit, value as a button), values with their exact digits; each button makes the same symmetric move as a click on that node; without JavaScript the buttons are present and disabled. "Download data" writes, beside the state: `view`, `symmetry` (`images`, `orbits` in labels, `order`), `folded` (`name`, `labels` of its rows, `cartan` in orbit order as the golden `folded_cartan`, `orbitsInBourbakiOrder`) or `null`, `foldMessage`, `averaged` (`name`, `classes`), `averagedSimpleCartan`, `bigPositions` and `foldedPositions` (every position up to the cursor, in label order; integers as JSON numbers when safe, else decimal strings). "Export SVG" and "Export PNG" are ToolFrame's, of the figure holding both boards. `quasi_split_real_form` (golden phase 6) is not shown or exported in phase 4. What the specification left open: the derived tables. Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.10).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: arithmetic, long numbers
Decision: a start with an entry beyond the safe range plays both boards on Big copies (`bigBoard` of the named board and of the folded board, same matrices, labels and names), and an `EngineOverflow` during a move replays everything in Big, with no message (SPEC 4 T1). Values above 12 digits are shortened on the nodes by `Node.svelte` (T1's `t01/longNumber.ts`), exact in `data-value`, the tooltip, the table and the export.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: the golden harness in a site unit test
Decision: `site/tests/unit/tools/T11.test.ts` imports `golden`, `matchRecord`, `intEq`, `labels` and `TYPE_NAMES` from `packages/engine/test/golden/_harness.ts` by a relative path and never calls `covers` (the key `folding` belongs to P4-A1's `p4-folding.test.ts`); importing the module registers nothing. Names and form values compare as exact strings. P4-A2's instruction tells that agent not to import the harness from the site; MERGE should settle one practice. The random plays use a small seeded generator written in the test (mulberry32, `SEED = 20261017`, 200 plays per row and game), not fast-check, so the site test needs no package of the engine workspace.

Decision: none; record. With the orbit expansion broken (`expandMove` returning the orbit's first node only) 8 of the 30 unit tests failed, the 200 random plays first, printing the board, symmetry, game, start and word; with the folded name broken as well (the big board's name shown) 14 failed, and 16 of the 21 browser tests failed against a build of that state. Both were restored from git and every test passed again. `npm run size -- --port 4661` (run once at the end, as the orchestrator asked): `/tools/fold/` 79.5 kB gzip of 90, 14 scripts; 28 routes measured, 0 over budget.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: defaults of SPEC section 10 relied on
Decision: D19 only (above). No [U] item was settled (2.13's [U] about the fixed subalgebra is lesson text the tool never prints); no dependency was added.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: repair after the wave 4.2 review, the random plays run through the tool's play path
Decision: the acceptance test `for every folding row with a folded board, 200 random symmetric plays agree between the two boards in both games` now drives `FoldBench`, the class `T11FoldingBench.svelte` plays with, instead of a separate `replay()` function that the component never called. Per row with a folded board, per view (`populations` plays mutation, `numbers` plays firing) and per play (200, the same mulberry32 generator and `SEED = 20261017`): `new FoldBench({ board, view, symmetry, start })` with a symmetric start drawn in [-9, 9] per orbit, then 1 to 12 clicks, each on a random node of either board as the component maps it (a big node at position p is `act(ctx.orbitOf[p])`, a folded node s is `act(s)`); after every click every node of every orbit in `bigValues()` equals `foldedValues()` at that orbit (`intEq` of the harness, on the decimal text read as bigint); at the end `state().moves` holds one entry per click, the orbit's smallest label; `foldedValues()` equals `applyWord` on the folded board of the clicked word (played on its own matrix, not copied); and the same moves read as a link (`new FoldBench({ ..., moves }, { defer: true })`, `needsWorker()` false, `replaySync()`) give the same positions after each move. `replay()` is removed from `t11/model.ts`: the plan's `replay` (both positions by `applyWord`) is `FoldBench.advance`, one `applyWord` per board per symmetric move, which clicks, main-thread link replays and the test share, so no second play path ships. Evidence: with `advance` firing only the first node of each orbit (`expandMove(this.ctx, s).slice(0, 1)`, the reviewer's break) the old test passed (1 passed, 29 skipped) and the new one fails (`A3 symmetry 3.2.1 mutation start -6,-2,-6 clicks folded:0: intEq failed`); with the folded board's move broken (`[0]` for `[s]`) it fails too; restored, all 30 unit tests pass. What the specification left open: nothing; SPEC 4 T11 acceptance and COMMON 4.3 (a test guards the tool's own code). Default used: none.

## 2026-10-17 MERGE: P4 wave 4.2
Decision: the branches cont/P4-A2-w4.2 (P4-A2's part 1, its wave 4.1 repair and its continuation in one branch) and repair/P4-A4-w4.2 (T11 and its repair) were merged in that order with --no-ff merge commits; no conflict. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: README 3.2.
- Pre-approved by the orchestrator and recorded here: P4-A2's ownership of site/src/components/draw/presetsT10.ts, scripts/presets.mjs, site/src/components/draw/DrawTimeProbe.svelte, site/src/pages/dev/draw-time.astro and site/tests/e2e/draw-time.spec.ts (the G4 drawing-time test, although README 4 lists the test under P4-A3); one edit of the root package.json appending node scripts/presets.mjs to the prebuild chain.
- Integration fixes (this merge): site/tests/unit/scripts.test.ts pins the new prebuild chain (P0-A6's test, P4-A2's request); DrawTimeProbe.svelte reads back one pixel inside the timed region so that Chromium's deferred rasterisation is measured (the review measured 10 to 59 ms with the read-back against about 1 ms without; still under the 100 ms of 7.6).
- Integration fixes found by the wave 4.2 merge check (2802 of 2804 unit tests passed): scaffold.test.ts's scan for the forbidden command skips .claude/, where the agents' worktrees live (a red-team lens's scratch script tripped it); site/tests/fixtures/content/build-fixture.mjs takes a lock per checkout so that the fixture builds of content-components.test.ts and released.test.ts, which share the site's .astro/.prerender directory, no longer race (ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND seen once here and once by P4-A2).
- Review and repair: T11's acceptance test "200 random symmetric plays agree between the two boards in both games" ran a replay function the component never called; the repair drives FoldBench, the tool's own play path, and a break of the tool's move now fails it.
- Deviations accepted: P4-A2 (T10's default configuration left to P4-A3; the preset files keyed by board and rings); P4-A4 (announcement wording for a symmetric move of several nodes; w holds one entry per symmetric move, the smallest label of the orbit; the preset 'average' fixes the tab, which schema 1 keeps out of the link; the plan rules for v and m in T11 links).
- Settled for site unit tests (P4-A4's question): a site unit test may import the read-only helpers of packages/engine/test/golden/_harness.ts but never calls covers(); coverage stays the engine suite's.
- Carried to wave 4.3 (a polish agent for T11): the review's minor points on T11 (labels converted by +1 and -1 in model.ts and T11.spec.ts instead of toLabels and toPositions; a caption test that compares a value with itself; the Copy link test reading the link without decodeState; the fold-tab line inviting symmetric play on boards that do not fold; the defaultStart wording).
- Passed to the owner and later phases: P4-A4's finding that workerWire.ts serialises any named board by its name, so a board whose positions are not in its name's standard order (a folded board, later a restricted board of phase 6) would be rebuilt in the worker with its positions permuted (T11 sends the folded matrix as a custom spec; a lead for RT-06 and P6-A1); P4-A2's note that the drawing time on a phone is the owner's part of G4.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the default state
Decision: the `default` preset is A3 with rings `(1,0,1)` (the root polytope of A3, the cuboctahedron wireframe of M7 beat 1), projection `xyz:1,2,3@20,30,0`, graph `polytope`. What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T10 names no default board; P4-A2's `PRESET_CONFIGS` fixes no `default` (it holds the seven configurations of the M7 figure table). Default used: the plan's (instruction 5.1). Its (board, rings) is the file `A3-r1.0.1.json`, already among the seven, so `presetsT10.ts` and `scripts/presets.mjs` were not edited (presetsEdited: false).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: default projection per rank, the default turn, the root-polytope preset
Decision: the default projection of a rank (`t10/config.ts` `defaultProjectionText`) is `coxeter` for rank 1 and 2 (flat: the plane itself; a line on A1), `xyz:1,2,3@20,30,0` for rank 3 (a wireframe turned 20 degrees about the horizontal axis and 30 about the vertical one, so that the snapshot shows depth), and `coxeter` from rank 4 on. The `root-polytope` preset is D4 `(0,1,0,0)` with `xyz:1,2,3@20,30,0`, so that beat 1's D4 differs from beat 6's Coxeter-plane D4; placed on another board (A2, A3) its rings become that board's root polytope, `toNumbers(board, highestRoot(board))`, and a projection that does not apply to the rank (any `xyz` on A2) becomes the default of the rank. What the specification left open: the default projection and turn. Default used: the plan's (5.1, 5.5). Reason: P4-A2's `defaultProjection(3)` has zero turn, which shows a flat-looking picture.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the Coxeter plane view at rank 3, and j=coxeter on rank 2 and on A1
Decision: rank 3 offers a "Coxeter plane" button beside "Three coordinates": `j=coxeter`, rows `E[0]` and `E[2]`, flat, no depth cue and no turning controls, so that A3 (8 of 12) and B3 with the short roots (12 of 18) state their distinct dots from the controls. `j=coxeter` on rank 2 draws the flat plane (no turning controls); it is the default there. On A1 `j=coxeter` is accepted and draws the line (two points, one edge), with no Coxeter button and no ring statistics. Deviation from the plan, which asked to treat `coxeter` on A1 as a value that does not parse: the codec accepts it, P4-A2's projector reads it and makes it the default of rank 1 (decisions/P4-A2.md), so refusing it here would make the tool's own default state a bad link. What the specification left open: rank 1. Default used: P4-A2's reading.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: reading of 6.6 Validation for the projection and the rings
Decision: `site/src/lib/urlCodec.ts` already refuses a `j` naming a coordinate above the rank or twice (so any `xyz:` on rank 1 or 2) and a negative ring, with its notice: nothing was needed in the tool for those. In addition, before any engine call, `t10/studio.ts` `admit` refuses (default state and the codec's notice sentence) a board that is not one of the 32 names and rings above `RING_MAX`; a projection that was not in the link but carried over from the configured state and does not apply to the new rank (the default `xyz:1,2,3@...` and a link `g=A2`) becomes the default of the rank without a notice (a key that is absent keeps the configured value when it applies). What the specification left open: 6.6 lists no rule about coordinates in `j`. Default used: the plan's (5.1), as above.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the largest ring
Decision: a ring is an integer from 0 to `RING_MAX` = 10^6 - 1 (six digits, `CAPS.formFinderDigits`, the cap of an entry typed into T3); the ring buttons, the number fields of the table view and links are held to it. What the specification left open: SPEC says "a non-negative integer on each node". Reason: no orbit of the 32 boards then leaves `SafeInt` (the largest root coefficient is 6), so no link can make the engine throw `EngineOverflow`; the shape depends only on which nodes are ringed. Every value from 0 to `RING_MAX` is reachable without a mouse (the two ring buttons and the number fields).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: make uniform
Decision: the constant c is the largest ringed integer, so the picture keeps its size; the option is off by default and is not a link field. It is disabled with the sentence "Equal numbers on nodes of one length are uniform already." exactly when every ringed node has the same d_i and the same integer (`uniformMatters`; one ringed node; B3 (1,1,0)); with equal d_i and different integers it still acts (it makes them equal). The replay runs along the tree of `orbitGraph`: vertex k is `applyWordFloat` of its parent's real position by the one move that reached it, the same sequence of float operations as replaying `wordTo(graph, k)` from the start (unit test compares the two exactly). The lengths shown are measured on the drawn coordinates y = E C^-1 a and agree with 2 d_i a_i^2 within 1e-9. What the specification left open: c. Default used: the plan's (5.7).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the limit wordings
Decision: refusal `This orbit has 69120 vertices, more than the 20000 the studio draws.` (`refusalText`, data-out `refused`); edge limit `483840 edges are more than the 70000 the studio draws: vertices only.` (`edgeLimitText`, data-out `limitMessage`), numbers from `orbitSize`, `wythoffFVector` and `CAPS`, digits ungrouped. A move graph above 70000 edges (possible only near the vertex cap on A8) gets the same edge-limit sentence. What the specification left open: the wording. Default used: the plan's.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: node names and announcements
Decision: the ring board is `Board.svelte` with `game: 'firing'` (data-board `numbers`), every node `canAct`, and the A-3 pattern with a fixed T10 template through Board's `spoken` prop and a null state: `Node 3, value 2, ringed` and `Node 3, value 0, not ringed`. Announcements (one polite region, ToolFrame's): after a ring change `Node 3 ring 1. 12 vertices, 24 edges; faces by dimension 12, 24, 14.` (`not ringed` for 0), after the shortcut, the picker, undo, redo and reset their own first sentence with the counts, the limit sentence itself when it applies, `Turn now 30, 30, 0 degrees.`, `Coxeter plane.`, `Move graph: 6 edges, 4 shared with the polytope graph.`, and after the worker `Drawn: ...` with the counts. What the specification left open: A-3 and A-4 have no template for ringing. Default used: the plan's (5.12); the orchestrator may standardise it.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: data-moves and the count of edits (deviation)
Decision: `ToolFrame.svelte` sets `data-moves` from `state.moves`, which T10 does not use (6.6 gives T10 no move list), so T10's root shows `data-moves="0"` always; the number of ring and board edits since load (the undo depth) is `data-out="edits"`. Deviation from the plan (5.12: "data-moves is the number of edits since load"): giving T10's state a `moves` field would write `w=` into its links. Request to P1-A2 / MERGE: an optional `moveCount` prop of ToolFrame that overrides `data-moves`, if the plan's reading is wanted.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: history semantics and the picker
Decision: undo, redo and reset (ToolFrame's buttons) act on the reader's ring and board edits (a ring click, the ring buttons, a number field, "root polytope", the picker): undo restores the whole state as it was before the edit; views (projection, turns, rotations in planes, graph, make uniform, short roots) are not edits. Reset returns to the configured preset (on the tool route the default). The picker sets `board`, resets the rings to the root polytope of the new board, the projection to the default of its rank, the graph to `polytope`, and clears make uniform, the short roots and the rotations. What the specification left open: history for a tool with no move list; the picker's reset for rings. Default used: the plan's (5.2, 5.12).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: turning, keys and the coordinate planes
Decision: six turn buttons (Up, Down, Left, Right, Counterclockwise, Clockwise) in one group named "Turn the picture"; while that group has focus, ArrowUp and ArrowDown turn about the horizontal axis (+10 and -10 degrees, P4-A2's sign: positive brings the top toward the viewer), ArrowRight and ArrowLeft about the vertical axis, Shift with ArrowLeft and ArrowRight about the viewing axis (counterclockwise and clockwise); the step is 10 degrees; a held key (key repeat) counts as turning until the key is released. A drag on the canvas turns by half a degree per pixel, as a convenience. From rank 4: two selectors of a coordinate plane (i, j), "Rotate +10" and "Rotate -10" buttons, the `+` and `-` keys while that group has focus, and "No rotation"; these rotations are not in a link (6.6). No continuous rotation and no zoom are offered, so A-9 needs no pause control. What the specification left open: keys and step. Default used: the plan's (5.5).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the turning subset
Decision: P4-A2's `selectSegments` unchanged: at rest every edge up to `CAPS.edgesAtRest`; while turning, above `CAPS.edgesTurning`, exactly that many by the fixed stride through the far-to-near order, the same every frame; the move graph uses the same selection, and every edge of the subset keeps its label. A single stepped turn (button, key press) ends at rest at once. What the specification left open: the subset. Default used: P4-A2's (decisions/P4-A2.md).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the move graph and its labels
Decision: the move graph joins mu to R_i mu for every node i with mu_i != 0, derived from the vertex list "once more" (5.4) by `t10/graphs.ts` with the engine's `fire`, deduplicated as sorted index pairs; for a preset it is read from the file's `moveAdjacency` (each pair's node found again with `fire`). Every drawn move-graph edge carries the label of its node as text, at every size (no threshold), on the canvas, in the SVG of the view and so in Export SVG and PNG: a numeral of 10 CSS pixels at the midpoint, in the text colour on a 3-pixel halo of the page colour (4.5 to 1 for numerals, A-6), drawn after the edges and points; the edge colour is `--edge-k` of the label (palette.ts `edgeColour`, 6.3); the depth cue acts on the edge width only (opacity 1), so no edge colour falls below its 3 to 1 against the page. A legend lists colour and label, and the table view lists each edge with its node (graphs of at most 300 edges). Where two edges cross at their midpoints (the two diagonals of the hexagon of A2) their labels overlap; nothing was thinned. Deviation from the plan's routing: the derivation is not sent to the worker above 2000 moves (the worker dispatches engine functions only, and no engine function computes a move graph); it runs on the main thread in slices of at most 2000 moves, one task each.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the preset files and the worker
Decision: on hydration, a link, the back button and every reader's change, the tool fetches the manifest (`import.meta.env.BASE_URL + 'presets/T10/index.json'`, once) and, when the state's (board, rings) is a configuration of it (`presetFor`), the data file, and draws from it (`source` reads `preset`); projection and graph changes need no further call. Otherwise the counts decide (`t10/studio.ts` `loadPlan`): both enumerations at most 2000 items run on the main thread (a macrotask between them, so they never add up in one task); a larger one, after a reader's change or the "Draw the orbit" button, runs through `engineCall('orbitGraph' | 'wythoffEdges', args, { board, kind: 'enumeration', expectedItems, finiteKnown: true })`; at load (a link, a placed figure that is not precomputed) it waits for that button, showing the exact counts, the face table and any limit message at once (worker-on-demand). Mapping (placed figure, file): beat 1 root-polytope on A2 `A2-r1.1.json`, on A3 `A3-r1.0.1.json`, on D4 `D4-r0.1.0.0.json`; beat 2 rings `A3-r1.0.0.json`; beat 3 face-table `B3-r1.0.1.json`; beat 4 two-graphs `A2-r1.1.json`; beat 5 make-uniform `B3-r0.1.1.json`; beat 6 showpiece `E8-r0.0.0.0.0.0.0.1.json`, coxeter-plane `D4-r0.1.0.0.json`; the default `A3-r1.0.1.json`: nine figures and the default, seven files. The links of M7 beat 7 (states for P4-A6): `g=E8&r=0.0.0.0.0.0.0.1&j=coxeter` (240, the file), `g=E8&r=1.0.0.0.0.0.0.0&j=coxeter` (2160 vertices, 69120 edges, "Draw the orbit"), `g=E8&r=0.0.0.0.0.0.1.0&j=coxeter` (6720 vertices, 181440 edges: vertices only, "Draw the orbit"), `g=E8&r=0.1.0.0.0.0.0.0&j=coxeter` (17280, vertices only, "Draw the orbit") and `g=E8&r=0.0.0.0.0.0.0.2&j=coxeter` (the doubled roots, 240 vertices, 6720 edges; its rings are not a file, so its edges wait for "Draw the orbit"); `j=coxeter` is written in each because a key that is absent keeps the configured value. Open question for the owner, as the plan says: whether beat 7's links are "configurations that section 3 names"; if so they would need data files of at most 30 kB, which 2160 points with 69120 edges, 6720 points or 17280 points cannot meet.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the snapshot without JavaScript, and the SVG of the view
Decision: during server rendering only (`import.meta.env.SSR`), the component computes the configured state in-process (`orbitGraph`, `wythoffEdges`, the move graph when it is the graph shown; E-9 at build time), projects it with the configured projection in a 360 by 360 box with the depth cue as the view asks, and renders it with P4-A2's `renderSvg` (polytope graph) or `t10/drawing.ts` `pictureSvg` (move graph with labels), with the colour tokens, a `<title>` naming the board, the rings, the counts and the polytope name in plain words, `role="img"`, inside `data-out="snapshot"`. In the browser the first value of that `{@html}` block is a placeholder string, so Svelte 5's hydration adopts the server's SVG nodes without recomputing them (no enumeration at hydration). The figure box is a square reserved in CSS (`aspect-ratio: 1`, at most 480 pixels), so the canvas replaces the snapshot without shift once the configured orbit is complete; after any change the canvas shows what is known at once (vertices while the edges come, an empty box for a refused or waiting orbit), so a picture of another state never stands in for the current one. The SVG stays under the canvas and is rewritten 400 ms after the reader's changes at rest (not at load), so that ToolFrame's Export SVG and PNG take the current view with the labels. The showpiece snapshot in the built HTML holds 240 circles and 6720 segments. What the specification left open: the mechanism. Default used: the plan's (5.11).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: mathematics (A-12)
Decision: board names come from `site/src/generated/names.json` (KaTeX's markup with MathML, written by `scripts/names.mjs` at the start of every build) through `t10/typesetNames.ts` and `import.meta.glob`, which is empty when the file is absent (unit tests and the type check run before any build in CI): in-process during server rendering, so the HTML of every instance carries KaTeX's markup; in the browser the module is fetched when the reader first leaves the configured board (until then hydration keeps the server's markup), and where it is not loaded the name is written as plain MathML `<msub><mi>E</mi><mn>8</mn></msub>`, as T4 and T16 do. The module is not named after KaTeX, so that no script whose name says katex is ever requested; KaTeX is never imported by client code. Polytope names exist only for the 26 golden `wythoff` records, typed from their `name` fields into `t10/names.ts` (a unit test checks the table equals the file); each `p_qr` becomes MathML `<msub><mn>p</mn><mn>qr</mn></msub>` and each board name inside one a board name; "rectified 4_21" is not in the table. Text that cannot hold markup (aria-labels, the live region, the canvas, the snapshot's title, the export footer) uses plain words and digits: `E8`, `polytope 4 21`. Captions are ToolFrame's plain text props and name boards as `A3`, `E8`. No request to extend `names.json` was needed.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the face table and the tables of A-10
Decision: the face table (HTML, in the figure, rendered without JavaScript) lists, for each dimension 0 to n - 1, a total row and one row per face type K (its nodes as labels; "none (vertices)" for the empty set) with its count as the engine's bigint in full digits; the full node set (dimension n, count 1, the polytope itself) is not a row, so the totals are the n entries of the golden `f_vector`. It scrolls in its own focusable region (at most 320 pixels high). The optional type names of the sub-boards were not added. The "show as table" view holds the rings (a button per node that rings like a click, and a number field per node), the vertices (the first 200 rows, the count stated) and the edges of the graph shown when there are at most 300.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: main-thread work against the 50 ms long task of 7.6
Decision: none; record. Bound used: 25 ms median of seven runs in Vitest (the margin 7.6 sets for its own benchmark). Measured on this container with other agents running (one run): counts and face table of E8 with one ringed node 5.5 to 18.3 ms (node 1 the largest); the move graph of the showpiece 1.0 ms; the largest main-thread enumeration the hint builder allows over the 32 types with one ringed node, B6 node 4, vertices (240 items) 0.8 ms and edges (1920 items) 17.2 ms, timed separately because the component runs them as separate tasks. A first version timed them together and failed once at 29 ms; they are now two tasks. In the one run of the whole site-unit project (46 files in parallel) the edge enumeration measured 31.6 ms and failed; rerun alone it measured 17.8 ms and passed (the task notes allow one rerun). `wythoffEdges` itself takes about 15 ms for 1920 edges here (each pair fires both ends at every node), about eight times an orbit enumeration of the same size, so at the E-9 limit of 2000 items it is the main-thread call closest to a long task on the phone; reported to P4-A1 and MERGE. Browser smoke check: no `longtask` entry after hydration on the E8 refusal link, the showpiece and its switch to the move graph. The phone-profile verdict is GATE's lab run.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3: the size of /tools/geometry/ (7.6: 90 kB of JavaScript before the first interaction)
Decision: what the route does not need before an interaction is loaded on demand, and what only the build-time snapshot needs is loaded only during server rendering. `t10/snapshot.ts` imports the SVG writer (`t10/svg.ts`), the move graph (`t10/moves.ts`: the derivation and its canvas renderer) and `t10/typesetNames.ts` with `await import(...)` behind `import.meta.env.SSR` at its top level (the browser build replaces the condition by false), because a static import there put them into the route's first chunk even though the call is dead in the browser (measured: Rolldown assigns statically reachable modules to the static chunk and leaves the dynamic import a facade). In the browser `svg.ts` is fetched at the first rewrite of the SVG of the view after a change, `moves.ts` when the move graph is first shown or derived, `typesetNames.ts` when the board first changes, and `Predict.svelte` only where a `prediction` is configured (until it arrives the question and a `<details>` with the answer and the reveal stand, the same no-JavaScript form as Predict's). `t10/presets.ts` restates the preset directory, the manifest's name and the lookup by (board, rings) instead of importing `presetsT10.ts` (a unit test checks the three agree with it on every configuration). `npm run size -- --port 4681` was run three times: 91.8 kB (over), after a first split that did not move code 89.3 kB, after these changes 88.2 kB, ok (17 scripts). The inline SVG snapshot is HTML, not JavaScript, and does not count (the default A3 snapshot is about 4 kB of markup; the showpiece's, on a lesson page, about 160 kB). What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: 7.6 [D]. Note for MERGE: every tool route carries the registry of every tool (the shared chunk); /tools/sandbox/ measured 89.2 kB in the same run.

Default used: the plan's (5.5).

Decision: none; record. `site/tests/unit/tools/T10.test.ts` 26 tests pass alone; `site/tests/e2e/T10.spec.ts` 24 tests pass against `astro build` and `astro preview` on port 4680 (the showpiece drew in 9.5 to 13.4 ms over the runs; budget 100), beside P4-A2's `draw-time.spec.ts` (passes); `nojs.spec.ts`, `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` and `a11y.spec.ts` pass on every route (92 tests in the last run, 117 with T10 and draw-time in the first). ## 2026-10-17 P4-A3 (repair, wave 4.3): T10's main-thread work in tasks of its own (7.6)
Decision: every piece of T10's main-thread work after hydration runs in a macrotask of its own (`t10/studio.ts` `nextTask`, a message to a channel of its own, so no clamp of nested timers). A link is read one task after hydration (the colours of the drawing are read first in that task, while the layout is still the one hydration found, so that the first drawing forces no style and layout). A change (a link, the back button, a ring, the picker, the shortcut, undo, redo, reset) goes through `request`: the ring board, the picker and the ring table show the configuration asked for (`target`) at once; its facts are computed by `prepareInTasks`, one task each: the verdict of `classify` the first time on a board, the counts (`orbitSize`, `wythoffFVector`), the face table (`wythoffFaceTypes`), the other facts (golden name, short roots, make uniform), the vertices when they belong on the main thread; then, in one more task, the change becomes `current` (figure, counts, face table) and its orbit loads. The load runs the data file's reading and its drawing in two tasks, and `enumerateInTasks` runs the vertices, `wythoffEdges` alone, and the index pairs with the drawing, one task each; the move graph is derived in slices of at most 2000 moves, the first in a task after the one that completed the orbit (`t10/moves.ts` `deriveInTasks`, there so that the route's first scripts do not carry it). One board object per name (`t10Board`), so that the engine's verdict of `classify` (kept per object, E-11) is found by every later guard; the short roots once per board. The figure no longer redraws when the move graph is added to the data while the polytope graph is shown. A newer change stops the work of an older one at its next task. What the specification left open: how the work is scheduled. Reason: the gaps of the wave 4.3 review (the E8 loads were one task of 68 to 245 ms on this container, and a ring click on B6 node 4 ran `wythoffEdges`, the index pairs, two drawings and the move graph in one task). Measured on this container (4 processors, load average 5 to 18 from other agents) with Chrome traces, thread time of the largest task, four runs each on a warm cache: before, E8 refusal link 39 to 77 ms, showpiece link 47 to 75 ms, B6 ring click 28 to 41 ms; after, 16 to 23 ms, 16 to 20 ms, 18 to 29 ms. The largest task left after a ring click is the engine's `wythoffEdges` for 1920 edges (B6 node 4) alone, 14 to 20 ms in Node and 18 to 31 ms in Chrome; it is one engine call that E-9 keeps on the main thread (at most 2000 items), so only a faster `wythoffEdges` or a lower main-thread limit for edge enumerations (an E-9 decision) can shorten it; the request to P4-A1 and MERGE stands.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3 (repair, wave 4.3): the browser long-task checks
Decision: `T10.spec.ts > no long task on E8 loads and the graph switch` now records long tasks from navigation on (a `PerformanceObserver` installed by `addInitScript`, buffered) and excludes nothing: parsing, hydration, the link's counts and face table, the data file and the drawing are all measured, on the E8 refusal link and on the showpiece link, then the switch to the move graph from the moment of the click. One first visit of the route comes before, so that the browser has compiled the route's scripts (the compilation is the same for every link; GATE's lab run measures the cold load on the phone profile). While a measurement runs the test waits with `page.waitForFunction`, never with `expect`, because an `expect` that retries computes an accessibility snapshot on the page's main thread (16 to 64 ms per poll in a CPU profile), which is not the tool's work. The island's end of hydration (`astro:hydrate`) is recorded and must be seen. New test `no long task after a ring click on the largest main-thread configuration`: the configuration with the most vertices and edges that E-9 keeps on the main thread over the 32 types with one ringed node, found with the engine (B6 node 4: 240 vertices, 1920 edges), opened with no ring, then its node clicked; no long task from the click to the end of the drawing and the move graph. Results on this container: against the build before the repair the load test failed 7 of 7 runs (a task of 67 to 100 ms containing hydration and the link's facts) and the click test 3 of 7; after it the load test passed 8 of 10 runs and the click test 7 of 10 (every failure at load average 7 to 14, tasks of 50 to 67 ms wall time; the traces of the same runs give the largest task 16 to 29 ms of thread time, so the rest is the time the container did not give the page; in the click the largest task is the `wythoffEdges` call above); in the run of 118 browser tests the load test failed once (66 ms) and passed alone 3 of 3 on the rerun, with the click test 3 of 3. The reviewer's mutation (a 150 ms busy loop in the E8 counts) fails the new load test (a long task of 159 ms after hydration). These are smoke checks on an unthrottled runner; the phone verdict is GATE's lab run.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3 (repair, wave 4.3): the Vitest bound in the parallel suite
Decision: `main-thread work stays under the long-task budget` keeps its bound (25 ms, median of seven) and now times each piece as the task the tool runs it in: `classify` on a new board object, the counts, the face table and the other facts of every E8 configuration with one ringed node, the showpiece's data file read into orbit data and its move graph, the largest main-thread vertex enumeration, `wythoffEdges` alone, its index pairs, one slice of its move graph. Alone the largest piece is `wythoffEdges` for B6 node 4 at 14 to 20 ms; in `npx vitest run` (all projects in parallel, as CI's `npm run check`) it measured 25.7 ms and failed. That is gap 3 of the review and stays open: the tool cannot shorten one engine call, E-9 keeps it on the main thread, and the Vitest configuration is not P4-A3's. Request to MERGE: run the timing tests in a project of their own that runs alone (for example a `site-timing` project with `fileParallelism: false` and a later `sequence.groupOrder` than the others, the way the engine benchmark of 7.6 runs alone through `npm run bench`), or decide E-9 for edge enumerations; request to P4-A1: a faster `wythoffEdges` (it fires both ends of every pair at every node and keys pairs by text). The bound was not loosened. Separately, two correctness tests that enumerate whole orbits (`drawn vertices and edges equal f_vector[0] and f_vector[1] where edges_enumerated`, 2_41's 69120 edges among them, about 2 s alone, and `the turning subset ...`) ran 8.4 s in the parallel suite and hit Vitest's default limit of 5 s; they now carry a limit of 60 s (`HEAVY`), a limit of the runner, not of the tool: their assertions are unchanged. The last parallel run of the whole suite here (load average 12 to 15) failed three tests: this bound (25.03 ms) and two tests of other owners at Vitest's 5 s limit (`packages/engine/test/properties/p10-affine.test.ts` property 10, `site/tests/unit/tools/T11.test.ts` the 200 random symmetric plays), which says the parallel suite on a busy runner is the problem MERGE has to settle, not T10 alone.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A5: repair of wave 4.3, the G2 builder challenge stays on G2
Decision: the T3 of the challenge "On G2, find weights that the builder accepts" (b13) now carries `linkTo="b11"`, `coords="populations"` and `start={[0, 1]}`. With `linkTo` T3 draws no board picker (`meter.ownBoard` is false), so its builder stays on its own board `G2`; its meter follows b11, the G2 board of the first challenge, which has no picker either (T1 shows its settings only on its own route), and the start equals b11's so the meter reads the same before b11 publishes. The caption says that the meter reads that board. What was wrong (adversarial review of wave 4.3, reproduced on the built page with a scratch probe): the check `{ kind: 'out', name: 'testVerdict', equals: BUILDER_TEXT.pass }` reads only the verdict, and the picker let the reader choose A2, where the starting weights 1, 1, 1 pass; the page then showed "Goal reached." without the goal. What the specification left open: how a challenge on one board is held to that board. Reason for `linkTo` over a check that reads the board: no check kind of `site/src/lib/pages/challengeCheck.ts` (P1-A4) reads a board, and `linkTo` is T3's only way to drop its picker; b11 is reused rather than placing a fifteenth tool, so the island count, the ids b1 to b14 and the word count stay as they were. Test: `M6.spec.ts > challenge: weights on G2 that the builder accepts` now asserts that b13 and b11 offer no `select` or picker, that b11 shows the two nodes of G2, that the starting weights 1, 1, 1 (those that pass on A2) fail with no `data-challenge-result`, then that the G2 weights pass with the result, and that a move on b11 shows in b13's table. It failed before the change (one picker in b13) and passes after.
Residual, not fixed here (outside this agent's files): a link made by hand still sets T3's own board, since T3 reads its state from the address whether or not it follows another instance; `?b13.g=A2` loads A2 into the builder and 1, 1, 1 then report the goal. No control of the page produces such a link for b13 any more. It is the general property of links (SPEC 6.6: a link restores any state), not of this challenge: on the same page `?b11.z=1&b11.g=G2&b11.c=p&b11.s=3,2&b11.w=` meets b11's reach check with no move (scratch probe). Request to P1-A4: a check that also reads the board (for example an optional `board` compared with the tool's board name) or a rule for states loaded from a link, so that no lesson's challenge can be met by an edited address. Request to P2-A8 (lesson M2): the builder challenges b7 (D4) and b9 (the triangle with a tail) of `m2-form.mdx` use the same check beside a T3 with a picker, so the same false success is reachable there.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A4: polish of wave 4.3, the review's five minor points
Decision: the five minor points that MERGE carried from the wave 4.2 review, and nothing else; no property, preset id, `data-out` or `data-control` name changed. Worktree `/home/user/mutation_game/.claude/worktrees/wf_28e2dd98-ae7-3`, branch `worktree-wf_28e2dd98-ae7-3`, fast-forwarded to `4a11e719` before any change. (1) E-7: `t11/model.ts` converts labels and positions only through `toLabels` and `toPositions` on the board concerned. The worker's folded state names its start and moves in the labels of the board the worker rebuilds from it (`resolveBoard({ cartan })`, a custom board whose labels are its own), and `acceptReplay` reads the folded rows back through the same board, instead of `s + 1`; `orbitsInBourbakiOrder` and `foldedInLabelOrder` read the folded board through `toPositions(folded, labelOrder(folded))` instead of `labels.indexOf(k + 1)`. The same pattern in the component's folded table (`labels.indexOf(j + 1)`, with `j + 1` printed as the label) is fixed the same way, because it is the same defect in the same tool; the output is unchanged. `T11.spec.ts` computes its expected values with `symmetryInPositions`, `toPositions` and the label order of each board instead of `l - 1` and `o.includes(l - 1)`. A unit test scans `model.ts`, `T11FoldingBench.svelte` and `T11.spec.ts` for a list shifted by one, a label looked up as a position and an index read as a label; it failed before the fix and passes after; a second check reads the worker states through the board the worker builds. (2) The preset caption test no longer compares a caption with itself: for each preset it checks the engine's folded name and averaged type against the golden row (`folds_to`, `averaged_roots_type`) and that the caption names its board and both names, the fold tab's caption in `gives the board B2 = C2.` and the average tab's in `a root system of type C3,` beside `gives B3:` (D19 note as on screen). With the two names of the average caption swapped the new test fails; the old one passed. (3) The Copy link browser test decodes the copied link with `decodeState`, loaded through `ssrLoader` as `T03.spec.ts` does, against the route's defaults (`T11_PRESETS.default` without tab and caption), and asserts no notice, board `D4`, symmetry `3.2.4.1` and moves `1.2`. With a copied link one start entry short it fails at the notice. (4) On a board that does not fold the fold tab's line reads `Numbers game, no symmetric play: the board does not fold by this symmetry.` (`Mutation game, ...` in the populations view) instead of inviting symmetric play; a browser test over A2, A4, A6, A8 and A4 in the populations view and two server-render checks failed with the old sentence and pass now. (5) The `defaultStart` docstring and the entry above now say one Martian in every city of the orbit of city 1, which on the folded board is city 1 only when that orbit comes first in Bourbaki order (city 3 of C3 for D4's swaps `3.2.1.4` and `4.2.3.1`, city 2 of G2 for the rotations); a unit test pins this on D4's five symmetries (it passes before and after, since only the wording changed). The plan's own sentence (instruction 5.3, "which on the folded board is exactly the 6.6 default") has the same flaw; the plan is not mine to edit. What the specification left open: nothing new. Default used: none.

Decision: the branches repair/P4-A3-w4.3 (T10 and its repair), repair/P4-A5-w4.3 (M6 and its repair) and the T11 polish branch were merged in that order with --no-ff merge commits; no conflict. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: README 3.2.
- Review and repair: T10's browser long-task test measured only the move-graph switch, not the E8 loads, and a ring change on B6 node 4 made tasks of 70 to 140 ms; the repair splits every enumeration, slice and fact into tasks of their own and the test now measures from navigation and a ring click. M6's G2 builder challenge reported success on another board (it now follows a G2 Game Board through linkTo), and its Lie-theory sentence about the fixed subalgebra contradicted SPEC 2.13 (it now names A_{2n-1}, D_{n+1}, E_6 and the rotation of D_4).
- T11 polish (the review's five minor points): labels and positions through toLabels and toPositions, preset captions checked against the engine's folded name and averaged type, the copied link decoded with decodeState, the symmetric-play line only on boards that fold, the default-start wording. The plan's sentence in P4-A4 instruction 5.3 that the populations default is the folded board's city 1 is false for four of D4's five symmetries (for the owner of the plan).
- Deviations accepted: P4-A3 (data-moves stays ToolFrame's and T10's edits are data-out="edits" until ToolFrame gains a moveCount prop; j=coxeter accepted on A1; the move graph derived on the main thread in slices of at most 2000 moves, one task each, because no engine function computes a move graph for the worker; rings capped at 999999; names from names.json); P4-A5 (M6 opens on B2 as its figure table does; the names table without its 'Lists compared' column; the warning box of beat 7 in the prose column).
- Integration fix after the wave 4.3 check (2835 unit tests green; 615 browser tests passed, 3 failed): M0.spec.ts's beat-5 check now reads which modules are released from the frontmatter of site/src/content/modules/*.mdx instead of a hard-coded range, which every release broke. The two failures of T10's new long-task tests (65 and 56 ms during the E8 load, partly before hydration, and 79 ms after a ring click on B6) were measured at a load average of 11 on 4 processors with six agents running; they are rerun alone before the gate, and the gate runs with nothing else on the machine.
- Carried to wave 4.4: the timing tests of the site unit suite (T07's and T10's main-thread medians against 25 ms) fail when the whole suite runs in parallel (T10 measured 31.6 and 36.9 ms in parallel, 17.8 and 19.4 alone); an infrastructure agent moves them to a serial timing run beside the 25 ms orbit benchmark. T10's minor points (the ring cap stated in words; the four-shell link test taking its edge count from the engine, not the page) and M6's minor points (b5's board guarded by a test; the Seen statement on invariant forms with positive city weights, as SPEC 2.5 says; the B2 roots and coroots table not paired row by row; golden numbers in a challenge explanation through the golden file; the first caption in one sentence; the typed pins of M6.spec.ts) go to polish agents.
- Passed to the owner: M6's lab Total Blocking Time 237.5 ms on this container (the margin of M0 to M5 that the owner accepted at G3; G4 measures M6 and M7); P4-A3's measurement that wythoffEdges costs about eight times orbitGraph per item (the main-thread call closest to a long task at the E-9 limit); whether M7's shell links are "configurations that section 3 names" (T10 opens them with exact counts and the limit message, and a button draws the orbit in the worker); P4-A5's request for a warning-box style in the lesson layout.
