## 2026-10-17 P4-A6: repair of wave 4.4, predictions whose answers are not on screen
Decision: the reviewer's gap is real, and both predictions are replaced. (1) The question on `b4` asked how many vertices A3 has with nodes 1 and 3 ringed, but the table of solids sat above `b4` with the row (1, 0, 1), cuboctahedron, (12, 24, 14), and `b2` in beat 1 already showed A3 at (1, 0, 1) with 12 vertices and the name cuboctahedron. (2) The question on `b6` asked whether the two graphs share their edges, but its own figure prints "Shared with the other graph: 4" and six edges from the start, its graph switch (`graphMoves`) stays enabled while the prediction is open (T10 disables only the ring board, the picker and the ring buttons on `frozen`), and the prose and caption just above state six and four. What changed: `b4` now asks "If nodes 2 and 3 are ringed as well, how many vertices will the orbit have?" (answer `wythoff[type=A3;rings=1,1,1].f_vector[0]`, 24, through `gv`; the reveal explains that U is empty, so there is one vertex per element of the Weyl group, and names the polytope through `fmt`), and the table of solids moved from the prose of beat 2 into its figure column, after `b4`, as `<figure data-figure="solids">` with a figcaption (the pattern of M6's `roots-compared`, with M2's `margin: 0 16px` so it keeps the prose gutter on a phone). `b6` carries no prediction. The second prediction is on `b7` ("predict a yes or no"): "Rings on nodes 2 and 3 of B3 give edges of two lengths. If node 1 is ringed as well, will every edge have the same length?"; the correct option is computed from `types.B3.symmetrizer` as the number of distinct values of 2 d_i a_i^2 over the ringed nodes of (1, 1, 1) (4 and 2, so "No, two lengths"), and the reveal prints those values. Both questions are about a configuration that the reader reaches only by clicking nodes, and the ring board stays frozen until the reader answers or presses "Show me", so neither figure shows its answer while the question is open; nothing above `b7` states the edge lengths of (1, 1, 1) (the table of solids gives rings, names and face counts only). Reason: SPEC 8.1 P5 ("ask for a prediction before a reveal") and 8.5. Tests: `M7.spec.ts > a prediction asks about what is not yet on screen` (new: exactly `b4` and `b7` hold predictions; `b6` prints its shared edges at load with the graph switch enabled; for each prediction no row of the table of solids that would state the answer, no tool on that board with the target rings and no name of the target polytope comes before the question; the board is frozen at its start; after a right answer the reader's clicks reach the target and the figure shows the answer), and the updated `> predictions show a details fallback without JavaScript`, `> a prediction freezes its board until answered`, `> renders without JavaScript ...` (the table of solids follows `b4`, inside a figure of beat 2) and `> every figure states its expected result` (no "Show me" on `b6`; "Show me" on `b7` before its checks). Against the build of the branch as P4-A6 left it, these five tests failed; a temporary copy of the new test with the old question's target (A3 (1, 0, 1)) reported `rows: ["A3:1,0,1"], figures: ["b2"], named: ["cuboctahedron"]`, the three giveaways above; after the change all 25 tests of `M7.spec.ts` pass. The lesson measures 1769 words. The request to P4-A3 stands: an option to hold a figure's counts, face table, shared-edge line and graph switch while a prediction is open would let a lesson ask about the figure's own state, as the plan's suggestions on `b5`, `b6` and `b9` do.

In the old files a comment line names the new home; T10.test.ts also loses the imports and the two helpers that only the moved test used (`classify`, `classifiedBoard`, `countsOf`, `edgeArgs`, `edgesToIndices`, `fitsMainThread`, `otherFacts`, `median`, `timeMedian`). Checked and not moved, because none asserts on a duration: T03's `capped root test timing is measured` (reads `performance.now()`, prints the median and asserts only that seven runs were made, as P2-A2 instruction 5.10 asks: "printed and not asserted"), T15's positive-level sweep (`Date.now()`, printed), and the fixture build time of `content-components.test.ts` (`Date.now()`, stored, never asserted). A search of `site/tests/unit` for `performance.now`, `Date.now` and `hrtime` found no other timer. What the specification left open: where the Vitest timing assertions of SPEC 7.6 run. Default used: the orchestrator's instruction for this wave (MERGE, P4 wave 4.3: "an infrastructure agent moves them to a serial timing run beside the 25 ms orbit benchmark"). Reason: `npm test` and CI's `npm run check` run every file of both projects in parallel, and there T10's medians reached 31.6 and 36.9 ms against 25 (17.8 and 19.4 ms alone; decisions/P4-A3.md); a bound measured under the load of the suite itself tests the runner, not the tool.

## 2026-10-17 P4-X2: the timing configuration and the one command
Decision: `site/tests/timing/vitest.config.ts` (project name `site-timing`) sets `root` to the repository, `include: ['site/tests/timing/**/*.timing.test.ts']`, `environment: 'node'` and `fileParallelism: false` (one file at a time; Vitest then uses one worker), and takes its `resolve.alias` from the `site-unit` project of the root `vitest.config.ts` (it imports that file and throws if the project or its aliases are missing), so `@engine`, `@components` and `@lib` resolve in both runs from one definition. The root `vitest.config.ts` is not changed: its `site-unit` project includes only `site/tests/unit/**/*.test.ts` and its other project is `packages/engine`, so `npm test` does not collect a timing file. Root `package.json` gains `"bench": "npm run bench -w packages/engine && vitest run --config site/tests/timing/vitest.config.ts"`: the 25 ms orbit benchmark of SPEC 7.6 first (P3-A2's `packages/engine/test/bench/vitest.config.ts`, unchanged), then the site's timing files, each run alone. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gains the step `Timing checks, run alone` (`npm run bench`) right after `Type-check and test` (`npm run check`), so the benchmark that SPEC 7.6 asks to fail above 25 ms now runs on the runner (CI did not run it before). What the specification left open: the wiring of the benchmark and of the tools' main-thread bounds (7.6 names only the benchmark and its bound). Default used: the orchestrator's pre-approved script and step. Reason: one command runs every timing check alone, locally and in CI, and a failing timing check shows as its own CI step instead of inside the unit suite. The aliases are read rather than copied so that the two runs cannot drift apart.

Reason: the instruction asks to update the test that pins the set of root scripts; it named `scripts.test.ts`, but that file only checks names that must exist, and the exact set is pinned in `scaffold.test.ts` (recorded as a deviation in the report).

## 2026-10-17 P4-A3 (polish, wave 4.4): the ring cap stated in words, and said when a ring is held to it
Decision: the cap of the rings (`RING_MAX` = 999999, the entry of 2026-10-17 "the largest ring") is now stated in words beside the ring number fields of the table view, a line `A ring is a whole number from 0 to 999999, the largest the studio takes.` (`t10/scene.ts` `RING_CAP_TEXT`, element `data-out="ringLimit"`, the `aria-describedby` of every ring field). A ring asked for outside 0 to 999999 (a typed value, or Ring up and Ring down at the ends) is held to the range by `clampRing`, and the tool says so in that line and in the live region: `Node 3 held at 999999: a ring is a whole number from 0 to 999999, the largest the studio takes.` (and `held at 0` below the range; Ring down on an unringed node, which was silent, now says this). When the ring held to is the ring already, nothing changes in the history, the sentence is still said, and the field shows the ring again rather than the typed value (it kept the typed digits before). Any other change of rings or board clears the sentence. A link with a ring above the cap on a board the tool offers still loads the default state (6.6 Validation), and its notice now gives the reason after the codec's sentence: `The link could not be read, so the tool shows its default state. It asks for a ring of 1000000, more than 999999, the largest the studio takes.` (`t10/studio.ts` `linkNotice`); every other refusal keeps the codec's sentence alone. A ring beyond the safe integers never reaches the tool: the codec refuses it as a value that does not parse, with its own sentence (`site/src/lib/urlCodec.ts`, not T10's). What the specification left open: the wording (4.0 Stated limits fixes only that a limit is said in words; 6.6 that the notice says the link could not be read). Default used: none of section 10. Hooks: one data-out name was added, `ringLimit` (in `T10_OUTS`, the registry's `outs` and the comment block of `T10.spec.ts`); no property, preset id, existing data-out or data-control name changed, and `ToolFrame.svelte` was not touched. Tests, each failing before (on the code of the integration head, in Vitest and against a build of it) and passing after: unit `a ring asked for beyond the cap is held to it and said; the cap is stated in words`, `a link with a ring above the cap says why it fell back`, `the ring fields state the cap in words beside them` (server rendering); browser `the ring cap is stated beside the ring fields, and a ring held to it is said`, `a link with a ring above the cap loads the default and says why`.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A5: repair of wave 4.4, the lesson leaves the mathematics to the engine
Decision: the lesson's exports no longer compute a form, a coroot or a squared length of their own (CLAUDE.md, Rules: mathematics lives only in `packages/engine`, and the pages call it). What was wrong (adversarial review of wave 4.4): the polish of point (3) built the beat 4 table with a hand-written invariant form `formOf` (the double sum p^T D C p over `types.B2.cartan` and `types.B2.symmetrizer`) and a hand-written `corootOf`, although the engine exports `formValue` and `coroot`; the report listed no deviation. Now `COROOTS` takes each coroot from the engine, `inLabels(b, coroot(b, fromLabels(b, r)))` with `b = boardFromName('B2')`, the existing label helpers, and keeps the golden file only for the roots (`types.B2.positive_roots`) and the lookup of the third column (`types.C2.positive_roots`); `B2D`, `B2C`, `formOf` and `corootOf` are gone. The built table is unchanged: (0, 1) with x2 and (0, 1); (1, 0) with x1 and (1, 0); (1, 1) with 2x1 + x2 and (2, 1); (1, 2) with x1 + x2 and (1, 1), the last two marked `data-differs`.
The same rule applied to the explanation of the G2 builder challenge (b13), which made the same kind of value by hand: the squared length `2 * d_2` and the road weight `d_1 * A_21` from `types.G2.adjacency` (the reviewer's grep of the exports matches that line too). The squared lengths, which are the passing city weights, now come from the engine (`squaredLengths('G2')`, `ratToString(formValue(b, e_i))` for each unit population, in label order); the road weight is printed as the weight of city 2, which is what the sentence before it says it must equal; the halves are the weights `types.G2.symmetrizer` as printed, with no arithmetic. The text the reader sees is unchanged ("squared length 6, ...: 2, 6 and 6, or 1, 3 and 3"), and `challenge: weights on G2 that the builder accepts` still checks it against `2 d_i` and `2 d_1 A_21` computed in the test from the golden file, so the road weight is verified independently and the builder is shown to accept it. Left as they are, since they compute nothing from a board's matrices or weights: `SHORT_ROOTS = 2 * types.B2.n_short_positive` (the short root populations with their negatives) and the round numbering of the script captions (two moves to a round). The test file keeps its own `formValue` and `corootOf` helpers: they compute the expected values from the golden file, independently of the engine, which is what makes the beat 4 test a check of the engine's coroots on the page.
Test: `M6.spec.ts > the lesson leaves the form, the coroots and the squared lengths to the engine` (source inspection): no export writes a sum (`.reduce(`); no `gv` or `resolveGoldenPath` of a golden Cartan matrix or adjacency; no golden symmetrizer, or export bound to one, in a product or a quotient; `coroot` and `formValue` imported from `@engine`; `COROOTS` calls `coroot(`; the b13 explanation uses an export that rests on `formValue`. Committed first, it failed on the lesson as merged (at the hand-written sum) and passes after. Break checks, each restored from a saved copy: the squared length written back as `2 * G2D[1]` fails it (the weights in a product); a coroot table without the engine fails it (at a hand-written sum, then, with no arithmetic at all, at "the coroot table"). Word count 1769, unchanged (no prose changed). `scripts/numbers-in-prose.mjs`: 0 numbers. `RELEASE=1` build: `open item E16 used on /modules/m6-multiple-edges/ (fallback shown)`, as before. Browser runs used a scratch copy of `playwright.config.ts` on port 5000 (never committed). What the specification left open: nothing. Default used: none.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-engine: the order of the guards of affineExtension (P3-RT-03-06)
Decision: `affineExtension` checks the kind of board (`NeedsTwoWayRoads`), then the class (`NotFiniteType` on every board that is not of finite type, in one piece or several), then connectedness (`NeedsConnectedBoard`, reached only by a finite board in several pieces). What the specification left open: SPEC 5.6 gives `affineExtension` `NotFiniteType` on every `gcm` board that is not of finite type and does not list it among the functions that need a connected board (E-10 names only `cartanClass`, `identify`, `identifyAffine`); it says nothing about a finite board in several pieces. Default used (SPEC section 10): none. The P2-A1 entries "`requireFinite` on a disconnected board" (connectedness for `affineExtension`, stated gap: a disconnected finite board) and "the order of checks inside each function" (connectedness "where required" before class) are kept for the case they were written for; for a board that is not of finite type the order now follows the 5.6 row, as the opening paragraph of COMMON.md requires when the plan and SPEC.md seem to disagree. Reason: SPEC 5.6 row `affineExtension` and E-10; the guard order of `guards.ts` (`requireFinite` itself checks kind, then class). The assertion of `guards.test.ts` row 8 that pinned `NeedsConnectedBoard` on the board "two components: A2 and the triangle" now asserts `NotFiniteType`; the finite board in several pieces keeps its `NeedsConnectedBoard` assertion in row 10.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-engine: request, the return type of symbolicPlay (P3-RT-01-01, needs-decision)
Decision: no source change; the request below is for the owner or `MERGE`. What the specification left open: CONTRACTS.md 2.4 phase 3 fixes `symbolicPlay(board, word): number[][]`, while SPEC 5.6 (second table, first row) says `symbolicPlay` works on one-way boards and on `gcm` boards that are not of finite type, and E-2 gives those boards `Big` because their entries grow without bound. On `[[2,-3],[-3,2]]` in `Big` the oracle's `symbolic_play` returns `(23416728348467685, 8944394323791464)` at move 40, which a `number` cannot hold; the engine throws `EngineOverflow` (the plan reading recorded as "P3-A2: the overflow rule of symbolicPlay"). Default used (SPEC section 10): none. Options: (a) CONTRACTS.md 2.4 becomes `symbolicPlay(board: Board<number>, word): number[][]; symbolicPlay(board: Board<bigint>, word): bigint[][]; symbolicPlay(board: AnyBoard, word): number[][] | bigint[][]` (the forms in the board's arithmetic, as the preamble of 2.4 says of vectors); a prototype made the reproducer pass with the engine type-check clean, and needs two follow-on edits: `packages/engine/test/golden/p3b-kinds.test.ts` (two tests on `Big` boards expect numbers, for example `[[1, 0], [1, 1]]`, and would expect `[[1n, 0n], [1n, 1n]]`) and `site/src/components/tools/T07SymbolicGame.svelte` lines 155 and 156 (`symbolicView` passes the result to `matchRows` and stores it as `number[][]`; T7 offers only the 32 named types, so a narrowing to `Board<number>` suffices); (b) keep `number[][]` and the owner amends the first row of the second table of SPEC 5.6 to say that `symbolicPlay` throws `EngineOverflow` when a coefficient passes 2^53, after which the reproducer is rewritten to expect the throw. Reason for recommending (a): E-2 gives these boards `Big` so that exact values survive, the oracle returns them at every length, and the only reader path (T7 on named finite boards) keeps its numbers.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-garden-follow: where the phase-3 orbit total lives (P3-RT-01-02, P3-RT-03-02, P3-RT-06-01)
Decision: the rule of SPEC 4 T2 Interactions "from phase 3 on a finite-type board it gives the exact size from `orbitSize`" is applied in `gardenTotal` (`site/src/components/tools/t02/phase2.ts`), not in `totalOf` (`t02/garden.ts`) as the P1-A6 entry "counter without a total" foresaw ("`totalOf` is the one function phase 3 changes for `orbitSize`"). What the specification left open: the file. Reason: the finite test from phase 2 is the verdict of `classify` (SPEC 5.6), which P2-A7 placed in `phase2.ts` beside the roots total; `totalOf` keeps the phase-1 answer of the bounded search, which `gardenTotal` returns off the finite types. The component already calls `gardenTotal` for the counter and its announcement. The comment of `totalOf` now points there.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-garden-follow: an orbit size beyond the safe integers
Decision: when `orbitSize` throws `EngineOverflow` (its group order is formed in SafeInt; on twelve nodes it fits, E-4, but a named board such as `A20` reached by a link has an order beyond 2^53), the counter gives what the bounded search gives ("more than 2000", or the count when the search completed). What the specification left open: SPEC 4 T2 names no case where `orbitSize` cannot answer. Default used: none in section 10 applies. Reason: the statement stays true and no number is rounded (E-1); the `Total` type of T2 holds a `number`.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-garden-follow: a population's orbit size (P3-RT-01-02)
Decision: in the mutation game the orbit of a population p is measured by `orbitSize` of its numbers a = Cp (`toNumbers`). What the specification left open: SPEC 5.4 gives `orbitSize` on positions of the numbers game only. Reason: a mutation changes Cp exactly as the firing of that node changes a (SPEC 2.4), and C is invertible on a finite-type board, so the two orbits have the same size (the oracle agrees: `orbit_size(C, -end)` for the A6 population (2, 1, 5, 1, 3, 8) is 2520).

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-garden-follow: followers keep the followed play (P3-RT-04-04)
Decision: a T2 or T3 following a T1 through `linkTo` keeps the positions of the followed play and, on each published state with the same board, coords and start, applies only the moves after the longest shared prefix (`extendPlay` in `t02/follow.ts`, `followPlay` in `t03/model.ts`); another board, coords or start is a load, replayed from the start. Both subscriptions run untracked inside their effect. This supersedes, on this point, the P2-A2 entry "the meter reads `positionsOf` of the last published state" and the reading of the P1-A2 entry "a follower recomputes its own positions" as a replay per move; positions still never travel between tools. What the specification left open: nothing (SPEC 7.4 "extends it by one position per move"); recorded because two builder entries said otherwise. Reason: SPEC 7.4; E-9 rule 2.

Decision: after hydration the `disabled` prop of `Board.svelte` no longer sets the HTML `disabled` attribute on the node buttons; it sets `aria-disabled="true"`, and activating a node calls no `onAct`. The roving tab stop, the arrow keys, the A-3 names (`cannot be fired` and the city states) and `data-can-act="false"` stay as they were, so a reader on the keyboard reaches the board and its values while the prediction waits, and no move is made. Before hydration the buttons are still rendered with `disabled` (7.4). The same holds while a tool passes `disabled` for a running play (`busy` in T1 and T9). What the specification left open: what "frozen" means for the board (4.0 Predict); A-1 and 4.0 "Each board is one tab stop" fix that the board keeps its tab stop. This replaces the words "and disabled" in the entry "2026-10-10 P1-A5: prediction check" for the node buttons; History and the step controls stay disabled while frozen. Reason: SPEC 7.5 A-1, 4.0 Keyboard and screen reader, 4.0 Predict, 7.4. No prop or hook name changed (CONTRACTS 5.1).

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-board-a11y: T16's import status spoken through the frame's region (P3-RT-05-07)
Decision: `data-out="importStatus"` (T16) and `data-out="counterStatus"` (T4) are ordinary text without `role="status"`, so each tool has the one polite live region of A-4, ToolFrame's `[data-live]`. T16BoardIO takes an optional `announce` prop (a component inside T16, not a contract of CONTRACTS 5) and speaks each status line through it; T16Sandbox passes `FrameApi.announce`. T4 already announced "Counting in the background." through the frame, so its line only loses the role. The wording recorded by P2-A5 ("The board could not be read: <reason>. The board was left as it was." and "Board imported: N nodes. The play starts again from the default start.") and both `data-out` names are unchanged; the parenthesis "(role status, ordinary text, no announcement of my own)" of the entry "2026-10-10 P2-A5: JSON import and export of boards" now reads "(ordinary text, announced through the tool's live region)". What the specification left open: nothing; A-4 fixes one polite live region per tool and 4.0 has that region announce each result. Reason: SPEC 7.5 A-4, 4.0 Keyboard and screen reader, CONTRACTS 5.1 ToolFrame.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-layout: which of the two edge-to-edge rules a lesson's tool figure keeps (P3-RT-05-01)
Decision: below 400 pixels Lesson.astro runs a figure at `100vw` only when the figure holds no ToolFrame (`[data-anatomy='figure']:not(:has([data-frame]))`); a figure that holds a tool stays in the page column and ToolFrame alone moves the tool's `<figure>` out by the page gutter, exactly as on the tool routes. The tool's title, controls and share row then keep the page gutter on lessons too. Static figures keep the `100vw` rule of decisions/P1-A4.md. A browser without `:has()` drops the whole rule, so its static figures stay in the column and no page scrolls sideways (the other order, an override that undoes the 100vw rule for tools, would bring the double breakout back in such a browser). What the specification left open: SPEC 6.1 [D] says only that the box runs from edge to edge below 400 pixels; the finding offered either rule. Default used: none (6.1 applied as written; decisions/P0-A5.md "negative margins equal to the page gutter" and decisions/P1-A4.md "100vw below 400 pixels" both still hold, each for its own kind of figure). Reason: the gutter-based rule is exact (`100vw` also counts a classic scrollbar), and it keeps lessons and tool routes alike.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-layout: the width of a number prediction's answer field (P3-RT-05-01)
Decision: Predict.svelte gives the answer field of a number question `width: 8em` (class `number`), the width T05 had set locally for the same reason. With the browser's default width (about 20 characters) the label and the field could not be narrower than about 323 pixels, so a tool with a prediction was 349 pixels wide; this was hidden on lessons while the figure was 352 pixels wide and failed the adopted test once the figure was 320. Position answers keep `size="4"`. What the specification left open: the field's width. Default used: none. Reason: A-11 (controls reflow at 320), 4.0 "Small screens".

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-layout: a lesson's Sources entries wrap long tokens (P3-RT-05-01)
Decision: Sources.astro sets `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on each entry, as Page.astro already does for the credits table. The W-MS entry holds the unbroken file name `MutationGameCoxeterGraphs.pdf`, which reached x = 326 at 320 pixels on M0, M1, M3, M4 and M5; like the Predict field it was hidden inside the wider figure and surfaced with the fix. What the specification left open: nothing beyond A-11. Reason: A-11.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-layout: every display formula of <Tex> is a tab stop (P3-RT-05-04)
Decision: Tex.astro adds `tabindex="0"` to the outer `<span class="katex-display">` of every display formula it typesets, and base.css gives it the focus ring of A-2. No role and no accessible name are added: on focus a screen reader reads the MathML inside (A-12), which an `aria-label` would replace. Every display box is a stop, not only those that scroll, because which formulas overflow depends on the reader's width and fonts and the layouts ship no script (P1-A4); this follows the scroll boxes of T7 (decisions/P3-A5.md, `tabindex="0"`). The build fails if KaTeX's display output no longer starts with that span, so that the stop cannot vanish silently on a KaTeX upgrade. Not taken: a script that marks only overflowing boxes (a script on every lesson); splitting the one formula of M2 beat 2 (the content would fit, the cause would stay). What the specification left open: how a scrolling formula is made keyboard operable (A-11, WCAG 2.1.1). Default used: none. Reason: SPEC 7.7 (no serious axe violation), A-11.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-layout: display formulas reflow at their break points, on every page (P3-RT-05-11)
Decision: the rule `.katex-display { overflow-x: auto; overflow-y: hidden }` moves from Lesson.astro and Page.astro to base.css, which every page loads, and display formulas may break between the pieces KaTeX already makes at the relations and binary operators of a formula's outer level (`.katex-display > .katex > .katex-html { white-space: normal }`), as TeX breaks an inline formula; a piece still wider than the column scrolls inside the formula's own box. The break is needed, not only the box: a formula written with `$$` goes through rehype-katex, cannot carry the tab stop of `<Tex display>`, and with the box alone a11y.spec.ts reported scrollable-region-focusable (serious) on /dev/math-check/ at 360. Wider than the column and without a break point, a `$$` formula would still be a box without a tab stop; giving those a stop needs a rehype step, which changes the mathematics route of decisions/P0-A3.md (left to the owner, see the report's open questions). What the specification left open: how a display formula reflows (A-11). Default used: none. Reason: A-11 on every route, SPEC 7.7.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-drawing: how the `roads` style draws more roads than the editor allows
Decision: `roadGlyphs` (site/src/components/draw/roads.ts) draws every road as its own curved arrow up to `CAPS.roadsEachWay` (4) roads in one direction, as before. A direction with more roads is drawn as one arrow (the first curve of that direction) with its count written beyond the apex in `var(--text)`, glyph kind `counted`. A pair therefore has at most 2 x 4 glyphs whatever its counts. What the specification left open: 6.2 [D] says that in the `roads` style "every road is drawn as its own curved arrow", and 5.6 caps the roads between two nodes at 4 each way in the editor only ("the engine accepts any count"); neither says how a board from outside the editor (a link with a `c:` matrix, which the codec accepts with any safe count) is drawn. Followed literally, 6.2 drew 2^53 - 1 arrows and hung the page (P3-RT-03-01). Reason: the orchestrator's direction for this cluster (the drawing must be bounded); the threshold is the editor's cap, so 6.2 holds unchanged for every board the editor, a preset or the golden file can produce (at most 4 roads each way), and the device for "too many to draw" is the one 6.2 already uses for the thick line of the `dynkin` style, which writes the counts as numbers. The `dynkin` style is unchanged (its glyphs were bounded already).

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-drawing: T16's import refuses more than four roads each way
Decision: the JSON import of the sandbox (T16BoardIO.svelte, `customSpec`) refuses a custom board with more than `CAPS.roadsEachWay` roads from one node to another, after the checks P2-A5 recorded, with the reason "it has N roads from node A to node B, and the editor allows at most 4 each way" inside P2-A5's sentence "The board could not be read: <reason>. The board was left as it was." The labels are the board's (`toLabels`, E-7). This extends the list of refusals in decisions/P2-A5.md ("JSON import and export of boards") by one item; nothing else of that entry changes, and the BoardEditor contract of decisions/P2-A3.md (props, `maxRoads` at most 4, the road selects 0 to 4) is untouched. What the specification left open: whether the editor's cap of 5.6 applies to an import. Reason: SPEC 4 T16 makes the sandbox "The editor of T4", T4's editor has "between two nodes, 0 to 4 roads each way", and 6.6 Validation refuses "a board that the tool does not offer"; both verifiers of P3-RT-03-01 read the texts the same way.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-codec-state: the 12-node cap on board names (P3-RT-04-01)
Decision: `resolveBoard` (site/src/lib/state.ts) refuses every board with more than 12 nodes, named or custom, with the RangeError "board has more than 12 nodes" that a custom matrix already got. A finite name (`A13`) and an untwisted affine name (`A12~`) are refused from their text (rank, plus one for `~`) before `boardFromName` builds a matrix, so that a 512-character name such as `A999...9` cannot freeze the tab; every other name (twisted, `pentagon`, `square`) is checked on the board built, which the engine bounds at 13 nodes. The codec turns the error into rule 2 as before; T16's import of a name above the cap gives the error's text as its reason ("The board could not be read: board has more than 12 nodes. The board was left as it was.") instead of "no board is named". What the specification left open: nothing on the rule (SPEC 6.6 Validation applies the 12 nodes to every board; CONTRACTS 4); the wording of T16's reason is plan wording. Reason: SPEC 6.6, 2.8 scope, CONTRACTS 4 resolveBoard comment.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-codec-state: a T5 link whose lambda the calculator cannot take (P3-RT-04-03)
Decision: a T5 link whose `d` parses but has a negative entry or an entry of more than 6 digits loads the whole default state with the notice, as every other link T5 cannot read does. This replaces the part of the entry "P3-A3: legal play only, and the link rules" (decisions/P3-A3.md) that kept the link's board, start and moves, reset lambda and showed the notice. What the specification left open: SPEC 6.6 allows only two outcomes, the whole link with no notice or the whole default state with the notice; the finding's two options were these. Default used: the second, because it keeps P3-A3's recorded rule that such a `d` is a link T5 cannot read (and its cap of 6 digits on a link), keeps every existing T5 test green (`a link is read with the legal rule and the lambda cap`), and needs no new behaviour in the calculator for a lambda it refuses. Reason: SPEC 6.6 Validation; finding P3-RT-04-03.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-codec-state: one board object per spec while a tool loads (P3-RT-03-05)
Decision: `resolveBoard` keeps the boards it builds in a map from the spec (a name, or the matrix as JSON; `xy` does not count) to the board object, and empties the map in a microtask queued when the first board of a run of synchronous code is stored. A tool loads its board in one such run (its script and its first render), so every `resolveBoard` of one spec in that load returns one frozen object and the verdict cache (keyed by object, E-11) is hit: one `classify` per load. What the specification left open: the scope of the reuse (SPEC 5.6 says "once when the board is loaded"; E-11 keys the cache by board object). Not chosen: a map kept for the life of the page, which the orchestrator's note suggested ("one board object per spec"). It would make the adopted reproducer read 0 calls instead of 1 for the triangle, which `t02/presets.ts` resolves when the module loads, so the reproducer (unchanged, as RT-FIX 5.3 requires) could not pass; it would also need a bound of its own choosing on the number of boards kept. Consequence for an existing test: `site/tests/unit/tools/T04.test.ts > T4 verdict > buildVerdict calls classify once per board object` obtained a second board object by resolving the same spec again in the same synchronous run, which now returns the same object; the test now awaits the end of the run first (`await Promise.resolve()`), and its assertion (`not.toBe(first)`) is unchanged. Reason: SPEC 5.6 last sentence, E-11; finding P3-RT-03-05.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-codec-state: the values of T9's caption of 2.12 (P3-RT-02-02)
Decision: T9's `EndPanel` (site/src/components/tools/t09/kostant.ts) gains two fields, `markedNumbers` and `markedReturnCounts`: the final number and the return count of each marked city, in the order of `marked`, read by `endPanel` at the position `toPositions` gives. `affineCaptionParts` pairs them with the marked labels instead of reading index `label - 1` of a list in label order. The data export of T9 names its fields one by one, so it does not change. What the specification left open: the shape of a tool's internal module (plan). Reason: E-7, "nothing else converts"; finding P3-RT-02-02.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-export-long: a rational whose denominator is 1 is an integer in the data export
Decision: `jsonSafe` in `site/src/lib/export.ts` hands the numerator of a `{ num, den: 1n }` rational to the integer rule of SPEC 6.6, so it is a JSON number when safe and decimal text otherwise; a fraction stays `p/q`. This replaces the words "`{ num, den }` rationals become `p/q` or `p`" of the entry "2026-10-10 P1-A2: data export". What the specification left open: whether an integral rational counts among the "integers" of 6.6 (SPEC 5.3 types the form values and the d_i as rationals, and 5.7 rule 4 stores the golden `form_value` as text, but that rule governs `golden_data.json`, not the export). Default used: none of section 10; the reading of the round's orchestrator ("rationals with denominator 1 are integers") and of the entry "2026-10-10 P1-A5: Download data" (safe integers as numbers, fractions as `p/q`). Reason: P3-RT-04-02, confirmed by both verifiers.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-export-long: tools hand values, not display text, to the export
Decision: T3, T4 and T6 convert the exact texts they show back to export values at their `derivedForExport` boundary: an integer text by the integer rule (T3 and T6 through their existing `exportValue`, T4 through `integerOut` of `lib/state.ts`), a fraction text as `p/q`. T3's small values export a typed whole number as that integer and an unfinished entry (empty, or a lone minus sign) as its text, which is not an integer. The display texts and every hook are unchanged. Reason: P3-RT-04-05 and the T6 half of P3-RT-04-02 (SPEC 6.6).

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-export-long: existing expectations that recorded the old export
Decision: two assertions written before the round expected integers as text and were changed to the integer rule: `site/tests/unit/export.test.ts` (`{ num: -4n, den: 1n }` exports as `-4`, was `'-4'`) and `site/tests/e2e/T04.spec.ts` (the star's weights export as `1`, was `'1'`). Both stay exact comparisons. Reason: they encoded the behaviour the two findings report; no assertion was removed or loosened.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-export-long: T5's long dimension (SPEC 4.0 Long numbers)
Decision: above 12 digits `data-out="dimension"` shows T1's `shortValue` (`1.0000... x 10^120 (121 digits)`), with the exact digits in its `title` (the tooltip), in the table view (the M / D cell, now marked `data-table-exact="dimension"` beside the existing `D` and `M`) and in the export (unchanged, a decimal string). In the MathML formula a long dimension is the same shortened text in an `<mtext>` (it is not a numeral); up to 12 digits it stays an `<mn>`. The live region's sentence `Dimension N.` keeps the exact digits: the live region is visually hidden and the A-4 sentences speak exact values, so nothing is "shown" there. What the specification left open: how a formula and a spoken sentence carry a long number. Reason: P3-RT-03-03.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-test-integrity: a scroll is not the first interaction for the worker check
Decision: `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` scrolls every island still waiting into view, in document order, and waits until Astro has hydrated it (`site/tests/e2e/hydrate.ts`), before it reads the worker requests; a scripted scroll is not counted as the reader's first interaction. What the specification left open: 7.6 [D] says the islands other than the first hydrate "when scrolled into view (client:visible)" and that the worker's script "is fetched when a tool first needs it. No tool may need it for its default state" (E-9 repeats the last sentence); it does not say whether a scroll is an interaction. Default used: none of section 10; the reading of P3-RT-06-02, which both verifiers accepted: the rule covers every tool of the page, not only those of the first screen. Reason: a client:visible island hydrates into its default state when it is scrolled into view and acts on nothing. The budget of `npm run size` ("without touching the page") is not changed by this: it still measures only what is fetched before any scroll. This replaces the comment of P1-A2's spec ("the others wait for a scroll, which would be touching the page"), which no decisions entry recorded.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-test-integrity: the worker check waits for the tools of an island
Decision: after the scroll, `worker-on-demand.spec.ts` waits for `data-hydrated="true"` on every tool root inside an `astro-island`, and fails when an island keeps the attribute `ssr`. What the specification left open: nothing; the test's own wording ("left alone until every tool has hydrated"). Reason: the stand-in root of `dev/lesson-check` (MERGE, phase 1 stage 1.1) is static markup, not an island, so nothing hydrates it; the previous spec asked `data-hydrated` only of the tools in the first screen, which the stand-in is not in.

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-test-integrity: the default figure of the run without JavaScript
Decision: `nojs.spec.ts` accepts as the default figure of a tool root only the tool's own drawing, outside the table view's `<details>` and any `<noscript>`: a board (a `data-board-group` holding `data-node` buttons, drawn by an `<svg>` with content beside it), a drawing of role `img` with content and a name (`aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `<title>`), or a snapshot `<img>` with a source and an `alt`. Any other `<svg>` (a decorative icon) no longer counts. Every node button of a game board carries the four hooks of 4.0 with a non-empty `data-value`; the nodes of the board editor (inside `data-editor`), which show no value (their `data-value` is empty by design), are left out of that check. A prediction's `<details>` must hold a non-empty `.answer-text` (the answer, as the lesson specs read it) and a non-empty reveal. What the specification left open: 7.4 asks the run to check that "every default figure" is present, and 4.0 Predict that the `<details>` holds "the answer and the reveal"; neither names a selector. This strengthens the predicate recorded in "2026-10-10 P1-A2: the run without JavaScript" (a builder's record, not a [D] choice or a default of section 10, as the refuting verifier of P3-RT-06-03 found). Reason: P3-RT-06-03. The entries of DECISIONS.md that made T2's one-vertex garden and T3's linked meter pass the old predicate stay satisfied: both tools have a named drawing of role `img` (the layer chart, the meter dial).

## 2026-10-17 RT-FIX-P3-test-integrity: the default state's values, server markup against the hydrated default
Decision: a sweep test in `nojs.spec.ts`, "the server markup shows the default state's values", compares, for every route and every tool root, the hooks read without JavaScript with those of the hydrated default (every island scrolled into view through `hydrate.ts`, nothing clicked, polled until equal): `data-moves`, the four node hooks of every `data-node`, the caption, and on the tools of ToolFrame (`data-frame`) the text of every `data-out`, all outside `<noscript>`. A root outside ToolFrame is compared without its `data-out`. What the specification left open: how the run checks that "the default state of every tool is rendered to HTML and SVG at build time" (4.0). Reason: P3-RT-06-03 ("it checks that elements exist, not the default state") and its proposed fix. The drawing-time probe of `dev/draw-time` (P4-A2's entry; not a tool of section 4, no `data-frame`) fills its timing outputs only when it hydrates, by design, so outputs are compared on the tools of ToolFrame only.

## 2026-10-17 MERGE: red-team round 1, group A fixes
Decision: the eight fix branches of group A (26 confirmed findings of lenses RT-01 to RT-06 over phases 0 to 3) were merged in cluster order with --no-ff merge commits: engine, garden-follow, board-a11y, layout, drawing, codec-state, export-long, test-integrity. Each cluster was fixed by one agent and checked by a second agent that reran the lens reproducer on the fixed branch. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: RED-TEAM-COMMON.md 7.
- Fixed and confirmed (20): P3-RT-01-02, P3-RT-03-02 and P3-RT-06-01 (one cause: T2's orbit total comes from orbitSize on finite-type boards), P3-RT-04-04 (a follower extends the play it keeps), P3-RT-03-06, P3-RT-05-01, P3-RT-05-02, P3-RT-05-03, P3-RT-05-04, P3-RT-05-07, P3-RT-05-11, P3-RT-03-01, P3-RT-04-01, P3-RT-04-03, P3-RT-03-05, P3-RT-02-02, P3-RT-04-02, P3-RT-04-05, P3-RT-03-03, P3-RT-06-02, P3-RT-06-03.
- Conflicts resolved: site/tests/e2e/T16.spec.ts (board-a11y and drawing each added a test to the regressions block: both kept); site/src/components/tools/T03FormFinder.svelte (the imports of garden-follow, followPlay and FollowedPlay, and of export-long, entryValue: both kept); site/tests/e2e/T03.spec.ts and T04.spec.ts (both sides' regression tests kept in one block).
- Needs a decision from the owner (no source change): P3-RT-01-01, the return type of symbolicPlay (CONTRACTS.md 2.4 fixes number[][], while SPEC 5.6 says the function works on boards off the finite types, where entries outgrow the safe integers; the fixer proposes an overload that returns bigint[][] on Big boards); P3-RT-05-06, the polygon radius 133 rather than 130 at a width of 320 (fixing it would overturn the recorded P1-A5 deviation "polygon drawing scaled with the square box").
- Taken up by the orchestrator with the deferred four of group B (the fixer left them because no fix of the cluster touched their files): P3-RT-05-08 (changedOnly with an empty clause reads "Fired node 3. . Move 1."; recommendation: omit the empty clause), P3-RT-05-09 (answering a prediction drops the focus to the body; recommendation: aria-disabled on the controls that are done), P3-RT-05-10 (T7's Letters panel of the numbers game carries the Populations icon; recommendation: the Numbers icon and accent).
- Side effects accepted: the guard order of affineExtension now checks the class before connectedness on boards that are not of finite type (a new decision beside P2-A1's "order of checks"); one existing expected value in guards.test.ts changed with it, since it pinned the confirmed bug; the RT-03 export-completeness test was not adopted, since phase 4 added fourteen exports with no row in the error matrix (a request to the phase-4 engine owner for those rows stands).
- Checks on the merged head (0f639eb): npm ci, npm test (110 files, 2887 tests), typecheck, build and size (32 routes, 0 over) pass; npm run e2e has 747 passed and 4 failed, all in nojs.spec.ts as strengthened by P3-RT-06-03, on the phase-4 routes that the fixer's base did not hold: M6's tool 9 (T11) has no default figure or text alternative in the server markup; M7's first prediction has no answer in its <details>; T10 on M7 and on /tools/geometry/ hydrates with source, sharedEdges and drawMs filled where the server markup leaves them empty. The test is right on the first three outputs; drawMs is a measured time that no build can render. Sent to the deferred fix round, after wave 4.5 (which polishes M7) is merged.

## 2026-10-17 P4-A6: polish of wave 4.4, the seven minor points of the M7 review

## 2026-10-17 P4-X2 (polish, wave 4.5): the guard reads every clock and follows each reading to its assertion
Decision: the guard of wave 4.4 looked in `site/tests/unit` for the text of the performance timer only, so an assertion on a duration read from the Date clock or from the process's high-resolution timer would have passed it. `describe('timing checks run alone')` of `site/tests/unit/scripts.test.ts` now has seven tests (three of wave 4.4, one of them rewritten, and four new):
- The clocks are the performance timer (`performance.now`), the Date clock (`Date.now`, and `new Date()` with no argument) and the process's high-resolution timer under any spelling (`process.hrtime()`, `process.hrtime.bigint()`, or `hrtime` imported from `node:process`), matched as patterns written in pieces so that the guard file does not count as reading one.
- "every clock reading left in the parallel suite is listed, with its reason": the files of `site/tests/unit` and of the engine's parallel suite (every file under `packages/engine/test` except `bench/`) that mention a clock, with the exact number of mentions, equal a fixed list: site `content-components.test.ts` 2 (the fixture build's time, kept in its result and never asserted), `lab-thresholds.test.ts` 1 (the text of the canary page, compared as a string), `tools/T03.test.ts` 2 (printed, P2-A2 instruction 5.10), `tools/T15.test.ts` 2 (printed, decisions/P2-A4.md); engine `golden/p3b-complete-games.test.ts` 2 (the limit of the runner, next entry) and `golden/p4-wythoff.test.ts` 2 (printed). Any new reading of any clock in either project, in any form, fails this test, so a new duration assertion is caught even where the search below could not follow it.
- "no assertion of the parallel suite rests on a clock reading, but the one limit of the runner that guards a hang": a search over the code of each file (comments, strings, regular expressions and the text of template literals blanked, the `${}` parts kept) finds every `expect(...)` or `assert...(...)` whose argument reads a clock or a name that holds a reading. A name holds one in the block where it is declared when it is assigned from such an expression (including `x[k] = ...`, `+=`, destructuring and a for-of over it), when such an expression is pushed or added into it, or, for a function, when it returns one; a property that receives one (`{ ms: ... }`, `build.ms = ...`, the shorthand `{ ms }`) holds it wherever it is read as `.ms`; keywords are never names; a list's length (`toHaveLength`) is not a duration. It finds nothing in `site/tests/unit`, and in the engine's parallel suite exactly `expect(elapsed).toBeLessThan(60000);` of `p3b-complete-games.test.ts`, whose comment right above must say "a limit of the runner that guards a hang, not a timing check".
- "the search for assertions on a clock finds the bound of every timing file": the search finds the bound of each of the four timing files (`T07.timing.test.ts`, `T10.timing.test.ts` through its helper `timeMedian` and a for-of over the medians, `orbit.bench.ts`, `weyl-dimension.bench.ts`), and gives the expected count on thirteen fragments written in the test (each binding it follows; a clock in a string, a comment or a template literal; a name of the same spelling in another test; a destructured reading beside `as const`).
- "the E8 timing of the roster runs in the engine bench run, unchanged in name and bound, and no longer in npm test": next entry.
- "each moved test lives in its timing file and no longer in the parallel suite" keeps its wave 4.4 checks; its T03-only part moved into the list above.
Limits, stated: the search does not follow a reading through a function imported from another file, through an `if (...) throw`, or through a value returned as an object from a function and read as a whole; the exact list of clock readings above covers those, since each needs a reading in the scanned files. What the specification left open: how the separation of SPEC 7.6's timing checks from the parallel suite is kept. Default used: the review's point (1) of wave 4.4. Reason: a guard that names one clock leaves the others open.

## 2026-10-17 P4-X2 (polish, wave 4.5): the engine's two duration assertions, one timing check and one limit of the runner
Decision: `p3a-weyl-dimension.test.ts > E8 3875 under one second > weylDimension(E8, [1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]) computes in under a second` (`expect(ms).toBeLessThan(1000)`) is a timing check, and moves unchanged in describe, name, body and bound to `packages/engine/test/bench/weyl-dimension.bench.ts`, which the engine's bench configuration (`test/bench/vitest.config.ts`, include `test/bench/**/*.bench.ts`, one file at a time) collects, so `npm run bench -w packages/engine`, the root `npm run bench` and the CI step `Timing checks, run alone` run it beside the orbit benchmark, and `npm test` no longer does. Its source is not a sentence of SPEC but the phase-3 roster's acceptance for P3-A1 ("weylDimension of E8 lambda for 3875 computes in under a second", "measured with `performance.now()`", gate G3); SPEC F9 asks only for the value. It is a timing check and not a guard against a hang: weylDimension plays one complete game of 120 moves on E8 and replays its word, which cannot run on, and a call slower than Vitest's own limit of 5 s a test fails as a timeout anyway; the 1 s bound is five times tighter than that limit, so it states a speed and measures the load of the parallel suite as much as the engine. The bigint test that shared its describe stays in `p3a-weyl-dimension.test.ts` under the describe `E8 3875 needs bigint` (the old name would describe a timing it no longer holds); the 30 records of `weyl_dimension`, 3875 for E8 among them, stay there with `covers('weyl_dimension')`. `p3b-complete-games.test.ts > allCompleteGames > plays that do not end` (`expect(elapsed).toBeLessThan(60000)`) is a limit of the runner that guards a hang and stays in `npm test`, with a comment above it saying so: P3-A2's acceptance asks that the search on `A2~` from all ones stop "in bounded time" (the oracle recurses there until Python's recursion limit; RT-03 item 26 names "a hang" as the failure), SPEC gives `allCompleteGames` no time, and Vitest's limit of 5 s a test (the engine project sets no other) is tighter and binds first. Measured in this worktree (Vitest's time for each test): the E8 test 1 ms in `npm test` before the move and 9 ms in the bench run after; the `A2~` test 32 ms. What the specification left open: whether each of the two bounds is a timing check of 7.6's kind or a limit of the runner. Default used: the review's point (2) of wave 4.4, which offered the two readings; the MERGE line of wave 4.4 called both "limits of the runner", and this entry decides the 1 s bound the other way for the reason above. Reason: a bound that states a speed runs alone, like every other timing check; a bound that only says "it ends" belongs in the suite whose runner would otherwise hang.

Each was reviewed adversarially; every review gap was minor, so no repair ran. What the specification left open: nothing. Reason: README 3.2.
- Deviations accepted: P4-A6 reworded the goal of b10 too (its fVector check is also met on B3) and two captions whose numbers were neither golden nor allowed; P4-A5 trimmed beat 7 outside the sentences named, to reach 1748 words; P4-X2 moved the 1 s E8 bound of weylDimension to the engine's bench run (a timing check of the phase 3 roster) and kept the 60 s bound of p3b-complete-games in the parallel suite as a limit of the runner, and edited a comment in ci.yml.
- Carried to a later polish pass (minor review points): M7's numbers test checks values and not their source, so a golden value typed into a caption passes; M7.spec.ts still types 12 for the rows of the table of solids; the grouping-space step of the numbers test never runs, since \s already turns U+202F into a space; two M7 challenge tests read Astro's island serialisation; M6 beat 7 opens with the warning box, with no short paragraph before it (8.2 item 3); M6 beat 2's [V] claim lost "every board"; the timing guard misses a no-argument Date read through unary plus, a toHaveLength bound on timings, and helpers outside the scanned folders; no permanent test pins the hrtime and new Date() patterns; weyl-dimension.bench.ts types 3875n.
- Passed on: P4-A6 asks P1-A4 for a board-aware challenge check and P4-A3 for an optional boardChoices on T10; the fixture-build race of released.test.ts that P4-X2 saw in 2 of 3 runs at 662eba4 (which carries the lock) goes to the deferred fix round with P3-RT-12-02.
